## Snapshot
World's largest pure-play foundry, ~70% of leading-edge foundry share (TSMC former exec, 2026-04-14); leading-edge share was ~2/3 in Q1-2025 vs Samsung 7.7% — the size of that gap is itself the central antitrust overhang (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). Sells wafer capacity to fabless logic designers (NVIDIA, AMD, Broadcom, Apple, Qualcomm, MediaTek) plus advanced packaging (CoWoS, SoIC). No products competing with customers — the structural moat per the former exec call. Mix has flipped to compute: in FY25 HPC was 58% of revenue (+48% y/y), smartphone 29%, IoT/auto/DCE the rest; advanced nodes (≤7nm) were 74% of wafer revenue, up from 69% in 2024 (Q4 FY25, 2026-01-15). AI accelerators (GPU/ASIC/HBM controllers — excludes data-center CPU) were "high teens %" of total revenue in 2025 (Q4 FY25). On a broader cloud-AI definition (logic + CoWoS dollar content of AI accelerators), Fubon estimates AI was ~12% of revenue in FY24, ~24% in FY25 and potentially 30%+ in FY26 (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28). The 20-F (filed 2026-04-16) reports FY25 revenue of TWD 3.8tn / USD 122bn, +35.9% in USD. Apple was the #1 customer at 24% in 2024; NVIDIA expected to take #1 at ~20%+ in 2025, roughly level with Apple (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Leading-edge logic foundry / wafer capacity (N3·N5·N2, ≤7nm = 74% of wafer rev) · 2) Advanced packaging (CoWoS, SoIC, COUPE CPO) · 3) Mature/specialty nodes (N7+, IoT/auto/DCE) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Fabless logic designers & AI-accelerator/ASIC customers — NVIDIA (#1 ~20%+ in 2025), Apple (24% in 2024), AMD, Broadcom, Qualcomm, MediaTek; AI accelerators "high teens %" of FY25 rev |
| **Key suppliers** | Semiconductor equipment (litho/etch/depo); ABF substrates (Ibiden ~70%, Unimicron ~20%); specialty chemicals & gases; back-end OSAT offload to ASE/Amkor/SPIL |

If that is right, glass core is a lower-stakes, lower-differentiation item for TSMC than the June read implies, and the competitive answer to EMIB-T has to come from CoWoS/CoPoS/SoIC and the *total solution* moat instead — which is exactly where UBS says TSMC is pushing.** ⚠️ **Expert opinion, one source, unconfirmed by TSMC or Intel; the CPO-switch-ASIC-first sequencing is corroborated only circumstantially (Digitimes, relayed by MS desk, 2026-09-15: GLW calls CPO *"irreversible"* as an industry trend, with TGV processing the key gating factor, and sees CPO and glass core as new semiconductor battlegrounds).**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-09 — JPM'S ASIA TEAM PUTS HARD NUMBERS ON THE LEADING-EDGE MOAT AND ON WHEN INTEL COULD MATTER: 95%+ N2/A16 SHARE, N5/N3 AT 100%+ UTILISATION INTO 2028, AI DEMAND VISIBILITY NOW INTO 2029-30, AND AN N2-FAMILY RUN RATE OF $50-60BN BY 2028 — THE YEAR INTEL 14A ENTERS MASS PRODUCTION.** (J.P. Morgan · **Gokul Hariharan**, Jennifer Hsieh, David Chou, Jason Chen, Subham Singhania, "Asian Semis – July WSTS", **2026-09-09**; TSMC named a **top pick**)
- 🔴 **"We continue to see 100%+ UTR at N5/N3 driven by strong demand for AI accelerators and agentic AI CPUs, with utilization expected to remain at 100%+ INTO 2028… TSMC has also indicated that AI demand visibility now extends into 2029-30."**
- 🔴 **"We believe TSMC will retain 95%+ market share in N2/A16. Although Intel competition remains a key investor focus, we DO NOT see major front-end project wins given the technology lag (INTEL 18A COMPARABLE TO TSMC N3E), with TSMC's N2 family expected to reach a $50-60BN REVENUE RUN RATE BY 2028 when Intel 14A enters mass production."** ⚠️ **That is the sharpest dated framing of the Intel-foundry threat in this window and it is a two-generation claim: 18A ~ N3E today, and by the time 14A is in MP, N2 alone is a $50-60bn business.** Cross-filed [[INTC]].
- **Mature nodes, the offsetting negative: 8'' pricing momentum is strengthening on AI-peripheral demand (power management, analog, MCU) but 12'' pricing "remains MIXED due to weak consumer electronics demand", with 12'' price hikes "confined to selectively tighter nodes such as 40nm and 65nm."**
- **Demand context JPM uses: 2Q26 combined AWS/MSFT/GCP revenue of $106bn, +43% y/y, ACCELERATING from +35% last quarter; and Neoclouds "including SpaceX" now a new vector of compute demand on top of CSP buying intent.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-08 — TSMC FINALLY COMMITTED TO HIGH-NA, AND THE PRICE OF ADMISSION IS A NEW PHOTOMASK STANDARD: HVM FROM 2030, A 12'' MASK PILOT LINE BY 2031, 12'' HIGH-NA IN PRODUCTION BY 2033.** (ASML/TSMC joint statement via DIGITIMES, Bloomberg, TrendForce and @QQ_Timmy/@austinsemis relays, **2026-09-08**; BofA · Didier Scemama, **2026-09-08**; Barclays · Simon Coles, **2026-09-08**)
- 🔴 **WHY TSMC RESISTED AND WHY IT STOPPED: High-NA exposes only HALF a reticle, and TSMC is the one foundry with a monopoly on reticle-size dies — NVDA-class GPUs — so stitching two 6'' reticles was a yield risk it would not take on its largest customers' biggest die. The 12'' mask initiative removes stitching entirely. BofA: "by moving to 12'' masks, TSMC and ASML essentially reduce a major pain point preventing adoption of high-NA for NVDA."** TSMC's stated rationale is that **AI chip transistor architectures are getting more complex and the NUMBER OF LAYERS requiring High-NA exposure will grow.**
- **Chairman C.C. Wei's framing per the relays: only industry-wide collaboration lowers the barrier so leading-edge processes scale; ASML CEO Fouquet's: 12'' masks raise scanner productivity. Initially High-NA still runs on 6'' reticles with stitching where required.**
- ⚠️ **BUT BOTH SELL-SIDE HOUSES SAY THIS DOES NOT MOVE THEIR NUMBERS — Barclays had already pushed High-NA adoption to 2030 in its model and BofA's tool ramp is unchanged. So the value here is VISIBILITY, not estimates: what changed is that TSMC's leading-edge roadmap now has a dated litho step in it, and the AI-accelerator die-size ceiling has a stated path past reticle limits.** Cross-filed [[ASML]], [[INTC]], [[SAMSUNG]].

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — TSMC'S OWN EQUIPMENT-ORDER INDEX HAS GONE 1.0x → 1.5x → 1.9x IN SEVEN MONTHS, AND MANAGEMENT SAID IT AT SEMICON.** (BofA · **Haas Liu**, "Semicon Taiwan: AI drives innovation across the semiconductor stack", **2026-09-07/08**, Estimate Change)
- 🔴🔴 **"TSMC's comment at SEMICON that stronger demand has led to A RISE IN THE EQUIPMENT ORDERS IT NEEDS TO PLACE WHICH HAS GROWN FROM 1x BY THE END OF 2025 TO 1.5x IN 1Q26 AND FURTHER TO 1.9x BY JULY."**
- ✅ **INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED THE SAME DAY: "TSMC's QUARTERLY CHIPMAKING TOOL NEEDS ALMOST DOUBLED THIS YEAR as it expands production to meet AI demand. TSMC is grappling with A LACK OF EXPERIENCED CONSTRUCTION WORKERS IN TAIWAN, and will work hard 'WELL INTO NEXT YEAR' to close the supply-demand gap"** (Bloomberg, relayed in JPM Tech HW/Semis Weekly, 2026-09-07). **Two sources, one from management and one from Bloomberg, saying the same thing with the same magnitude ("1.9x" ≈ "almost doubled").** ➤ **This is the single most important semicap datapoint of the week and it belongs on every WFE page — but note what the Bloomberg version adds and the BofA version omits: the binding constraint TSMC names is CONSTRUCTION LABOUR, not tool lead times. An order index that doubles while the fab shell is labour-constrained means the ORDERS are real and the REVENUE RECOGNITION is the timing risk.**
- 🔴 **BofA raises industry capex on the back of it: LOGIC FOUNDRY 2027 to +23% y/y at US$105bn, and PACKAGING & TESTING to +36% y/y at US$33bn.** *"The spending strength continues to be led by TSMC but now SUPPLEMENTED BY COMMITMENT FROM OSATs."* Utilisation is expected to **stay high in '27 across advanced nodes and packaging EVEN WITH AGGRESSIVE EXPANSION.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE PRICING LINE, WHICH IS THE P&L CONSEQUENCE: "Tight supply and higher equipment cost pass-through should bode well for ADVANCED TECHNOLOGY PRICING HIKE BY 10-15%+ YoY IN '27."** BofA **lifts PO and estimates on TSMC, ASE, All Ring and [[MEDIATEK]]**, staying positive on Chroma and GUC. ⚠️ *New PO not quoted in the summary; full note unretrieved.*
- **On manufacturing method: "foundries and OSATs are COLLABORATING FOR STCO (system-technology co-optimization) as the technology development is SHIFTING FROM SERIAL TO PARALLEL across chip production, package, materials and system players. Specifically, TSMC noted the importance of higher-level BACK-END TOOL AUTOMATION AND MODULARIZATION as it could REDUCE TIME FOR RE-VALIDATION and provide the supply chain with HIGHER LEVEL OF FUNGIBILITY for different generations of technologies."** ➤ *Tool fungibility across generations is a quiet negative for the "every node needs a new tool" WFE bull case and a positive for TSMC's own capital efficiency — worth watching which way it is argued.*

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — AND THE PRICE-RELIEF QUESTION GETS A DATE: NOT BEFORE 2027.** (DIGITIMES, "No foundry price relief before 2027 as utilization nears 90%", 2026-09-07) **"AI demand is driving a BROADER REPRICING CYCLE across the foundry industry in 2026. Tightness that began at LEADING-EDGE nodes is SPREADING INTO MATURE [nodes]…"** with industry utilisation **nearing 90%.** ⚠️ *Trade press; the article body was not retrieved beyond the lede.* ➤ **The leading-edge→mature spread is the part with read-through beyond TSMC — it is the mechanism by which [[SMIC]], [[TSEM]] and the analog IDMs get pricing power they have not had since 2022.**

**🆕 2026-09-07 — CALENDAR: SEMICON TAIWAN 2027 MOVES FORWARD BY NEARLY A MONTH TO AUGUST 18-20, "ALIGNING WITH TSMC'S 40TH ANNIVERSARY"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-09-07).

**🆕🔴 2026-09-02 — AN N2 ORDER PULL-IN AT [[AAPL]] THAT MORGAN STANLEY CALLS UNUSUAL, PLUS SEMICON TAIWAN COLOUR PUTTING THE BOTTLENECK ON SUBSTRATES RATHER THAN WAFERS.**
- 🔴 **THE N2 DATAPOINT, AND IT IS THE MOST SPECIFIC WAFER-ORDER MARK IN THIS WINDOW: *"Over the past week, we have seen **modest INCREASES AND PULL-INS in TSMC's 2nm wafer orders for Borneo** (the A20 Pro application processor), equivalent to roughly **2-3M units of incremental iPhone production in C4Q26 and C1Q27**. **It is UNUSUAL for Apple to raise supply-chain forecasts AHEAD of a major product launch**, particularly when those plans already reflect the company's internal 'bull case.' If anything, the recent revisions suggest **Apple is most concerned about SECURING COMPONENTS given recent shortages**"* (MS · **Erik Woodring**, iPhone 18 Launch Preview, Overweight AAPL PT $360, 2026-09-02).* ➤ ✅ **iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max are TSMC's first high-volume N2 product; a pre-launch pull-in is a direct positive on N2 ramp utilisation. ⚠️ Context that limits it: MS also reports Apple *"is considering shifting some volumes of iPhone 18 Pro and Pro Max production from C4Q26 into C1Q27, potentially due to MEMORY supply constraints"* — so the wafer pull-in and a possible unit shift are running at the same time, and the binding constraint on Apple's build is memory, not litho.**
- **N2's stated benefit, from TSMC via the same note: *"N2 delivers up to **10-15% higher performance at the same power** and up to **25-30% lower power at the same performance** versus N3E."*** Apple's first 2nm silicon, the **M6**, adds *"2 'super CPU cores' above the existing performance and efficiency tiers, 2 additional GPU cores, and up to **100% higher peak neural-engine performance**"* vs the 3nm M5. **A20 Pro also moves to WMCM (wafer-level multi-chip module) packaging from InFO.** Total iPhone component builds **265-270M in CY26 (+5-7% y/y)**, of which **~81M combined iPhone 18 Pro / Pro Max / foldable in C2H**.
- **SEMICON TAIWAN 2026 — MS's takeaways from the CEO fireside (Hon Hai chairman Young Liu, Unimicron chairman Shan-Chieh Chien):** *"HH sees AI demand as **unprecedented in speed and scale**, pressuring the supply chain to expand capacity while **raising capital intensity**"*; diversification is *"**structural**, accelerated by geopolitics and countries' push for local production"*, with Hon Hai's strategy framed as **"Made with Taiwan"** — *"exporting Taiwan's manufacturing IP, system-integration know-how and execution speed to end markets via local partnerships and production."* 🔴 **The constraint call: *"UMTC said AI lifts **ABF SUBSTRATE** demand and complexity (**large sizes, higher layer counts, more advanced designs**), **WIDENING SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS**"*, with **10+ supplier/customer capacity-alignment meetings in the past 18 months** and **upstream key materials (ABR and T-glass) remaining TIGHT** (MS · Howard Kao, Irene Yen, Andy Meng, Industry View **In-Line**, 2026-09-02).**
- ➤ 🔴 **THE CROSS-READ THAT MAKES THIS WORTH LOGGING HERE: on the SAME DAY, [[AVGO]]'s Hock Tan named **substrates** among his four binding constraints and said Broadcom is standing up its **own Singapore substrate plant in FY27** to fix it, while a Jefferies colleague relayed that *"**Intel thinks the substrate shortage is way more impactful than memory**."* [[NVDA]]'s IR gave a conflicting read of its own — JPM's readout says the bottleneck is *"memory and ADVANCED WAFERS"* while BofA's says *"memory and SUBSTRATE/BOUNDARY WAFERS."* **Three of the largest leading-edge buyers point at substrates in the same 24 hours; only one of them points at front-end wafers. For TSMC that distinction is the whole question — a substrate bottleneck caps the industry's shippable units WITHOUT capping TSMC's wafer starts.** Cross-file [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).** ⚠️ **The Intel remark is third-hand (a Jefferies salesperson relaying a colleague's meeting) — a hypothesis, not a fact.**
- **Also on the tape, unsized:** *"**European chip ambitions hinge on more than subsidies, says TSMC**"* (DIGITIMES, 2026-09-02) — headline-level only in the material read here.



**🆕⚠️ 2026-08-26/27 — TWO ITEMS THAT CUT AT TSMC'S TWO MOATS FROM DIFFERENT ANGLES: [[INTC]] EMIB IS NOW REPORTED AS A *YIELD-CONFIRMED* CoWoS RIVAL WITH FOUR NAMED PROGRAMMES, AND THE BINDING 2027 CONSTRAINT MAY HAVE MOVED DOWNSTREAM TO SUBSTRATES.**
- 🔴 **THE PACKAGING CHALLENGE IS THE MORE SERIOUS OF THE TWO: *"**Intel EMIB advanced packaging has become a CONFIRMED RIVAL to TSMC with strong yields and satisfied customers**, media report, citing unnamed supply chain sources, who say **Intel's work with clients [[GOOG]] and [[MEDIATEK]] has been so smooth it indicates that costs and production yields are meeting targets.** And **with TSMC CoWoS capacity STILL TIGHT, clients are likely to DEEPEN work with Intel**"* (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-27, ~84k views). DIGITIMES: *"**MediaTek's EMIB solution has met Google's ORIGINAL REQUIREMENTS for technology, yield, cost, and production capacity**"* — and *"while **MediaTek still considers TSMC its PRIMARY FOUNDRY PARTNER and TRIES TO MINIMIZE DISCUSSION of alternative suppliers**, several senior executives on its U.S.-based ASIC team are **former Intel employees**"* (via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-27). ⚠️ *Press/supply-chain relays citing unnamed sources; not company-confirmed.*
- 🔴 **AND A FOURTH VALIDATION FROM A COMPETITOR'S OWN PUBLISHED ROADMAP, WHICH IS HARDER TO DISMISS THAN A RUMOUR: [[SKHYNIX]] at Hot Chips 2026 *"compared how **CoWoS-S, CoWoS-L, and Intel EMIB** impose different mechanical and thermal stresses on HBM stacks and silicon interposers"*, and **its 2.5D HBM roadmap features EMIB alongside CoWoS-L, CoWoS-R and CoWoS-S** (Wccftech / ServeTheHome, via Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26). ➤ **A memory maker engineering FOR EMIB in its own deck is a stronger signal than a supply-chain check. Together with the 08-25 entry on an EMIB win in SK Hynix's next-gen HBM, EMIB now has [[GOOG]], [[MEDIATEK]] and [[SKHYNIX]] attached. See [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).**
- ⚠️ **THE CONSTRAINT MAY HAVE MOVED PAST THE FOUNDRY: *"**ABF substrates & PCB/CCL are the biggest constraints for 2027.** [[NVDA]] or [[GOOG]] can reduce HBM per GPU and DRAM per server to increase shipments, but **they can't work around this**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-27). Concretely, **Kinsus** reports ABF utilisation rebounded to **85%** heading to **~95% by year-end**, with **T-glass shortage stranding *"approximately 10% to 15% of potential MONTHLY REVENUE"*** — *"the key operational bottleneck"* — and plans **+25% high-end ABF capacity in 2027** built in **Taiwan** (Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26). Taiwan's tape agreed: *"TAIEX +1.5% with **CPO/ABF/CCL/Cooling leading the rally**."*
- ✅ **THE DEMAND SIDE IS UNAMBIGUOUS, AND THE LEAD TIME IS NOW QUANTIFIED FROM A CUSTOMER: [[ANET]] says *"demand visibility for projects extends 1-2 years, with customers planning **18-24 months out due to 52-WEEK LEAD TIMES FOR TSMC WAFER STARTS**"* (ANET investor call, 2026-08-26). And JPM's Gokul Hariharan has **NVDA CoWoS up 65% in C27**, with the desk caveat that *"we don't think it's necessarily going to be that easy to get extra wafer — **particularly N3 for Vera, LPX and other add-ons**"* (JPM desk · Meyers, 2026-08-26).**
- ✅ **AND THE STRUCTURAL ARGUMENT OF THE WEEK IS A TSMC ARGUMENT: *"What [[OPENAI]]'s Jalapeño made me realize is just how much lower the barrier to chip design is going to become. Ultimately, **MANUFACTURING WILL BE WHERE THE REAL MOAT REMAINS**… Design and engineering outside of manufacturing will gradually lose their moat"* (@jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-25 — ~305k views, the most-viewed post of this window). ➤ **If design commoditises and fabrication does not, TSMC captures a rising share of a rising number of programmes. Bernstein's mask-blank work says the same thing from the consumables side: **advanced-node chip designs growing at a 21% CAGR (2025-28)** because *"every CSP and AI company is making their own GPU, CPU, and AI accelerators."***
- **[[AAPL]] read-through on the leading edge: *"Apple Rolls Out Desktop With **2nm SoC**"* (Semiecosystem · Mark LaPedus, 2026-08-26).**


**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — THE JPM ASIA TECH TOUR PUTS THREE HARD NUMBERS AROUND TSMC AND ONE OF THEM IS A COMPETITIVE CAP.** (JPM · Gokul Hariharan and team, *"Asia Tech Tour: Key takeaways"* — 11th annual, **37 companies, 115 investors**, 2026-08-25; JPM is **OW with a NT$3,200 PT** vs NT$2,375 spot on 08-24, **22.0x/16.8x FY26E/27E P/E, 42.7%/40.3% ROE**)
- **Capacity:** *"Leading-edge demand remains robust, with **TSMC building 13 fabs in Taiwan**. The capacity planning is informed by **direct engagement with both customers and CUSTOMERS' CUSTOMERS on end demand**, while also factoring **power supply and datacenter construction** into the capex decisions."*
- 🔴 **The [[INTC]] cap, and it is quantified:** *"we believe **Intel may secure some project wins in packaging (e.g. EMIB-T for TPU v9); however, at the front end we still do not see any major project wins**, given the technology lag — **Intel 18A is comparable to TSMC N3E**, and by the time **Intel 14A enters mass production in 2028 we expect the TSMC N2 family to already be approaching a $50-60bn revenue run rate** — and the typical 3-5 year engagement cycle from initial design to mass production."*
- **Mature nodes are two different markets:** demand is *"very strong **only in selective areas — power management, analog and MCU** — while consumer-electronics-related demand remains lackluster… we expect [that] to persist into 2027."* **Price hikes are concentrated in 8"** (VIS negotiating another round now, likely a further round in early 2027), while **12" mature keeps adding capacity (VSMC, UMC SG 12i P4 and Tainan 12A P7/P8, China-based foundries)** so hikes there are **confined to selectively tighter nodes such as 40nm and 65nm**. **Equipment from any TSMC ramp-down "will likely be sold to other foundries — likely VIS."**
- **The bottleneck has moved to the tool queue:** *"OSAT capacity tightness is intensifying, with the bottleneck shifting from clean-room availability toward increasingly prolonged equipment lead times. **Advanced packaging equipment is emerging as a key bottleneck, particularly for CoW tools (lead times up to 12 months), as traditional OSATs and TSMC compete for the same tool set.**"* **Asian OSAT 2026 capex up 80%+ y/y to record highs; brownfield conversion 6-9 months vs ~2 years greenfield; tightness extends "at least into 2027/28."**
- **Demand cross-check from MS's supply-chain checks the same day:** **5.4M Blackwell units in 2026 and nearly 7M Rubin/Rubin Ultra units in 2027, with ~90k Rubin NVL72 racks in 2027**; MS dismisses Blackwell "inventory" concerns as supply-chain buffers to be absorbed during 2026. 🔴 **And the constraint MS puts on top: "~19M 2027 CoWoS-implied GPU/ASIC units equate to roughly 38GW at 2kW/chip. An incremental 8GW of [[SPCX]] compute capacity alone would correspond to ~4M Rubin GPUs, or ~20% of TSMC's potential 2027 AI accelerator output"** (MS · Charlie Chan, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **A CoWoS-implied unit count converted to GW at an assumed 2kW/chip — an analyst construction, not a TSMC disclosure.**
- **Node loading datapoints elsewhere on the complex:** **[[OPENAI]]'s Jalapeño compute die is on N3P with an N3E I/O chiplet** and taped out its **CoWoS design in November 2025** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-25); **Xiaomi's XRING O3 is on TSMC 3nm — ~24bn transistors (up from 19bn on the O1), die 133mm² (from 109mm²)** — with two more XRING parts at TSMC (**6nm O100 NPU, 3nm D100 for autonomous driving**) (Reuters/Cailian via Jefferies, 2026-08-25); and **[[SKHYNIX]] has confirmed [[INTC]] EMIB for next-gen HBM "mainly due to the continued shortage of TSMC's CoWoS capacity"** (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-25). 🔴 **That last one is the sharpest: a CoWoS constraint is now visibly routing a named workload to a competitor's packaging.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — A CUSTOMER PUT A NUMBER ON ITS OWN TSMC RAMP: CISCO EXPECTS ITS VOLUMES WITH TSMC TO INCREASE ~10x IN FY27 ACROSS 3nm, 5nm AND 7nm, WITH 3nm THE TIGHTEST NODE.** *"Memory, pump lasers and leading-edge silicon remain constrained across the industry, though management believes CSCO is navigating the environment better through its balance sheet, larger purchase commitments and direct relationship with TSMC… Volumes with TSMC are expected to increase ~10x in FY27 across 3nm, 5nm and 7nm, with 3nm remaining the tightest node."* (MS · Meta A. Marshall, [[CSCO]] Nasdaq Asia conference recap, 2026-08-24 — MS hosted Cisco IR and its SVP of Sales for APJC/Greater China on 18-19 August.) 🔴 **A named non-hyperscaler customer disclosing a 10x wafer ramp, and describing a "direct relationship with TSMC" as a competitive moat, is a demand datapoint this page has not previously carried from outside the AI-accelerator set.**

**🆕 2026-08-21/24 — THREE SEPARATE CROSS-READS ON TSMC THIS WEEK, ONE POSITIVE, ONE NEUTRAL, ONE NEGATIVE.**
- **✅ Positive — Taiwan is absorbing the equipment China isn't:** **ASML-related Taiwan litho import value +79% y/y in July and +74% y/y YTD**, against **China -12% YTD**, *"directionally consistent with expectations"* (BofA · Didier Scemama, "China Semicap Import Tracker — Jul 2026", 2026-08-21). Filed to themes/semicap-wfe.
- **➖ Neutral — CoWoS allocation is holding at the top:** **"Nvidia's CoWoS share at TSM will remain stable in 2027"**, with **460k CoWoS (ASIC) wafers set for 2027** and **[[AVGO]]'s Sunfish volume cut for 2026/27 on a packaging re-design** (@sssjeffpu, "ASIC Warp-Up", 2026-08-24 — **independent handle, no rating/PT**). Separately, an independent preview has **CoWoS capacity "revised up to at least 200k wpm by end-2027"** (FundaAI NVDA FY27Q2 preview, 2026-08-23 — ⚠️ **note the two figures are different units: 460k annual ASIC wafers vs 200k wafers per month of total capacity; they are not comparable**).
- **⚠️ Negative, and unconfirmed — COUPE slipping, with Intel named as the alternative:** *"With TSMC's COUPE CPO slipping, NV is working with $INTC on an all INTC Rubin CPO variant (18A, Intel OCI, EMIB), a 4-die ve[rsion]…"* (@ShanghaoJin via @bubbleboi, 2026-08-23 — **independent handle, no broker corroboration on this page**). Filed to [[INTC]] and themes/optical-cpo. **Treat as a lead to verify.**
- **⚠️ A talent-poaching tell worth watching:** UBS found **duplicate Terafab job postings in both Austin, TX and Hsinchu City, Taiwan** and *"suspect SpaceX would want to tap into TSMC's talent pool, though it is less clear to us how Taiwan-based hires would directly support the physical fab ramp in TX in the next few years"* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, "SemiBytes Weekly: … Terafab Update", 2026-08-24). Filed to [[SPCX]].
- **Enforcement context, from the trade press:** *"How Nvidia's whitelist was beaten: 74 B300 servers, Taiwan to China"* — Taiwan's Keelung District Prosecutors Office **indicted nine people over the illegal export of high-end AI servers built on Nvidia's B300 GPUs** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-24). Filed to themes/china-export.


**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 — A THIRD-PARTY COWOS CAPACITY PATH, AND IT IS THE MOST GRANULAR ON THIS PAGE: 70kwpm at end-2025 → 120 / 180 / 220kwpm by end-2026 / 27 / 28.** (Jefferies · Sherman, via Conor O'Mara's Asian TMT desk note, 2026-08-19, in the context of raising C Sun's TP NT$480 → NT$750.)
- **The supply-chain economics attached to it:** every incremental **2-3kwpm of TSMC CoWoS capacity ≈ US$2.5-3.0mn of revenue for C Sun** (equipment). **CoPoS mass production is targeted for 2028**, and the transition **could more than DOUBLE equipment content per unit of capacity** on both extra process stations and higher equipment ASPs.
- 🔴 **The localisation datapoint is the one with strategic content: domestic Taiwanese suppliers currently account for only ~10% of TSMC's advanced-packaging equipment spending, and Jefferies' checks suggest TSMC plans to raise localisation to 20-30% in following years.** Also flagged: **meaningful equipment demand from TSMC's US AP9 facility likely emerging in 2028-29**.
- **The OSAT side, which is where the outsourcing signal lives: ASE/SPIL OS capacity is forecast to go from 35kwpm at end-2025 to 85 / 145 / 185kwpm by end-2026/27/28**, supported by "an increasing number of OS process stations **outsourced by TSMC**" plus their own additions. Every 2-3kwpm of incremental OSAT capacity ≈ **US$3.0-3.5mn** of C Sun revenue (higher than the TSMC-direct figure, because OSATs buy automation TSMC develops in-house).
- ⚠️ **All of the above is a broker's supply-chain model built to value an equipment supplier, not a TSMC disclosure. It is logged as the best available external CoWoS path, explicitly not as guidance** — and it should be read against the 08-18 primary material on this page, where management declined to give any capacity number ("I don't have a capacity number to give you").

**🆕 2026-08-19 — TWO OPERATIONAL ITEMS, ONE OF THEM SERIOUS.** (@dnystedt, @trendforce/@NEWS2082680, 2026-08-19.)
- **A worker was killed at TSMC's Fab 20** — crushed by electrical equipment that slid out of control while being moved; the victim was 22. Reported by Taiwanese media. Logged because fab-safety incidents can carry regulatory and schedule consequences, not as a financial estimate.
- 🔴 **The CoWoS crunch is reportedly pushing some back-end packaging orders to [[INTC]] MALAYSIA — "a shift C.C. Wei has welcomed."** This is the concrete instance of the position management stated on the 08-18 material already on this page: packaging is "strategic, not financial," and Amkor/ASE/Intel EMIB are welcomed because they mean more wafer business for TSMC. ⚠️ Trade-press/social sourcing; unsized.
**NEW / MIXED 2026-08-18 — TSMC'S CONSERVATISM TRANSFERS RISK TO CUSTOMERS, THEN CREATES ITS OWN ROUTE-AROUND.** Ben Thompson argues that TSMC rationally avoids the 30-year overcapacity risk of a fab, but the risk does not disappear: it shows up as foregone hyperscaler revenue during acute shortage. His characterization is that capacity growth slowed through 2023-25, so today's spend becomes useful compute only around 2028-29. The consequence is strategically bearish at the margin: scarcity makes the otherwise-negative insurance cost of qualifying [[INTC]] or [[SAMSUNG]] economically rational, creating geopolitical redundancy "for free." Thompson also warns that overwhelming US AI superiority could make an attack on Taiwan/TSMC the game-theoretic response, while full China decoupling remains economically unrealistic (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-12 — TWO HOUSES RAISE TSM MID-QUARTER ON THE SAME MECHANISM — "HOT LOTS" AND ACCELERATING AI WAFER DEMAND — AND SUSQUEHANNA NOW SITS ~7%/~11%/~10% ABOVE CONSENSUS ON CY26/27/28 EPS.**

- **🔴 SIG's checks put 3Q AT THE HIGH END OF GUIDANCE, and the reason is a margin-accretive one: "Recent checks suggest 3Q26 revenue is tracking to the HIGH END of guide range of $44.6B-$45.8B, driven by increased use of 'HOT LOTS' across both GPU and ASIC AI customers."** SIG's reminder on why that matters: *"incremental demand is often fulfilled through 'hot lots,' which carry **higher ASPs than standard production** and therefore provide a favorable uplift to **both revenue and GM**"* (SIG / Mehdi Hosseini, "Increased AI Supplier Competition Bodes Well for TSM", **Positive, PT $600 / NTD3,500**, 2026-08-12).
- **Wafer-shipment revisions (SIG, 2026-08-12):** **GPU-related wafer shipments +10% q/q** in 3Q (from +8% prior); **ASIC AI-related +12% q/q** (from +10%). For the year: **GPU wafers +22–24% y/y in 2026, ASIC wafers +35% y/y** — *"although ASIC is growing faster, it is coming off a smaller base, currently accounting for **10% of total wafer shipments versus 30% for GPUs**."*
- **🔴 THE MIX FORECAST, and it is the cleanest statement of the ASIC-vs-GPU debate in wafer terms this page carries: "We expect the ASIC share of total wafer shipments (including both compute and networking applications) to rise to 15% BY 2028, while GPU share reaches 40%. This implies ASIC wafer shipment CAGR of 35% 2025-2028 vs. 25% for GPUs and just 4% FOR SMARTPHONE."** ⚠️ **Read the smartphone number — 4% CAGR. On SIG's math the handset business, historically TSMC's anchor, becomes a rounding error in growth terms while AI goes to 55% of wafers. That is the mix shift, quantified.**
- **Revenue path: CY26 +42% y/y "with upside potential toward 45% as customers utilize hot lots" vs the updated guide of 40%; CY27 +30–35% y/y "with the potential for growth reaching 40%+, similar to what we are seeing in CY26."**
- **🔴 EPS vs consensus — the house-vs-street gap is large and explicit: SIG CY26/27/28 EPS $17.80 / $24.10 / $30.14 vs consensus $16.64 / $21.74 / $27.30** (**+7% / +11% / +10%**); on NTD, **NTD113 / NTD154 / NTD193 vs consensus NTD107 / NTD141 / NTD178**. **PT $600 (NTD3,500) = ~23x 2027 EPS, 15x EV/EBITDA, 10x EV/sales, 10x current P/B.**
- **✅ AND THERE IS OPTIONALITY SIG EXPLICITLY EXCLUDES: "Our updated estimates do NOT include any contribution from the COUPE platform used for CPO assembly, nor from PIC adoption in NPO transceivers."** ⚠️ **That is a real omission with a name on it — [[LITE]]'s same-week call put NPO ramping late 2027 for scale-up and CPO shipments on track for 2H CY27, and Aurelion's channel work on this wiki ties COUPE volumes to 200–300k CPO switches in 2027 scaling to 600–800k in 2028. Whatever COUPE/PIC is worth, it is upside to these numbers, not in them.**
- **Corroborating capex raise from a second house on the same day: JPM lifts TSMC capex to $62 / $81 / $90bn for FY26/27/28 (from $58 / $78 / $84bn), with leading-edge (N5 and below) capacity growing at a 24% CAGR over 2026-28, and notes "TSMC visibility now extending into 2029-30"** (JPM Asia Pacific / Gokul Hariharan, June WSTS note, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **Two independent upward capex/estimate revisions in one day, both mid-quarter, both AI-mix-driven — and note the capex raise is ~+7%/+4%/+7%, i.e. TSMC is being marked up on BOTH the revenue and the spend line. Read-through to [[ASML]], [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[TOKYOELEC]].**
- **⚠️ One non-AI item on the tape, unquantified: "Apple's shadow looms over TSMC's Sony sensor deal"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-12) — logged as a watch-item only; no numbers attached in what came through.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — BERNSTEIN TAKES TSM's PT TO $554 FROM $430 (+29%) AND THE DRIVER IS NOT AI ACCELERATORS — IT IS THE CPU (Bernstein / Mark Li, via David Patafio, 2026-08-11).**
- **The new growth leg: "CPU is the next growth driver and we project CPU revenue to grow from $15-16B last year to high-30s$B in '27, driven by demand from [[AMD]], [[INTC]], [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[ARM]], etc."** — i.e. roughly **+140% over two years on a base already larger than most people's AI-ASIC lines.** This is the first CPU-specific TSM revenue build on this page.
- **N2 ramping ahead of prior nodes: "N2 will contribute revenue ahead of prior nodes and we expect N2 to contribute HSD% of TSMC's total wafer revenue this year and 20% in '27."**
- **Capex, three years out: US$64B / 75B / 82B in 2026 / 27 / 28.**
- **🔴 The CoWoS statement is a deliberate strategic choice, not a share loss to be feared: "TSMC is strategically 'ALLOWING' share losses in CoWoS capacity so that TSMC's front-end wafer service won't experience a downstream bottleneck, especially with the addition of [[INTC]]'s EMIB-T in 2028."** ➜ **This directly reframes the advanced-packaging-share thread on this page: outsourcing packaging headroom protects the higher-margin front end. Worth testing against the CoWoS-share notes already logged.**
- **Earnings and valuation: "EPS to rise by 67% this year & at 31% CAGR in 2027 & 28. TSMC trades at 18.8x forward P/E, i.e. 14% & 25% discount vs. SOX & Nasdaq and we view the entry point as attractive here."**
- **Corroborating capacity/yield datapoint from the packaging side (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-11):** TSMC's *"CoWoS guy"* Jun He — **CoWoS and SoIC capacity roughly DOUBLING annually for several years, with CoWoS running at better than 98% yield**, and the framing that TSMC is *"nearing"* AI packaging demand after that run of doublings. **A >98% yield on the industry's tightest bottleneck is the strongest argument that CoWoS is no longer the binding constraint** — consistent with Mark Li's "allowing share losses" read.
- **Bernstein's WFE note the same day puts logic/foundry equipment at +20% (2027) and +19% (2028) "as TSMC, INTC and Samsung invest in leading edge nodes"** (Rasgon / Dai, 2026-08-11) — a demand-side corroboration of the $64/75/82B capex path.

**🆕 2026-08-11 — SONY / TSMC IMAGE-SENSOR JV, FORMALLY ANNOUNCED: "Advanced Vision Semiconductor Manufacturing Corporation," Kumamoto, Japan.** The JV *"will serve as a core hub for the development and manufacturing activities for the volume production of image sensors for smartphones utilizing advanced manufacturing process technology, with **volume production expected in 2029E**"* (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11; also Semiecosystem / Mark Lapedus, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Reads directly onto the Kumamoto question this page already tracks: the second Kumamoto-area vehicle is a Sony-anchored specialty node, not leading-edge logic, and the revenue is a 2029 event. Treat it as capacity-utilisation and customer-lock-in news, not as a near-term earnings item.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 (PM) — JPM re-underwrites the July print and, more usefully, answers the Kumamoto question (JPM / Gokul Hariharan · Jennifer Hsieh · David Chou, 2026-08-10; OW, Jun-27 PT NT$3,200 on ~20x 12-mo fwd P/E; ref NT$2,380).**
- **The tracking math:** July NT$467.6bn **= ~32% of JPMe/Bloomberg consensus 3Q26 revenue**, putting the quarter **at the mid-to-high end of the US$44.6-45.8bn guide** (JPMe US$45.5bn).
- **Margin: the N2 dilution is quantified.** JPM models **3Q26 GM ~66.5%, down 1.2pts q/q** on N2 ramp dilution, and holds **mid-to-high 60% GM "over the next few years"** on pricing uplift + leading-edge utilization + improving N2 yields. Growth frame: **low-40% USD revenue growth in 2026, followed by another 30%+ year in 2027.**
- **✅ THE KUMAMOTO EARTHQUAKE QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND IT CLOSES A [[AAPL]] OPEN ITEM:** *"we have seen some investor queries on a potential 3Q26 shortfall due to the Kumamoto earthquake; while **TSMC did see some Fab disruption in its Image Sensor (ISP) production for Apple iPhones (through Sony)**, we believe **backup capacity in its Taichung Fab should have been able to pick up the slack, resulting in NO MEANINGFUL IMPACT to iPhone 18 series release timelines**."* Cross-ref [AAPL.md](AAPL.md) — this is the supply-side offset to the Sony/TSMC ¥1trn JV item below.
- **Capex and packaging, with the competitive read attached:** JPMe capex **US$81bn (FY27) / US$90bn (FY28)**; datacenter-AI revenue CAGR **~69% (2024-29E)** on visibility *"extending into 2029-30"*; **95+% share of N2/A16** expected, **A14 "leading well ahead of peers."** On packaging: **CoWoS +85% y/y in 2026 and +65% in 2027**, **3DSoIC more than doubling by YE2028 vs YE2026**, CoPoS ramping. ⚠️ **And JPM takes the other side of the [[INTC]] EMIB-T bear this wiki has been building: *"we don't see Intel EMIB-T posing a material threat to TSMC CoWoS."*** Logged as a disagreement, not a resolution — the wiki carries three independent EMIB-T unit marks (UBS 2.3M 2028E, Mizuho ~3M from 2028E) on the [INTC.md](INTC.md) page against this.
- **The rotation argument, stated explicitly:** *"Recent **de-speccing trends in HBM/memory** are also likely to drive **incremental investor interest towards the AI compute complex, AWAY FROM MEMORY**."* Cross-ref [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md), [NVDA.md](NVDA.md).

**🆕 2026-08-10 — July revenue a record; Q3 on track; and a ¥1trn image-sensor JV with Sony.**
- **July sales NT$467.58bn (~US$14.5bn), +5.6% m/m, +44.7% y/y — a record month.** UBS: that **tracks 32% of its Q326 estimate** and Q3 q/q growth is **on track with UBSe of +15%**; UBS forecasts **2026 USD sales +41%**, driven by robust **N3** growth from new AI accelerators/CPUs and a faster **N2** ramp. **Buy** (UBS / Sunny Lin · Randy Abrams · Nicolas Gaudois, 2026-08-10). DIGITIMES pegs the 2026 growth forecast at **>40%** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10).
- **Sony/TSMC image-sensor JV:** the two plan to invest **~¥1 trillion (US$6.3bn)** in a joint venture to mass-produce next-generation image sensors in Japan **as early as 2029**, JV to be established **by March 2027**, **Sony ~60% / TSMC ~40%**, built at Sony's chip-unit plant in **Kumamoto**. The JV is planned to **supply high-performance camera sensors for Apple's iPhones**, with R&D facilities and production lines on site; both cite future demand from AI robots and AVs (Nikkei via The Information, 2026-08-10; DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [AAPL.md](AAPL.md).
- **The customer-side constraint is now on the record:** Apple's CEO attributes its September-quarter supply constraint specifically to **"the availability of the advanced nodes that our SOCs are produced on"** — i.e. TSMC leading-edge logic, explicitly *not* memory (AAPL FQ3'26 call, 2026-08-06, via Stratechery 2026-08-10). Also: **Apple foundry pricing is no longer discounted** — Jefferies' Sherman wrote a month ago that "Apple will be paying 10% more next year like everybody else" (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-09).

**The print (primary sources: TSMC 2Q26 [earnings release](../TSM/TSM_2Q26_EarningsRelease_2026-07-16.pdf), [management report](../TSM/TSM_2Q26_ManagementReport_2026-07-16.pdf) & [investor presentation](../TSM/apresentações/TSM_2Q26_Investor_Conference_2026-07-16.pdf), all 2026-07-16).** Revenue **US$40.20bn** (NT$1,270.38bn), **+12.0% q/q, +33.7% y/y in USD** (+36.0% y/y in TWD) — at the **high end** of the US$39.0–40.2bn guide, a record quarter. **Gross margin 67.7%** (+1.5pt q/q, +9.1pt y/y) — **above** the 65.5–67.5% guide, on cost-improvement + higher utilization, partly offset by overseas-fab dilution. **Operating margin 60.3%** (+2.2pt q/q) — above the 56.5–58.5% guide. **EPS NT$27.25** (US$4.31/ADR), +23.4% q/q / +77.4% y/y; **net income NT$706.56bn** (net margin 55.6%, **ROE 45.9%**). Headline EPS/NI were flattered by **NT$63.20bn of disposal + mark-to-market gains on Vanguard (VIS) shares** (total non-operating gain NT$95.83bn) — so the *clean* operating signal is stronger in revenue/OpM than in EPS: **gross profit +14.5% q/q, operating income +16.3% q/q**. Effective tax 18.1% (undistributed-earnings accrual). Wafer shipments **4,336k 12"-equiv (+3.9% q/q)**; avg FX NT$31.60/US$. (TSMC 2Q26 management report & earnings release, 2026-07-16)

**Mix (2Q26 — TSMC management report, 2026-07-16):** by node — **2nm 3%** (first contribution, from 0% in 1Q26), 3nm 30% (from 25%), 5nm 33% (from 36%), 7nm 11%; **advanced (≤7nm) = 77%** of wafer revenue. By platform — **HPC 66%** (+20% q/q, from 61%), Smartphone 22% (−4% q/q), IoT 5% (+4%), Automotive 4% (+15%), DCE 1%, Others 2%. By geography — **North America 78%** (from 76%), Asia-Pac 8%, China 6%, Japan 4%, EMEA 4%. Inventory days rose 7 to **87, "primarily due to N2 ramp."**

**3Q26 guidance:** revenue **US$44.6–45.8bn** (midpoint US$45.2bn, ~+12% q/q); **gross margin 65–67%**; **operating margin 56–58%**; on an FX assumption of **NT$32.0/US$** (vs 31.60 realized in 2Q — a weaker-TWD *tailwind* that the 65–67% guide absorbs, i.e. underlying N2-ramp + overseas dilution pulls GM down ~2–3pt off the 67.7% base). "Continued strong demand for our leading-edge process technologies, **including the steep ramp-up of our 2-nanometer technology**" (CFO Wendell Huang). (TSMC 2Q26 earnings release & presentation, 2026-07-16)

**FY26 outlook — the headline:** management now guides **2026 revenue to grow "slightly above 40%" in USD** — raised from **"above 30%"** at Q1 FY26 (and "close to 30%" at Q4 FY25). This **clears the single most-watched pre-print bogey** (MS sales desk: "at least high-30s is a must for the stock"; buy-side had ~40% modelled). (TSMC 2Q26 presentation, "Future Outlook," 2026-07-16)

**Capex — raised, CONFIRMED on the call:** 2Q26 capex **US$15.70bn** (NT$496.0bn), up from US$11.10bn in 1Q26 → **1H26 YTD US$26.80bn**. On the call, CFO Wendell Huang **raised the FY26 capital budget to US$60–64bn** (from ~US$52–56bn in Jan / "closer to US$56bn" in Apr), split **~70–80% advanced process / ~10% specialty / ~10–20% advanced packaging, testing, mask & others** — "given the continued strong structural demand… including the newly emerging agentic AI market." C.C. Wei attributed the ~US$10bn YTD raise to customer demand pressure **plus tool-price inflation**. The **next three years of capex will be "even more significantly higher"** than the prior three (upgraded from Q1's "significantly higher"); TSMC again **declined a numeric 2027/28 guide**. A US$60–64bn full-year implies 2H26 capex ~US$33–37bn vs US$26.8bn in 1H — a steep step-up; it clears the ~US$58bn buy-side bogey and is the clearest positive surprise for the equipment/materials chain ([[ASML]]/[[semicap-wfe]]). (TSMC 2Q26 earnings call [transcript](../TSM/transcripts/TSM_Q2-2026-earnings_2026-07-16.md), 2026-07-16; 2Q/1H capex per the management report)

**Scorecard vs the pre-print bogeys** (bogeys: MS sales / Amir Amerian 07-13; BofA / Kevin Wang poll 07-12):

| Metric | Pre-print bogey | Actual / new guide | Read |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2Q revenue | buy-side US$40.8bn (guide hi US$40.2bn) | **US$40.20bn** | Met guide high-end; ~US$0.6bn shy of the buy-side bogey |
| 2Q gross margin | **≥69%** for a positive day-1 | **67.7%** | Beat the guide (+20bp) but **missed the ≥69% bogey** |
| 3Q revenue | buy-side US$45.7bn | US$44.6–45.8bn (mid 45.2) | Clears only near the top of the range |
| 3Q gross margin | buy-side 68.2% / JPM 69.5% | **65–67%** | **Below** — the key negative (N2 + overseas dilution) |
| FY26 revenue | "high-30s a must"; buy-side ~40% | **"slightly above 40%"** | **Clears/beats** the most important bogey |
| FY26 capex | ~US$58bn | **US$60–64bn** | **Beat** — positive surprise (confirmed on the call; next 3 yrs "even more significantly higher") |

**Net:** thesis intact; near-term setup **mixed → a margin debate**. TSMC delivered the demand and capex signals an AI bull wanted — high-end 2Q revenue, FY guide raised to >40%, a steep N2 ramp (already 3% of wafer revenue) and a capex raise — but the **3Q gross-margin guide (65–67%) sits below the elevated buy-side margin bar**, so the near-term stock debate turns on margin, not demand. JPM's market-intelligence desk framed it a "headline beat with capex ahead of the buy-side bar" while flagging the 3Q margin guide below its 69.5% estimate; Taiwan futures ticked lower immediately post-print (JPMorgan International Market Intelligence, 2026-07-16). Next reads: the official call transcript (overseas-dilution bridge, 2nm ramp economics, whether US$60–64bn capex implies further leading-edge acceleration). Digested internal note: [2Q26 earnings review](../TSM/TSMC_2Q26_Earnings_Review_2026-07-16.md).

**Key messages from the call (C.C. Wei / Wendell Huang, [transcript](../TSM/transcripts/TSM_Q2-2026-earnings_2026-07-16.md), 2026-07-16):**
- **Arizona — an additional US$100bn investment announced**, taking TSMC's total planned Arizona commitment to **US$265bn**: "several more" logic fabs for **2nm-and-below** plus advanced-packaging fabs — C.C. Wei, "**four or more fabs… front and back end**," schedule demand-dependent but "as fast as possible." Separately, TSMC is building **13 leading-edge + advanced-packaging fabs in Taiwan** over the next several years. This **overshoots** the pre-print "US commitment toward ~US$200bn under US pressure" bear framing.
- **Agentic AI = a CPU resurgence:** "the emergence of agentic AI is leading to a resurgence in the role of CPUs in AI data centers… no matter what CPU approach — x86, ARM-based or RISC-V — they are almost all TSMC's customers" — an incremental silicon leg on top of accelerators (corroborates the server-CPU thesis on this page).
- **AI-semi revenue CAGR — raised only qualitatively, no number:** asked to update the Jan "mid-to-high-50s" 2024–29 AI CAGR, C.C. Wei declined a figure but said it is "**stronger and stronger and stronger… stronger than what we said before**." (The watched 70–80% number was *not* formally given — directionally up only.)
- **GM bridge:** 3Q GM −1.7pt q/q to 66% (mid) as the **N2 ramp dilutes ~3–4pt** (both 3Q and across 2H), partly offset by strong leading-edge demand + cost/productivity gains and across-node capacity optimization; **overseas-fab dilution reaffirmed 2–3% early → 3–4% later**. Pricing philosophy unchanged — "we are a partner… we don't suddenly increase price 4x or 5x"; C.C. Wei "jealous" of memory's ~86% GM ("68%, I'll be happy").
- **Supply stays short into 2029–30:** demand "very strong… all the way to probably 2029, 2030"; the N3-and-below demand-vs-supply gap is "**really big**" (declined to quantify vs an analyst's 30–50%). TSMC is tracking AI-data-center build/power progress to avoid shipping into inventory.
- **N3 capacity:** three additional N3 fabs (Taiwan, Arizona, Japan) + N5→N3 tool conversion + cross-node N7/N5/N3 flexibility.
- **Packaging / EMIB-T:** TSMC's packaging is "so tight… limited by customers' growth," so C.C. Wei **welcomes** competitor alternatives (Intel EMIB-T) as flexibility that "helps TSMC's front-end wafer business" (the majority of revenue) — front-end and back-end are "two different things." A glass/alternative packaging line announced earlier is "**about another one year to be mature**." COUPE in production now, ramping.
- **A14 family:** vs N2, **+10–15% speed / −25–30% power / ~20% density**; risk production 2027, **volume 2028**; ~90% device performance & ~90% 256Mb SRAM yield in internal vehicles. Extensions **A13** (6% die-area saving via a 97% optical shrink) and **A12** (super power rail) → both **volume 2029**.
- **Mature node:** increasing (not cutting) capacity in higher-value segments (JASM Fab 1 CIS, ESMC auto/industrial); only AI-linked mature (PMIC 0.18µm/90nm, sensors) is tight — commodity/consumer mature demand "not that strong."
- **Capital returns:** 2026 cash dividend **NT$24/share (+33% y/y)** after NT$18 in 2025; committed to continued increases in 2027. No customer-financing/equity-investment strategy ("current model works smoothly").

## Q1 FY26 recap & guidance baseline (2026-04-16)
_Superseded by the 2Q26 print above on the Q2 guide (→ actuals), the FY26 revenue guide ("above 30%" → **"slightly above 40%"**) and the capex range; the structural node/margin framework below remains the working baseline into 3Q._
Revenue USD 35.9bn, +6.4% q/q, above the high end of guidance. GM 66.2% (+390bp q/q), OpM 58.1% (+410bp), EPS TWD 22.08, ROE 40.5% (Q1 FY26). Mix: HPC +20% q/q to 61% of revenue, smartphone −11% to 26%. N3 = 25% of wafer revenue, N5 36%, N7 13%. Capex USD 11.1bn in the quarter.

**Guidance / messages (Q1 FY26):**
- Q2 FY26 revenue USD 39.0–40.2bn (+10% q/q, +32% y/y at midpoint); GM 65.5–67.5%, OpM 56.5–58.5%; Q2 tax ~20% (undistributed-earnings accrual), FY tax 17–18%.
- FY26 revenue now guided **"above 30%" in USD** (raised from "close to 30%" at Q4 FY25, 2026-01-15) — "AI-related demand continues to be extremely robust… shift from generative to agentic AI is another step-up in tokens" (C.C. Wei).
- FY26 capex revised to the **high end of USD 52–56bn** (i.e. ~USD 56bn); 70–80% advanced process, ~10% specialty, 10–20% packaging/test/mask (Q4 FY25). CFO: next 3 years of capex "significantly higher" than the USD 101bn of the prior 3 years (Q1 FY26). Fubon models FY26 capex US$50bn+ on its own bottoms-up (advanced nodes 80% / specialty 10% / packaging 10%), using ~US$28bn per 50kwpm of N2, $20bn N3, $16bn N5, $12.5bn N7, and overseas-fab build cost ~10% (Japan) / ~60% (US) above Taiwan (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28).
- N2 in HVM since Q4 FY25 with good yield, ramping at Hsinchu + Kaohsiung. N3 capacity being *expanded* (a first — historically TSMC doesn't add to a node post-target): new Tainan N3 fab (1H27), Arizona Fab 2 N3 (2H27), Japan Fab 2 N3 (2028), plus N5→N3 tool conversion. A14 on track for 2028.
- Margin puts/takes: N2 ramp dilutes FY26 GM by **2–3pts**; overseas fabs dilute **2–3pts early / 3–4pts later**; N3 GM crosses corporate average in 2H26; Middle East situation may lift chemical/gas costs (unquantified). Long-term targets reaffirmed: GM **56%+** and ROE high-20s through cycle (Q1 FY26, raised from 53%+ a year ago).

**Monthly revenue (latest — pre-2Q26 print):** June 2026 revenue **NT$442.68bn (+6.2% MoM, +67.9% YoY)** — a record, and the first June MoM *increase* since 2021 (June had averaged −11% MoM the prior 4 years). Implies **Q2 revenue ~NT$1.27tn (~US$40.2bn), +12.3% QoQ / +34% YoY** — ~US$0.1bn above the US$39.0–40.2bn guidance high end (a record single quarter). H1 revenue ~US$76.1bn (+36.7% YoY, already above full-year 2022's US$75.9bn); H2 needs only +33.6% YoY to hit a full-year +35% target, and the run-rate implies ~US$167bn / ~+37% for FY26 (@jukan05, 2026-07-13; @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-13).

**2Q26 earnings preview / GM-upside call (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08):**
- **Reiterate Buy, PT NT$3,500 (top Buy pick), 42% upside** vs spot NT$2,465. 2Q26 on track to **beat** guidance in both USD and TWD (stronger-than-expected AI + general-server-CPU demand + FX tailwind); Fubon models **2Q26 GM ~67.1% (from 66.2%)**. 3Q26 guide expected to beat — Fubon expects TSMC to guide **revenue +10-12% QoQ** vs consensus HSD% QoQ (Fubon, 2026-07-08).
- **2H26 GM confidence up** — despite the market's N2-dilution worry, Fubon's checks say TSMC now has more confidence in the 2H26 GM trend on customers' **super-hot-run wafer orders with premium pricing**, an optimized product mix, and — most importantly — **faster-than-expected Arizona improvement: phase-1 GM already ~50% at fully-loaded UTR + 90% production yield**. Fubon thinks TSMC "very likely" **raises its FY26 full-year guide again to +30-35% YoY** (Fubon, 2026-07-08).
- **FY27 GM upside** — supply/demand gap **widens to 15-20% in FY27**, with advanced nodes (N2/N3) and advanced packaging becoming **more serious supply-chain bottlenecks** (general-server-CPU incremental demand a key driver). Fubon sees FY27 wafer-price hikes **starting at 10% for all customers incl. Apple**, lifting blended ASP more meaningfully than FY25/26 and pushing **full-year GM toward the 70% benchmark** (Fubon FY27 GM 68.8%; EPS NT$144.13 vs cons NT$127.84) (Fubon, 2026-07-08).
- **Capex / capacity in spotlight** — Fubon keeps FY26 capex at **US$56-57bn**, and now forecasts **FY27 capex US$75bn** (any further upside gated on ASML EUV support/delivery). By end-FY27: **N3 to ~210kwpm, N2 to ~120kwpm**, and **CoWoS to ~180kwpm** (AP7/AP8 "very full," designed for more than the CoWoS expansion; TSMC considering restructuring old AP fabs for further FY28 expansion → total CoWoS **210-220kwpm by end-2028**) (Fubon, 2026-07-08).
- **Second-source noise = supply-tightness reflection** — Fubon reads the YTD underperformance (Intel-competition + Apple-second-source noise) as reflecting TSMC's FY27 supply tightness, which hands TSMC the bargaining power to price more aggressively → more GM upside (Fubon, 2026-07-08).

**Capacity build (third-party estimates):**
- Front-end 12" managed capacity ~17.2m wafers (2024) → ~17.9m (2025) → ~18.5m (2026) → ~19.1m (2027); headline growth slows to 3–4% as TSMC converts N7→N5 and N5→N3 tools rather than adding gross capacity (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28).
- N2: ~40–45k WPM end-2025 → 65–70k end-2026 → 110–120k end-2027 (N2 nearly doubles in 2027); N2 GAA+backside-power requires net-new tooling vs N5/N3 tool reuse. N2 first-year revenue contribution ~6% in 2025, with ~60k capacity exiting Q4-25 but shipments lagging (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20; Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28).
- N3: ~140k WPM end-2025 → ~160k end-2027; N7/N5/N4 capacity declines 2026–27 on tool conversion (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28).
- Intel already booking ~10k WPM of N2 by end-2026 (Nova Lake outsourcing) — an incremental 2027 growth driver (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28).
- Long horizon: ~770k WPM of sub-7nm (1–7nm) capacity by 2030; US (Phoenix) only ~3–5% of total even by 2030 (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20).
- **Fab-by-fab leading-edge build (MS Research estimates):** see the "Leading-edge capacity by node & fab (MS)" table below — N3 →200–205k, N2/A16 →210k by 2028, A14 →~120k by 2029; MS runs above both UBS and the StoneX/Fubon figures here.

**Pricing / wafer-shortage / EUV (Conor O'Mara Asia trip, 2026-06-23):**
- 3nm "tight and nobody's cutting anything." **2026 wafer-price hikes by customer: AI/HPC ~+10%, consumer ~+5%, Apple low-single-digit → blended ASP ex-2nm only +3-4%.** **2027 wafer prices +≥10% across ALL customers including Apple**, with GM potentially reaching **high-60s to 70%**. Supply/demand gap widens to **15-20% in 2027**. (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23)
- **Wafer-shortage risk:** TSMC already at its prior peak of **~8M wafers/month** with limited new capacity, so the agentic-CPU surge could tip the system into a broad wafer shortage; Samsung acknowledged the same risk (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **EUV constrains capex upside:** TSMC has already **booked 40-45 EUV units for 2027** and will struggle to get more given competition from memory makers (DRAM EUV demand) — capping 2027 capex around **$70-75B** vs other brokers' $80-85B (consistent with the FY27 capex view above) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **H2'26 GM tone turned more positive** — flattish vs prior guidance of a 2H decline (super-hot-run orders + favorable AI/HPC mix) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **CPU outsourcing to AAC:** TSMC outsources Vera (NVIDIA) and Venus (AMD) CPU packaging to **AAC**, with KingSus benefiting from Unimicron capacity constraints (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **Aggressive SOIC expansion 2027-28:** each 10,000-unit SOIC line needs **15-20 hybrid bonders**; new SOIC use cases = silicon-photonics chips + Broadcom's next-gen TPU (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **⚠️ CONTESTED as of 2026-07-28 — Fubon says the SoIC expansion is being SLOWED, not accelerated, and cuts its own 4Q28 number.** Verbatim: *"Compared to our earlier forecast of **90k** capacity in 4Q28, we revise down our capacity expansion forecast to **70k in 4Q28**"* — a ~22% cut, and the stated reason is that **"TSMC [is] slowing down its SoIC expansion to prioritize the CoWoS expansion"** (which is also why Fubon flags **potential upside to its 220kwpm end-2028 CoWoS** number; end-2027 CoWoS held at **180kwpm**). **This directly contradicts the Redburn bullet immediately above and Susquehanna's "SoIC ramping meaningfully through 2028" (Hosseini, 07-11) — both remain on the page.** Read-through if Fubon is right: negative for the hybrid-bonder chain (**BESI** most exposed, TSMC being its largest customer), mildly positive for TSMC's own CoWoS output and for the AI-accelerator customers competing for it. Same note raises **ASE's FoCoS capacity to 50kwpm for 2027** (Fubon's own prior mark was 45kwpm by end-2027, itself raised from 30k on 06-05) "to support CPU and networking customers' demand" — the offload leg of the same prioritisation. (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)

**HBM supply chain — TSMC's dual role (base-die fab + CoWoS packaging):**
- TSMC sits on **both ends** of the HBM4 supply chain, collecting revenue twice on the same unit's journey: (1) as **base-die fabricator** — TSMC confirmed two process variants for HBM4 custom base dies, **N12 and N3**, fabricating base-die wafers on behalf of designers (NVIDIA, Broadcom, hyperscalers) since SK Hynix/Micron/most ASIC designers lack leading-edge logic capability in-house; and (2) as **CoWoS packager** — the base-die wafer TSMC fabs is consigned OUT to the HBM manufacturer (e.g. SK Hynix) for TSV formation + DRAM-die stacking, then shipped back and consigned back IN to TSMC for CoWoS packaging with the logic/XPU die (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11, p.25/41-42).
- Rationale for CoWoS-mandatory: HBM3E-class routing between XPU and HBM requires 1,000+ wires, a density unachievable on PCB/substrate — only an interposer (CoWoS) can route it (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11, p.3).
- TSMC is the acknowledged **leader in advanced packaging for HBM**, but memory makers retain the TSV-formation/stacking leverage — that IP stays proprietary to SK Hynix/Micron/Samsung, not TSMC, so TSMC's HBM moat is packaging-only, not full-stack (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11, p.43).
- TSMC's own hybrid-bonding (HB) experience in advanced logic is cited as the read-through for HBM: HB adoption timelines run long even where the performance case is clear, implying HBM HB (stacking without TSV) also lags (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11, p.13).

**Advanced packaging (CoWoS / SoIC / CPO):**
- In-house CoWoS: ~75–80k WPM exiting 2025; expanding to ~100–105k by end-2026 (TSMC not raising FY26 plan — CoW tools carry 6–9mo lead time, no time to accelerate AP7) (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28). **UBS (Sunny Lin) now raises TSMC CoWoS capacity to 130k WPM by end-2026 (from 120k) and 180k WPM by end-2027 (from 150k)**, citing re-accelerating expansion; backend/advanced packaging estimated at ~12%/15% of TSMC sales in 2026/27E, with advanced-packaging sales sustaining ~50% CAGR over the next 5 years — TSMC remains the largest CoWoS/advanced-packaging player even as Intel EMIB-T and TSMC's own CoPoS enter mass production in 2028 (UBS/Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01). AP8 already fully loaded; new AP7 phase 1 is Apple WMCM, phase 2 not ready until late-2026.
- **CoWoS ramp across customer segments (MS):** Morgan Stanley (Charlie Chan, Daniel Yen, Daisy Dai, Tiffany Yeh, Lucas Wang, Ethan Jia, Henry Zhao) published a 50-page "Greater China Semiconductors: TSMC preview and CoWoS update — CPU, GPU, ASIC, and Optical" report (Industry View: Attractive), detailing the CoWoS ramp broken out by CPU, GPU, ASIC, and optical customer segments (MS, 2026-06-29).
- **CoWoS/CoPoS fast-track (supply chain under gag order):** DIGITIMES reports TSMC is fast-tracking CoWoS capacity expansion and accelerating next-gen panel-level packaging (CoPoS), with the entire supply chain reportedly under a gag order regarding the rollout — broadly lifting Taiwan equipment, materials, and packaging suppliers (DIGITIMES, 2026-06-30).
- Offload strategy: TSMC routing non-AI-accelerator packaging (AMD Venice CPU, AVGO networking processors) to ASE FoCoS and SPIL to free in-house CoWoS for GPUs/ASICs; all AI accelerators stay on TSMC CoWoS (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28; Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). US CoWoS will come via Amkor outsourcing (~20–30k WPM by 2028, US$7bn invest); TSMC's two US AP fabs do SoIC (AP9) / CoPoS (2030), not CoWoS (Fubon, 2025-10-28).
- CPO: TSMC's **COUPE** (Compact Universal Photonic Engine) is emerging as the integration option of choice — NVIDIA showed COUPE optical engines at GTC-2025 (first to ship), AVGO and Ayar Labs on the roadmap. EIC on N7, PIC on SOI; TSMC is the only foundry with die-to-wafer hybrid bonding at scale (volume on AMD parts). Adopting COUPE locks customers into TSMC PICs (TSMC won't package SiPho wafers from other foundries) (CPO/SemiAnalysis, 2026-01-02).
- **COUPE, the full technical + competitive read — the source behind the bullet above, now read end-to-end (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01).** ⚠️ **_Framework note, Jan-26 vintage (~7 months old). It PREDATES the Susquehanna FAU-capacity checks (07-11), the ECTC/EMIB-T read (07-02) and the 2Q26 print below, and supersedes none of them — where a 2026 number contradicts it, the 2026 number stands. Read as the structural "why COUPE wins" underneath the capacity numbers this page already carries._** **(1) The stack, node by node.** EIC on **N7** — high-speed modulator drivers, TIAs *and* the heater controllers that do wavelength stabilisation; PIC on **SOI N65**, with TSMC supplying photonic circuit/layout design, verification and RF/noise/multi-wavelength simulation; the two bonded with **TSMC-SoIC-bond**, "a bumpless interface offering the shortest trace length possible without being monolithic." ★ **The number that carries the moat: at iso-power, SoIC-based optical engines offer more than 23x the bandwidth density of an OE integrated with bumps** — parasitics from trace length are the binding constraint in EIC↔PIC communication, and "3D integration is the only way to reach the performance goals of CPO from a bandwidth and power perspective." **(2) Coupling — and why it dictates customer redesign.** COUPE uses one common bumpless EIC-on-PIC structure for both flavours: **COUPE-GC** adds a **silicon lens on a 770µm Si-carrier plus a metal reflector directly under the grating coupler**, WoW-bonded to a CoW wafer; **COUPE-EC** adds an EC facet. "**TSMC also clearly has a higher preference for GC**," and grating coupling is what allows multiple fiber rows and OEs on the interposer. 🔴 **This is a switching cost imposed on customers, not just a process choice: [[AVGO]]'s prior CPO generations used edge coupling + MZMs, so moving to COUPE (GC + micro-ring modulators) means "Broadcom must essentially start fresh on some aspects of their technology."** **(3) Roadmap — substrate now, interposer later, and the bandwidth prize.** "The first iterations of COUPE will be optical engines on the substrate with the ultimate goal being able to put the OEs on the interposer," which offers far more I/O density and raises the ceiling to **~12.8 Tbit/s per individual OE ≈ ~4 Tbit/s/mm**; the gating problem is **scaling interposer size**, which is more expensive than package substrate. **(4) ★ CPO is CoWoS-area-hungry — a direct read into this page's packaging capacity debate.** Nvidia's **Spectrum-X photonics switch ASIC package substrate measures 110mm × 110mm**, against a **Blackwell package at 70mm × 76mm** ("a very big chip in its own right"); **36 known-good OEs must be flip-chip bonded onto the substrate BEFORE the interposer module is bonded** for the on-substrate CoWoS step, with yield and **warpage** problems worsening as interposer/substrate size scales. i.e. each CPO switch consumes materially more advanced-packaging area than a comparable accelerator — relevant to the CoWoS 130k→195k wpm and COUPE <0.5k→5-10k wpm tracks above. **(5) Why the modulator choice locks the PDK.** "TSMC's advanced CMOS expertise is well suited for fabricating **MRMs** with high-precision and great Q-factor," and MRMs plus their heaters "are already part of the PDK" — which is why [[MRVL]]/Celestial's **EAM** approach "will also have some work ahead of them integrating their EAM design into a foundry whereas other CPO companies can lean on TSMC COUPE." A second-order lock-in beyond the PIC-sourcing one already noted. **(6) The competitive verdict, stated plainly.** "Unlike its dominance in traditional CMOS logic, TSMC previously had a limited presence in silicon photonics, where Global Foundries and Tower Semi were the preferred foundry partners" — but TSMC "is the only foundry that has successfully demonstrated **die-to-wafer hybrid bonding at reasonable scale**" (AMD parts in volume), while **GF and Tower's "lack of leading edge CMOS and advanced packaging constrain their ability to provide the higher bandwidth OEs of the future"** and Intel "has faced significant challenges in pioneering this technology." On AVGO's forced migration: "**This highlights TSMC's technological edge, enabling them to secure wins even in optics, a domain where they have historically been considered weaker.**" And on intent: "**Like other major players, TSMC aims to capture as much of the value chain as possible.**" **(7) The COUPE customer roster as of Jan-26** — [[NVDA]] (COUPE OEs shown at GTC-2025, **first COUPE products to ship**; the Quantum-X OE is a **PIC on N65 + EIC on N6, hybrid bonded via COUPE**), **[[AVGO]] committed for future switch AND customer-accelerator roadmaps**, **Ayar Labs** (3rd-gen TeraPHY pivots off GF Fotonix to COUPE, 13.5 Tbit/s uni-di per OE), **Lightmatter** (COUPE + GF 45nm SPCLO), with Celestial AI (now [[MRVL]]) on **TSMC 5nm** for its Photonic Fabric chiplets and memory appliance. "Many CPO focused companies have indeed pivoted decisively towards making TSMC's COUPE as part of their go to market solution for the next few years." (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html)
- **COUPE FAU-constrained through YE27; high-volume CPO a 2028 story (Susquehanna / Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-11):** SIG checks put COUPE PIC capacity at **<0.5K wpm now, reaching only ~5-10K wpm by YE27**, with **FAU (fiber-array-unit) challenges holding COUPE shipments <0.5M units/qtr** (a contributor to Spectrum-X yield constraints) — the FAU layer is where the near-term supply-chain leverage sits. Broader packaging: SIG models **CoWoS rising from ~130K wpm (YE26) to ~195K wpm (YE28)**, **SoIC ramping meaningfully through 2028**, and **advanced-packaging spend per wafer rising to ~$8 over 2025-28 (from ~$5 in 2021-24)**. Adoption sequence: industry takes **NPO + 400G/lane pluggables first**, with broader CPO deployment at the **3.2T node ~2028**; COUPE is pivotal for the CPO-enabled switches expected to displace copper scale-up switches over time. **GLW's FAU / glass-packaging stack (co-developed with partners incl. GlobalFoundries) is a near-term alternative** to TSMC's CoWoS-L/COUPE platform (see [[GLW]]) (Susquehanna / Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-11).
- **Glass Core Substrate (new debate):** on June 11 in Japan, TSMC discussed Glass Core Substrate technology and named Ibiden and Innolux as development partners; MS's Derrick Yang reads this as signaling only proof-of-concept completion, not a mass-production commitment — adoption timing more likely 2H28 (vs the market's assumed "CoPoS" 2029 timeline), and Glass Core wouldn't hit full-year earnings until 2029 (the "Feynman" node mass-production year) (MS/Amir Amerian sales note w/ Derrick Yang, Charlie Chan, Meta Marshall, 2026-06-30). Rationale: TSMC is potentially pursuing Glass Core (replacing organic CCL substrate with glass + through-glass-vias/TGV) to close the performance gap vs Intel's EMIB-T for very large chips — a positive read for GLW (glass supplier) and Innolux/BOE (panel makers) if adopted (MS/Amir Amerian, 2026-06-30).
- **ECTC 2026 read — CoWoS is the incumbent platform being attacked on multiple fronts (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-02):** at ECTC (the industry's premier packaging conference), TSMC showed **direct-to-silicon microfluidic cooling** on a large GPU-like test vehicle (CoWoS-R, 3.3× reticle interposer, 4 SoC dies + 8 HBM): silicon **micropillars** formed on the die backside dissipated **4kW at 4 LPM and 5.3kW at 8 LPM** (uniform power >5kW across the vehicle), pulling ahead of lidded/lidless cold plates once TIM saturates — a thermal enabler for multi-kW packages (Microsoft showed a parallel direct-to-silicon microchannel approach on a real Nvidia GH200, ~50% lower package thermal resistance). But SemiAnalysis flags a competitive tell: TSMC ran **only 3 papers at ECTC vs Intel's 12 and Samsung's 11**, and **Intel's EMIB-T is positioned as "the most credible alternative to TSMC's CoWoS platform for large-package AI accelerators."** CoWoS is still ahead on several vectors (TSMC already deploys DTC/eDTC integration, is further along on integrated voltage regulators and active LSI, and remains the volume-proven ecosystem), but the round of interposer-less / panel-level / reconstituted alternatives (EMIB-T, FO-EB, glass-core, IBM DBrM, Unimicron silicon bridges) all target the size/wafer-utilization limits of TSMC's **circular** silicon-interposer platform — corroborating the CoWoS-vs-EMIB-T and CoPoS debates already on this page (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). Also relevant to TSMC's HBM role: **Marvell's custom-HBM base die** and **Nvidia's Feynman custom HBM** both move interface logic off the accelerator die — a shift in where HBM PHY value sits, though the base die still fabs on advanced logic (TSMC).

## N3 bottoms-up supply/demand model (UBS)
_Source: UBS (Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams), N3 capacity model accompanying the Q2 FY26 preview (2026-06-28). Quarterly columns are **kwpm** (monthly run-rate that quarter); annual columns (**2026E / 2027E**) are **thousand wafers** for the full year (≈ sum of the four quarters' kwpm × 3). Die size in mm²._

**Read:** the model shows N3 running at **102–110% utilisation every quarter through 2027** (a structural sold-out node), with the demand mix flipping hard from consumer to Cloud AI. Cloud AI goes from **7% of N3 demand in Q126E to 64% by Q426E and ~71–72% across 2027**, as Apple's consumer base (iPhone/Mac/iPad) gives way to NVIDIA Rubin/Rubin Ultra/Vera CPU, Broadcom (Google TPU v7/v8i, Meta MTIA v3, OpenAI), Alchip (Amazon Trainium 3) and MediaTek (Google TPU v8t). Total N3 demand grows from 1,837k wafers (2026E) to 2,266k (2027E) against TSMC capacity of 1,710k → 2,190k — i.e. demand persistently runs ahead of capacity, consistent with the "supply-demand gap widens to 15–20% in 2027" view elsewhere on this page and UBS's N3-capacity road to ~220kwpm by 2028E.

## Leading-edge capacity by node & fab (MS)
_Source: **Morgan Stanley Research estimates** — "Exhibit 16: TSMC fab roadmap" (MS TSMC coverage, Charlie Chan; exhibit date not stated — tentatively the MS Asia AI Supply Chain note, 2026-06-23, date inferred). Figures are **end-of-year installed capacity, kwpm** (monthly run-rate); ranges are MS's. Tainan F22 P7/P8/P9 (N2) is highlighted in the source as a recent addition._

**Read:** MS's fab-by-fab supply roadmap. N3 plateaus at **~200–205k by 2028** (broadly in line with the ~220kwpm-by-2028E road and the 102–110% N3 utilisation in the UBS N3 model above), while **N2/A16 is the real ramp — 45k (2025) → 90–100k (2026) → 150–170k (2027) → 210k (2028)** — led by Kaohsiung F22 (→100k) and the newly-added Tainan F22 P7/P8/P9 (→60k). **A14** (HsinChu F20 + Taichung F25) starts in 2027 and scales to **~120k by 2029**, with **A10** (Tainan F26, 5k 2029) and the German **N28/16** fab (F24, →40k by 2028) rounding out the build. MS's N2/N3 numbers run **above** both UBS and the StoneX/Fubon figures elsewhere on this page (MS N2/A16 →210k by 2028 vs UBS N2 ~160k by 2028E; MS N2/A16 150–170k for 2027 vs Fubon's N2 110–120k) — a genuine cross-broker divergence on the N2 ramp.

## Node-level customer demand (MS) — and vs the UBS N3 model
_Source: **Morgan Stanley Research estimates** — node-by-customer demand breakdowns (Ex.8 2nm, Ex.10 3nm, Ex.11 5/4nm; MS TSMC coverage, Charlie Chan; date inferred 2026-06-23, MS Asia AI Supply Chain). Ex.9 (N2-family capacity, "70% CAGR 2026-28e") is sourced to **TSMC**. **The kwpm figures below are read off MS's stacked-bar charts (no gridline labels) → ESTIMATE, ±~10-15 kwpm; directional, not precise.** Units: k 12"-equivalent wafers/month (kwpm)._

**MS demand by node (chart-read, kwpm):**

| Node | 2024e | 2025e | 2026e | 2027e | Shape |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|---|
| 5/4nm | ~150 | **~185** | ~160 | ~150 | **peaks 2025e, then declines** as AI/HPC migrates up-node |
| 3nm (N3) | ~62 | ~103 | ~167 | ~218 | steep ramp; NVDA/AVGO/AI-ASIC stack rising, Apple/QCOM/MTK consumer base flat-to-down |
| 2nm (N2) | ~0 | ~4 | ~58 | ~145 | inflects 2026→27; AAPL the largest single block, then AMD/AI-ASIC/AVGO |

_(5/4nm history: ~22 (2020) → ~52 → ~103 → ~123 → ~150 → ~185 (2025e peak). 3nm history: ~1 (2022) → ~15 → ~62 → ~103 → ~167 → ~218.)_

**MS vs UBS on N3 (3nm) demand** — the one apples-to-apples cross-check against the UBS N3 model above (2026-06-28). UBS reports annual k-wafers + quarterly kwpm; converting UBS to a comparable monthly run-rate (annual ÷ 12 = full-year average; Q4 = year-exit):

| N3 demand (kwpm) | 2026e | 2027e |
|---|--:|--:|
| **MS** (Ex.10, chart-read) | ~167 | ~218 |
| UBS — full-year avg (1,837k / 2,266k ÷ 12) | 153 | 189 |
| UBS — year-exit (Q4 run-rate) | 180 | 194 |
| **MS vs UBS avg** | +9% | **+15%** |
| **MS vs UBS exit** | −7% | **+12%** |

**Read:** MS and UBS agree closely on **2026** N3 demand (MS ~167 sits between UBS's 153 avg and 180 exit), but **MS runs ~12–15% hotter on 2027 N3 (~218 vs UBS 189 avg / 194 exit)**. Since both shops put N3 *capacity* at ~190k for 2027 (MS fab roadmap above; UBS Q427E 190 kwpm), MS implies a tighter node — ~**115% utilisation** vs UBS's stated 102–110%. Both reinforce the "N3 structurally sold out / supply-demand gap 15–20% in 2027" view elsewhere on this page; MS just sits at the more-oversubscribed end. There is no 2nm demand curve in the UBS material on the page, so N2 isn't directly comparable — but MS's 2nm demand (~145k 2027e) sits just under the MS N2/A16 capacity roadmap (150–170k 2027), i.e. N2 still filling.

**Footnote — TSMC's "70% CAGR" vs the MS roadmap:** TSMC's Ex.9 claims N2-family capacity grows at a **70% CAGR 2026→2028e**; the MS fab roadmap (90–100k → 210k) implies only **~49%** ((210/95)^½−1). The two reconcile only off different 2026 bases — 210k @ 70% CAGR requires a ~**73k** 2026 base vs MS's 90–100k. Treat TSMC's "70%" as its own framing off a lower/narrower (likely N2-only) base, not consistent with MS's bundled N2/A16 90–100k→210k path.
