_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo Felipe TSM 1Q26.xlsm`, 2026-06-10)._

| | 2024 | 2025E | 2026E | 2027E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| EPS (NT$) | 45.3 | 66.3 | 102.5 | 143.5 |
| Revenue (US$bn) | 90 | 122 | 165 | — |
| Gross margin | ~56% | ~60% | ~66% | — |

**House read:** EPS NT$45 → NT$143 (24-27E, +40/+46/+55/+40%); USD revenue ~+30-36%/yr; GM expanding to ~66% on the ramp of N2. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-10)

## House sensitivity - 2026-28 capex, EUV, revenue and 70% GM bridge (2026-07-17)

_Additive house exercise; does not supersede the official Capstone model, Bloomberg consensus or broker estimates. Full arithmetic, input tags, guardrails and sources: [TSMC 2026-2028 capex/capacity/revenue/GM70 bridge](../TSM/TSMC_2026_2028_Capex_Capacity_Revenue_GM70_House_Bridge_2026-07-17.md)._

| HOUSE ESTIMATE | 2026 | 2027 | 2028 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Total capex | US$62bn | US$80bn | US$100bn |
| Advanced-process capex (75%) | US$46.5bn | US$60bn | US$75bn |
| EOY capacity: A14 / N2-A16 / N3 | 0 / 95 / 185kwpm | 30 / 160 / 200kwpm | 60 / 210 / 220kwpm |
| Commercial A14+N2+N3 wafers | 2.34m | 3.87m | 5.10m |
| Advanced-node revenue | US$56.5bn | US$123.3bn | US$191.6bn |
| Other nodes + packaging/services | US$115.5bn | US$113.5bn | US$111.6bn |
| **Total revenue** | **US$172.0bn** | **US$236.8bn** | **US$303.2bn** |

- **2028 ASP sensitivity:** requested +10% change gives effective A14 / N2 / N3 ASP of **US$41.6k / US$42.6k / US$32.8k**, adding ~US$17.4bn versus the prior US$285.8bn 2028 anchor. The residual-company bridge is **PARTIAL / directional**, not an official forecast.
- **Bristlemoon check:** **11 EUV tools/25kwpm N2, US$240m/tool, US$4.7bn total fab capex/10kwpm and 45 TSMC tools in 2027/28**. Forty-five tools cost US$10.8bn directly, support ~102kwpm and imply ~US$48.2bn all-in fab capex. US$100bn can theoretically fund ~93 all-in EUV equivalents, or ~70 within the 75% advanced envelope; ASML delivery and commissioning are the practical constraints (Bristlemoon Capital, June 2026).
- **A14 lithography (HOUSE ESTIMATE):** +20% Low-NA intensity versus N2 -> **13.2 tools/25kwpm**, 31.7 tools for 60kwpm and ~US$29.5bn total fab capex. Annual N2/A16+A14 requirements become **44.4/45 tools in 2027** and **37.8/45 in 2028** (ASML Investor Day, 2024-11-14; Bernstein/David Dai, 2026-07-06; house sensitivity).

| 2027 GM-to-70% bridge, fixed volume/COGS | Required pricing |
|---|---:|
| Start at current 66.0%; N2 alone | **+53.5%; N2 ASP US$56.6k** - implausible |
| Conditional 68.8%; N2 alone | **+16.1%; N2 ASP US$42.8k** |
| Conditional 68.8%; uniform N2/N3/N5 | **+5.5-6.0%**; midpoint 5.7% |
| Conditional 68.8%; differentiated | **+6.55% N2/N3; +3.28% N5** |

The 68.8% starting point applies Fubon's FY27 GM to the house revenue base and is a conditional sensitivity, not Fubon's node model. JPM expects ~8-10% N2/N3 hikes and milder pricing elsewhere. An illustrative +10% / +10% / +5% N2/N3/N5 case reaches ~70.6% GM, but the N5 point is house. Both independent reviews returned **PARTIAL**: algebra passes; N5 revenue, node COGS and residual-company revenue remain directional (Fubon, 2026-07-08; JPM/Gokul, 2026-07-08/09).

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** EPS NT$45.3 / 66.3 / **102.5** / 143.5 (2024-27E, +40%/+46%/+55%/+40%); USD revenue $90B / $122B / $165B (2024-26E, ~+30%/+36%/+35%); GM expanding ~56%→60%→66%, op margin ~46%→51%→58%. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-10)
- **Bull:** Sole credible leading-edge supplier — ~70% foundry share, Samsung ~1yr behind, Intel ~1.5–2yrs behind on yield/density (TSMC former exec, 2026-04-14). Wafers remain the binding AI constraint into 2027 ("we expect this to continue to be very tight," C.C. Wei, Q1 FY26). Bernstein (Mark Li) OP, PT **$430** vs ~$351, calling TSM the "most trustworthy compounder in AI" — 28% EPS CAGR thru '28, +50% EPS this year, '27 capex $68B, trading at ~20% discount to SOX (briefing, 2026-05-18). AI-accelerator revenue still guided to mid-/high-50s % CAGR 2024–29 (Bruce Lu Q, Q1 FY26). Pricing has real headroom — N3 hot-run/super-hot-run premiums rising further in Q3, regular N3 hikes ~10% and N2 ~5–6% into Jan-27 (Gokul/JPM CoWoS update, 2026-05-27); AI-related wafer prices set for 10%+ hikes in 2026 on top of the value-priced packaged-chip model (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28). GW-scale data-center economics are a structural tailwind: each GW ≈ 7,200 NVL72 racks ≈ ~33k TSMC wafers and ~US$1.0–1.5bn of TSMC revenue (foundry + CoWoS + other BOM); ~40GW of announced sovereign/hyperscaler projects imply ≥1.32m incremental wafers and ~US$60bn of potential revenue (≈50% of FY25 sales), before counting the major US hyperscalers (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28). AI-accelerator die (800mm²+) run only ~50–60% yield vs ~90% on consumer SoC, so wafer demand per shipped unit nearly doubles — a hidden volume amplifier (Fubon, 2025-10-28). Intel tie-up notwithstanding, Intel likely outsources more leading-edge to TSMC (Bernstein deck, May-2025). **Server-CPU TAM is a fresh leg:** UBS (Sunny Lin) sizes the server-CPU market at **~$170bn by 2030** (units 23m→63m, 30% CAGR) and models server CPUs needing ~200kwpm of leading-edge capacity by 2030 (from 71kwpm in 2025), of which TSMC serves **122kwpm — a ~$44bn TSMC revenue opportunity (11% of sales) by 2030, up from $3.7bn/3% in 2025**; this drove UBS to raise TSMC sales 3-7% / EPS 4-9% in 2027-30 (UBS "Quantifying the server CPU opportunity", 2026-05-21). **Pricing tightening into 2027:** Fubon estimates the 2027 supply-demand gap widens to **15-20%**, N2/N3 utilization runs 110-120%, super-hot-run orders carry a **15-20% wafer premium**, and 2027 wafer-price hikes start at **10% including Apple** (vs only +1-2% for Apple in 2026, +10% AI/HPC / +5% consumer) — lifting blended ASP ≥10% and pushing GM to a **68-70%** range; Fubon's FY27 rev NT$7,057bn (+37%, a Street high, vs cons NT$6,057bn) and FY27 EPS **NT$144** (vs cons NT$123) (Fubon "2027 semi outlook", 2026-06-05). On overseas margin dilution, Fubon's checks say **Arizona P1 GM has already reached ~50% after two years** — better than management's conservative framing (Fubon, 2026-06-05).
- **GW→WAFER CONVERSION CARD, NOW WITH A NODE SPLIT — NEW 2026-08-25, and it does NOT agree with the Fubon card above on the logic leg.** **Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis)** gives the per-GW wafer bill for **Vera Rubin** split by node: **55,000 N3 wafers + 6,000 N5 wafers + 170,000 DRAM wafers per GW ≈ 231k 300mm wafers/GW.** **Source: Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis), in interview — relayed by Felipe, 2026-08-25.** ⚠️ **VINTAGE, not attribution, is the open item: Patel dates the figures to an interview “a few months” earlier, so carry them as ~2Q26 vintage rather than a fresh mark — which matters because the SOCAMM spec has been halved since (see the sensitivity below).** **House reconciliation on this wiki's own inputs (Capstone, 2026-08-25).** Denominator first: VR200 NVL144 = 72 Rubin packages (144 GPU dies) + 36 Vera CPUs per rack, and the rack draw is **firm at ~220-225kW** — TrendForce (~225kW, 2026-06-28, on [[NVDA]]) and Bernstein (220kW compute-TDP, 2026-07-20) agree independently ⇒ **~4,440-4,545 racks/GW**. ⚠️ JPM's **330-444kW VR200 'rack design power'** is nameplate, not draw — never build a rack count on it. **(1) The DRAM leg checks out.** 72 × 288GB HBM4 = 20.7TB + 36 × 768GB SOCAMM = 27.6TB per rack (the post-cut spec; the pre-cut 1.5TB/CPU is what ties NVDA's official *75TB of fast memory per NVL144* to 20.7 + 54). At MU's own HBM silicon-displacement ratio (**3:1 at HBM3E → ~4:1 at HBM4E**, 2026-08-15) and ~2,450 GB per conventional/LPDDR wafer, that is **141-205k DRAM wafers/GW** (central ~155-186k, depending on the 288GB-vs-192GB HBM SKU mix — JPM assumes 70% take the downsized spec) — so **170k sits mid-corridor**. **(2) The logic leg is ~2x too high for the compute rack alone.** 55k N3 + 6k N5 = **61k foundry wafers/GW = 13.4 logic wafers/rack**, against **~5.5-6.5 from a die-area bottom-up** (144 Rubin dies at ~750mm², 65-70 gross dies/wafer, **Fubon's own 50-60% AI-die yield** ⇒ ~4.0 wafers; + Vera CPU ~0.5 + NVLink-6 switch ~1.0 + CX9/BlueField ~1.0) ⇒ **~25-30k N3 wafers/GW** — and against **the Fubon card's own 4.6 wafers/rack** (33k ÷ 7,200 NVL72 racks). It is reconcilable only if the 55k counts the wider Rubin-family silicon (standalone **Vera CPU** racks, **LPU** racks, **Spectrum-6/CX9 CPO**) or a far worse yield than 50-60%; ⚠️ **Rubin CPX cannot close it** at JPM's assumption of **144 CPX/rack at only 10-15% attach in 2027/28**. **(3) The 6k N5 is the right size for the HBM4 base-die leg** — 8 stacks × 72 packages = 576 base dies/rack on advanced FinFET (AMAT DRAM/AP masterclass: HBM4 base die moves to advanced FinFET) at ~110mm² ⇒ ~1.2 wafers/rack ⇒ **~5.5k/GW**; the NVLink-6 switch leg is the same order (~4.5k/GW) — **one of the two, not both**. ➜ **For TSMC the live question is only the logic leg: 33k (Fubon, Blackwell) vs 61k (Patel, Rubin) per GW is the difference between Fubon's ~$1.0-1.5bn and ~$1.9-2.8bn of TSMC revenue per GW (scaling Fubon's own $/wafer 61k/33k)** — which is why the scope of that 55k has to be pinned down before it goes into any TAM. Ledger: [`wafers-per-gw`](_meta/assumptions.md).
- **Bear:** Margin dilution stack is real and structural — overseas (US/Japan/Germany) wafers genuinely cost more (US labor ~30% higher) yet TSMC prices uniformly, so Arizona/JASM/ESMC ramps compress GM (former exec, 2026-04-14; CFO Q4 FY25). Pricing capped by monopoly-scrutiny fear, not just customer goodwill — "we could have 70–75% gross margins… they're worried about being called a monopoly" (former exec, 2026-04-14). Antitrust is the binding lid on the multiple: with ~2/3 leading-edge share, the legal team hired ~40% more antitrust lawyers in 2024; strong earnings/profit growth but the stock can't re-rate while geopolitical/antitrust risk sits ahead — TSMC even *wants* Intel to survive to relieve antitrust pressure (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). US pressure mounting: CHIPS-to-equity Intel stake, tariff leverage, and a push to lift TSMC's US commitment from US$65bn toward US$200bn (~US$35bn+ gap) (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). Demand-double-counting risk: customers' forecasts historically sum to multiples of the real end market; TSMC itself discounts them (former exec). Consumer softness from memory price hikes hitting price-sensitive PC/smartphone (C.C. Wei, Q1 FY26). Packaging is the bottleneck shifting away from TSMC: CoWoS increasingly farmed to ASE/Amkor, EMIB (Intel) and panel-level CoPoS threaten to move value to substrate vendors (Gokul, 2026-05-27); EMIB-T flagged to make a meaningful contribution from 2H26 for Google TPU v9 and Meta MTIA as a multi-vendor hedge against CoWoS scarcity (Q425 Techknowledge/Jefferies, 2025-12-17).
- **Barron's "Fab Five" framing (2026-06-01):** Barron's cover story "Rally in Chips" (Adam Levine) names TSM one of the "Fab Five" pillar AI names — the enabler of the fabless model — while flagging Taiwan-concentration risk explicitly: **"80% of its long-term assets are still in Taiwan."** Valuation framing: **2027 P/E 21x, PEG 0.58x**, EPS "expected to grow another 90% by 2027" (Barron's, 2026-06-01). PEG 0.58x on a still-cheap-for-growth read reinforces the Bernstein/UBS "compounder trading at a discount" thesis above; the 80%-Taiwan figure is the sharpest single-number version of the geopolitical-concentration bear point already on this page.
- **Where the sell-side stands:** 🔴 **Bernstein OP, PT RAISED to NT$3,300 / ADR US$554 (from NT$2,780 / US$430) on 2026-08-10** — same 20x forward P/E, entirely driven by estimates (FY26/27/28E EPS NT$110.70 / 144.85 / 188.70, up 8.9% / 16.2% / 19.9%; 13-29% above consensus), on the CPU-as-third-growth-wave thesis; ~39% upside vs the NT$2,380 close (Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai, "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU", 2026-08-10). That puts Bernstein at the top of the Street range on this page, just above Fubon's NT$3,500-equivalent cluster on ADR terms. **Prior Bernstein marks, retained for the rating history:** OP, $430 (2026-05-18), **reiterated OP US$430 (NT$2,780; 20x forward Q5-8 EPS NT$139) in the 2026-07-06 "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive" note** — where the High-NA read-through is that **TSMC is the slowest of the leading-edge logic players to adopt High-NA (~2030, at A10)**, having publicly called HNA "too expensive," but is still likely to migrate; Bernstein reads TSMC's caution as prudence (optimizing existing tech + waiting for HNA availability/throughput to improve so cost-per-exposure falls) rather than a rejection of HNA's merits (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06). China H20/B20/MI308 export restrictions seen as ~1% revenue hit, effectively negligible given lead times + prepayments and no China AI baked beyond 2025 (Bernstein deck, May-2025). **JPM (Gokul Hariharan) Overweight, PT raised to NT$3,100 (from NT$2,500), 20x 12m-fwd, price NT$2,460 (2026-07-06)** — raising FY26/27/28E EPS +5%/+10%/+16%; expects 2Q26 GM close to 70% (JPMe 69.5%) staying high-60s into 2H26/1H27; raises Datacenter-AI revenue CAGR to 69% (2024-29E) from 59% (vs TSMC guide mid-to-high-50s); raises FY26/27/28 capex to $58bn/$78bn/$84bn (JPM "2Q26 preview: Stronger GMs and aggressive capacity plans," Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-06). JPM (Gokul) also constructive on packaging — raising CoWoS capacity forecasts again to 115k/175k/220k WPM by end-26/27/28, total CoWoS consumption +86% this year / +30% next (2026-05-27). Fubon (distributed by Jefferies) Buy, TP NT$1,650 (~20x FY26 EPS NT$73.5), longer-term bull case NT$2,025 (22x FY27 EPS NT$91.2); FY27 the inflection on N2/A16 + GW data-center demand (Fubon/Jefferies, 2025-10-28). Fubon has since **raised its TP to NT$3,500** (post stock-split-equivalent base; top pick in Taiwan semis) on the ASP/GM upside (Fubon, 2026-06-05). **Fubon reiterated Buy / PT NT$3,500 (42% upside; still its top Buy pick) in the 2026-07-08 "Expecting GM upside going forward" earnings preview** — 2Q26 on track to beat (GM ~67.1%), 3Q26 guide expected to beat (rev +10-12% QoQ vs cons HSD%), FY26 guide likely raised to +30-35% YoY, FY27 GM toward the 70% benchmark on a 15-20% S/D gap + 10%-for-all wafer hikes incl. Apple; FY26/27 EPS NT$103.90/NT$144.13 vs cons NT$99.52/NT$127.84; FY27 capex US$75bn; N3/N2 to 210k/120kwpm and CoWoS to 180kwpm by end-FY27, CoWoS 210-220kwpm by end-2028 (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08). UBS **Buy, PT raised NT$2,650 → NT$3,000** (23x 2027E PE, on a 23% LT earnings CAGR), FY27/28 EPS NT$130.5/170.0, ~5-12% above consensus, viewing competition from Intel/Samsung as "manageable given the much larger expected TAM" (UBS, 2026-05-21). **UBS has since raised again — Buy, PT NT$3,650** (share price NT$2,350, priced 24 Jul 2026; market cap US$1,931,966m; 2026/27E P/E 21.6x/15.8x, P/BV 8.0x/5.8x, ROE 43.4%/42.7%), per the Figure-1 valuation table of its advanced-packaging deep dive — up from the NT$3,400 carried on this page since 06-28 and now the second-highest PT here after Fubon's NT$3,500 (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?", 2026-07-27). Consistent Street view that supply stays tight through 2027. **TD Cowen (Krish Sankar) is the lone cautious voice — Hold, PT $400 on the TSM-US ADR vs a $436.98 spot (i.e. a PT ~8% BELOW spot)** — the only rating on the page implying downside, a sharp contrast to the NT$3,000-3,500 (~US$95-110 ADR-equivalent) bull cluster; its capex assumptions are actually the most aggressive on the page (**$56-60B 2026 / $80B 2027 / $100B 2028** — the 2028 figure above JPM $84B and UBS $95B), and it sees TSMC securing **38 of ASML's 88 EUV tools in 2027, a bit below its ~45-tool demand** (an EUV-constraint read consistent with Redburn's "40-45 booked" and the capex-ceiling debate on this page). TSMC is adding 3nm capacity via Fab 18, Arizona, a possible Japan 3nm fab, and converting some 28nm→3nm in Taiwan (Fab 15) (TD Cowen "Earnings Preview: Puts & Takes", 2026-07-09). **Post-2Q26 (Outlook, 2026-07-16→20):** **BofA (Haas Liu) BUY, PO NT$3,100** (20x '27E; FY26/27/28 EPS raised to NT$110.2/153.2/182) and **MS (Charlie Chan) OW, PT raised to NT$2,988** (via the MS "Global Reflections" recap; primary on Matrix) join the NT$3,000–3,500 bull cluster — leaving **TD Cowen (Hold, $400 ADR)** still the lone cautious voice. **Arete (Jim Fontanelli) reiterate Buy, PT raised to NT$3,366 (from NT$3,354, 45% upside vs NT$2,320)** — rolled to FY28E; FY26 rev NT$5.45tr (+43% YoY) / EPS NT$108.42 (+64%), scaling to **FY28E rev NT$9.6tr (+28%), GM 67.5%, OpM 60.9%, EPS NT$190.4 (from NT$183.4 prior)**; PT = avg of SOX 25.1x-less-32% + TSMC 5yr 16.4x + DCF (WACC 8.2% vs 8% prior, 30% '25-30 sales CAGR) (Arete "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).
- **🆕 JPM PT NT$3,300 (2026-10-01 /run-inbox):** J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan raises the Jun-27 PT to **NT$3,300 from NT$3,200** (~20x 12m-forward P/E), OW, with 2027E EPS NT$141.37 → 147.80 and capex US$64/86/100bn for 2026-28 (J.P. Morgan "TSMC: Expect strong 3Q26 results, healthy 4Q26 guidance and a faster pace of capacity ramp: Raise PT to NT$3300", 2026-09-30). The NT$3,100 (07-06) and NT$3,200 (08-10 / 09-17) marks above are history; see `## Changelog`. Fubon reiterates Buy, TP NT$3,500 in its Sept-2026 deck.
- **🆕🔭 JPM 3Q26 preview CALL (Gokul Hariharan (J.P. Morgan) @ JPM 'TSMC: 3Q26 Preview' webinar, 2026-10-01 - Capstone notes (Notion transcript)):** the variant-perception items vs this page are (1) **2027 GM *"can't reach 70%"*** — JPM 67-68% on N2 depreciation and an N2 yield/margin penalty that lasts to 4Q27 / 1Q28, 70%+ only in some 2028 quarters, vs Fubon's 70.2% / 70.4% 3Q-4Q27 path and the Capstone internal view that 70% comes in 2027 (GS-Communacopia debrief, 2026-09-16); (2) **capex bias ABOVE the new US$86bn / US$100bn** if the whole iPhone family moves to A14 in 2H28 (60-80k wafer starts by 2Q28, *"not in our numbers"*), while JPM's A14 30-40k wpm by end-2028 is half the house sensitivity's 60k; (3) **blended wafer price +~25% in 2027** (like-for-like +10% N3/N2, first-in-cycle +3-5% N7/12/16nm) — the same +25-26% as the 07-08 call, now with a mature-node leg; (4) **60-70% of future CoWoS DEMAND to OSATs**, no royalty (a demand-share basis; the written note's 23% / 30% is a capacity-share basis). All JPM model / checks, not guidance — TSMC gives no 2027 numbers before January. Detail in the Q3-window block (2026-10-01, notion-ingest).
- **🆕🔴 STIFEL INITIATES FOUNDRY COVERAGE — TSM at BUY, TP US$515 (ADR), ~9.0x FY28E EV/SALES — AND THE DEFINING FEATURE OF THE CALL IS THAT IT CLAIMS *NO* ESTIMATE EDGE: *"We are in-line with consensus on TSM, with our sales and EPS estimates relatively similar to that of the street"* (Stifel · Sahej Singh, "Foundries: The Bottleneck — Initiation of Foundry Coverage", 2026-09-01).** ➤ **That is the analytically useful part, so log it as the point rather than the caveat: a new house joins at Buy with ~35-40%-type upside and explicitly declines to argue the numbers. Its own FY28E model — revenue $296.5bn, operating margin 58.7%, ADR EPS $28.83 — sits essentially on top of the street bar it reports. So the call is entirely on MULTIPLE and CYCLE ENTRY: TSM framed as a *"long horizon 'must-own' stock"* and an INDUSTRIAL-CYCLICAL TROUGH-ON-TROUGH GROWER that *"should trade like an industrial cyclical, so investors should look to double-down during trough periods"* — the recommendation is to *"focus on opportunistic entry points"*, not to underwrite an estimate gap.** ⚠️ **CURRENCY/LINE BASIS, stated so nobody nets targets across two securities: Stifel's $515 is on the US ADR and is therefore comparable to Barclays' US$650 (07-17) and Bernstein's US$554 (08-10) — NOT to BofA's NT$3,100 (08-31), Fubon's NT$3,500 or MS's NT$2,988, which are Taiwan-line (2330.TW) targets. This is an ADDITION to the PT ladder, not a revision of anyone's mark.**
  - ⚠️⚠️ **THE CONSENSUS BAR STIFEL SAYS IT IS JOINING — LOGGED AS STIFEL'S CHARACTERISATION OF THE STREET, SOURCED BY STIFEL TO *FACTSET*, AND NOT AS STIFEL'S OWN ESTIMATES NOR AS BLOOMBERG CONSENSUS:** *"~94% of analysts rated Buy, per FactSet. Price targets have ranged from ~$500-650. The street sits at approximately **$296B revenue, 59.8% operating margin, and $29.36 EPS for FY28E**."* ⚠️ **Basis discipline: this page's own consensus block is BBG and is CY-based; the FY28E figures above are a broker's FactSet-sourced read printed inside its own note, so they must NOT be used to score Stifel (or anyone else) against "consensus". Their value here is as the SENTIMENT reading — a 94%-Buy, $500-650-PT tape is a crowded long, which is precisely the setup Stifel's "buy the trough" framing is built for.**
  - **THESIS PILLARS, and two of the three are confirmations of material already on this page rather than net-new: (i) *"TSMC has raised its long-term model TWICE IN TWELVE MONTHS — to a ~25% revenue CAGR with AI-accelerator revenue compounding at a mid-to-high-50s% CAGR (2024-29) — while lifting the through-cycle gross margin FLOOR to 56%+ despite absorbing overseas-fab and N2-ramp dilution"*, with **2026 revenue guided +30%+ in USD**; (ii) **advanced packaging as a compounding differentiator — *"CoWoS is only ~10% of TSMC's wafer volume yet it GATES AI-accelerator shipments industry-wide, and management has repeatedly pushed the supply/demand balance point out (now 2028-29)"***; (iii) **value capture broadening beyond the wafer — *"TSMC is compounding three curves at once: node, package, and now photons."*** ➤ **The 56%+ GM floor and the mid-to-high-50s AI CAGR are already carried here from management; Stifel CONFIRMS them from a new house. The genuinely net-new framings are the CoWoS-as-~10%-of-volume-but-100%-of-the-gate ratio, the balance point being pushed to 2028-29, and the three-curve (node/package/photons) value-capture frame.**
  - **VALUATION FRAME AND THE SENSITIVITY GRID (the mechanical version of "the multiple does the work"):** Stifel puts TSM at **~8-11x EV/FY2 sales — a leadership premium to the foundry group yet BELOW AI-levered fabless peers — and argues *"the Taiwan-strait discount arguably caps the multiple despite twice-raised guidance at scale."*** Its published price-to-sales × 3-year-revenue-CAGR grid runs **6x→11x against 30%→40% CAGR**, with the **9x / 34%-CAGR cell = +20%** and the **8x / 32% cell = +2%**; conclusion: *"the sensitivity scenario favors upside potential relative to downside risk."* ⚠️ **Stifel deliberately valuates on SALES not EPS, for two stated reasons — to standardise against a turnaround [[INTC]] and to normalise industrial-cyclicality (high-growth periods flatter P/E and vice versa). Note the frame it implies: on this grid the entire upside is the multiple, since revenue CAGR is a street-consensus input.**
  - **RISKS STIFEL NAMES, and one of them widens a number this page already carries: (i) *"a material escalation across the Taiwan Strait would impair TERMINAL VALUE"*; (ii) 🔴 **MARGIN DILUTION OVERSHOOTS THE FLOOR — *"overseas-fab dilution is to widen from 2-3pp toward 3-4pp ANNUALLY, layered on 2-3pp from N2 ramp; if utilization softens, margin floor gets tested"*** — i.e. the dilution stack in `## Risks` on this page ("2-3pts early → 3-4pts later") is restated as an ANNUAL run-rate that compounds against a 56%+ floor rather than a one-time step; (iii) **AI-capex digestion / customer concentration — *"a hyperscaler pause would hit the AI-accelerator CAGR."*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/STIFEL_Initiating_Coverage_on_the_Foundries_-_TSM_Buy_GFS_Buy_TSEM_Buy.html)
- **Moat durability — expert calls (2026-06-30):** a Capstone expert call with a former Samsung EVP (now a photonics-startup CEO) described TSMC as the "undisputed leader" in leading-edge foundry with **85-95% market share** (85-90% overall, up to 95% in some advanced nodes) — Samsung a distant second, capable of taking only 10-15% share from TSMC overflow demand. The same expert called the market's **$300bn WFE-spending** narrative "completely unrealistic," expecting growth past $200bn but not to the advertised highs, given fab-capacity and power constraints (Capstone expert call/former Samsung EVP, 2026-06-30). Separately, an AlphaSense expert call with a former AMD Director views TSMC's **CoWoS as the clear leading advanced-packaging technology versus Intel's EMIB-T**; switching packaging suppliers is seen as complex/costly given extensive re-qualification, and TSMC's moat is seen as difficult to erode over the next 5-10 years given its scale of capital investment and non-competitive customer stance (AlphaSense expert call/former AMD Director, 2026-06-30; also flagged via AlphaSense Company Profile Watchlist Alert, 2026-06-30).
- **Jefferies (Silk Road desk, Cristina Titu) — shares +3% in Asia trading (2026-07-01)** as investors position for what could be a **70% gross margin print** next quarter — echoes the Jefferies buy-side GM survey debate already on this page (Jefferies/Cristina Titu, 2026-07-01).
- **🆕 GM DEBATE — THE COMPANY DECLINED TO BLESS A ~70% 4Q26, AND THE HOUSE MODEL NEVER ASSUMED ONE (2026-09-10 → house cross-check).** Asked by Capstone whether the 4Q revenue ramp plus lagging depreciation gets to *"almost 70 percent of gross margin for the last quarter"*, TSMC IR answered that the 3-4pt N2 dilution in 2H26 is *"very real"*, *"will continue next year"*, and on 4Q *"we cannot tell you. We'll tell you in October"*; margins should *"sustainably trend in the right direction… we're not the rocket ship. That's probably the memory guys"* (TSMC IR @ investor group Q&A, 2026-09-10 — Capstone Notion transcript). **House cross-check (⚠ rough house arithmetic, inputs stated, not a model change):** the Capstone CY26 GM of **66.0%** (Snapshot) on BBG CY26 revenue NT$5.44tn, with 1H actuals (1Q NT$1,134bn @ 66.2% · 2Q NT$1,270bn @ 67.7%) and 3Q at the guide midpoint (~NT$1.43tn @ 66%), implies a **4Q26 GM of only ~64.5%** — i.e. the house already sits *below* the 3Q midpoint for 4Q and embeds the "dilution is real" case; IR's pushback does not threaten the **NT$102.50** CY26 house EPS. A 70% 4Q would lift CY26 GM to ~67.6% and add roughly **+NT$3/sh (~3%)** to house EPS (17.5% tax, 25.9bn shares). The CY27 house **NT$143.50** sits beside a BBG CY27 GM of 66.4%, which is where IR's "dilution continues next year" meets the page's ≥10% 2027 price-hike thesis — **the October 4Q guide is the first hard read on which wins.**
- **Citi (Laura Chen) — Taiwan design-service read-through for TSMC's ASIC/advanced-packaging demand (2026-07-06):** Citi prefers **MediaTek & GUC** for AI-ASIC growth into 2027, and flags the supply chain (foundry / advanced packaging / HBM / ABF) as **remaining tight** — a demand read-through supportive of TSMC's ASIC business and CoWoS/advanced-packaging pull (Citi / Laura Chen, 2026-07-06).
- **Chris Wood (Jefferies) flags TSMC capex as the AI-cycle proof point (2026-06-25).** In his GREED & fear newsletter, Chris Wood cites TSMC's estimate to increase capex to **US$56bn in 2026 (vs US$41bn in 2025)** and estimates of **US$65–70bn in 2027**, alongside Taiwan GDP +14.55% YoY in 1Q26 and export orders +53.4% YoY (3 months to May). He frames TSMC as part of the "picks and shovels" AI trade — the primary semiconductor-foundry read-through from the ongoing AI capex arms race. The macroeconomic data (Taiwan GDP fastest quarterly growth in 48 years) is a direct validation of the TSMC demand pipeline. Bull read-through (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html)
- **Jefferies sales (Cristina Titu) — into the 2Q26 print (07-16), citing Fubon's Sherman Shang (2026-07-09):** expects TSMC to beat again; **GM expanding to ~67.1% (from 66.2%)**; **3Q26 revenue guide of +10-12% QoQ vs Street HSD%**; **Arizona Phase-1 GM already ~50% with yields approaching ~90%** (faster than investors expected); argues TSMC could lift its full-year revenue-growth outlook into the **30-35% YoY** range. Net-new Jefferies-desk color on top of the same-week Fubon "GM upside" preview already on this page (Jefferies / Cristina Titu, 2026-07-09). (bull)
- **JPM (Gokul Hariharan) — Asian Semis / May WSTS read (2026-07-09):** models TSMC capex **$58/$78/$84bn for 2026/27/28 (~$219bn cumulative)**; **N3 capacity scaling to 167/213/240k wfpm by end-2026/27/28** and **N2 to ~170k wfpm by end-2028**; N3 UTR running **110-120%** with an N3 supply-demand gap **~600k wafers**; expects TSMC **2026 revenue +mid-to-high-30% YoY (USD)** and datacenter-AI revenue **CAGR 69% over 2024-29**; TSMC raised advanced-node prices **+6-10% in early 2026** with another round expected in **2027 (+8-10% for N3/N2, mild for N5/N7/CoWoS)** (JPM / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-09). (bull)
- **Susquehanna (SIG / Mehdi Hosseini) — COUPE is FAU-gated; near-term leverage sits in the FAU layer (bull, 2026-07-11):** COUPE (TSMC's co-packaged optics, on 3D/hybrid-bonding) capacity stays **FAU-constrained through YE27** and its high-volume ramp is a **2028 story** — so adopting COUPE locks customers into TSMC PICs, but the binding near-term bottleneck (and thus the supply-chain leverage) is the **FAU layer**, where GLW's glass-packaging stack (co-developed with GlobalFoundries) offers customers an alternative to TSMC's CoWoS-L/COUPE platform (see [[GLW]]). SIG frames **CoWoS 130K→195K wpm (YE26→YE28)** and advanced-packaging **$/wafer $5→$8** (2021-24→2025-28) as the structural backdrop (Susquehanna / Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-11).
- **@dnystedt read-through corroborates the CoWoS ramp (bull, 2026-07-10):** a media rumor puts **TSMC's 2027 CoWoS capacity at ≥200,000 wpm** (called a conservative estimate) as AI-chip packaging stays in severe shortage — consistent with SIG's ~195K-by-YE28 trajectory and pointing to ≥200K as soon as 2027 — with nearly all plant-construction projects on 24-hour shifts (partly competition from Intel); a further rumor says TSMC has **not decided order allocation**, which some read as holding back to force **CoWoS-equipment price cuts** (@dnystedt, 2026-07-10; the ≥200K rumor is also logged in the intra-quarter full log).

Foundry read-through for TSMC: **AMD has obtained incremental N4 wafers at TSMC** freed up by MediaTek's smartphone-build cuts (supporting AMD server-CPU unit growth +15–20% this year), and **NVIDIA has raised Vera CPU capacity by 1.2–1.5M units to 2.5–3.0M** to service agentic-AI demand; AMD/INTC both target **>50% server-CPU unit growth in 2027**. Directly corroborates the UBS ~$44bn/2030 and JPM (07-12) server-CPU-TAM legs on this page, and adds a concrete Vera-CPU volume mark (2.5–3.0M, vs MS's 5.75mn 2027 / KeyBanc's this-year figure) (@firstadopter, 2026-07-14). (demanda, bull)
- **Competition at the margin — Samsung TPU I/O + Intel Nova Lake in-housing (mixed/bear, 2026-07-15):** two competitive reads landed the day before the print. (1) **Samsung considering outsourcing back-end design of Google's 2nm TPU (codename "Icefish," 10th-gen) I/O die** — but the read is nuanced *for* TSMC: the **compute die stays on TSMC 1.4nm (A14)**; only the smaller **I/O die goes to Samsung 2nm**, and the article explicitly frames Samsung's 2nm wins (Google, Tesla, plus newly Anthropic/DeepX) as **overflow "TSMC cannot accommodate due to capacity constraints"** — i.e. a capacity-tightness tell more than a share loss (@jukan05, 2026-07-15). (2) **Intel pulling most of "Nova Lake" production back in-house** — on 18A yield improvement, Intel Foundry now expected to make **80–90% of Nova Lake tiles** vs the prior plan to outsource **60–70% to TSMC** — a modest bear at the margin for TSMC's Intel-outsourcing thesis (contrast the "Intel already booking ~10k WPM of N2 by end-2026 (Nova Lake outsourcing)" read on the page); Intel's 18A capacity is ~30kwpm across Fab 52 (AZ) + Hillsboro (@QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-15). (competicao, mixed/bear at the margin)
- **JPM server-CPU model — the agentic-AI server "super-cycle" a fresh foundry/OSAT leg for TSMC (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-12).** JPM's new proprietary server-CPU shipment model forecasts total server CPUs from **26mn units 2025 → 68mn 2028 (38% CAGR)** — AI-accelerator shipments ~50% CAGR FY25-28 lifting AI head-node CPU demand, plus a **155%-CAGR agentic-AI** server-CPU vector — with overall server shipments sustaining **20%+ growth**. JPM frames the super-cycle as **positive for TSMC** (foundry — AMD's continued x86 server-CPU share gains + AI-accelerator/ARM-CPU volume, e.g. Nvidia Vera / Google Axion) **and ASE** as the key foundry/OSAT vendors, alongside memory (SK Hynix / Samsung). TSMC OW, NT$2,415. Corroborates the server-CPU-TAM leg already on the page (UBS's ~$44bn TSMC server-CPU opportunity by 2030) and the CPU-as-a-material-2.5D-consumer read (MS). Bull read-through (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, "server CPU shipment model", 2026-07-12). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html)
- **🆕 Structural (dated 2026-05-21, folded 07-24) — the EDA lens on TSMC's moat: customer, gatekeeper, and 17% of Big-3 EDA revenue from <2% of world GDP (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** A different and unusually clean measure of the ecosystem lock-in this page normally frames through OIP and yield. TSMC is **both a customer and the gatekeeper**: it **specifies "Calibre-clean" DRC/LVS and Synopsys PrimeTime timing signoff as tape-out requirements at 7nm/5nm/3nm** — "customers literally cannot tape out without Synopsys signoff" — **co-develops PDKs with EDA vendors ~24 months before production**, and thereby "effectively mandat[es] specific signoff software for the entire ecosystem." The quantified ecosystem effect: **Taiwan is 17% of Big-3 EDA revenue from under 2% of global GDP, "entirely a TSMC ecosystem effect"** (the US-Taiwan-Korea triad is 65-70% of Big-3 revenue and "no alternative geography offers comparable semiconductor design density"). Two corroborating datapoints for the OIP moat already on the page: **TSMC's Silicon IP library grew 31x from ~3,000 items (2010) to 93,000 (2025)**, with y/y additions accelerating to 33-45% through the 2022-23 AI-accelerator wave — "every additional 10,000 items raises the cost of porting a flagship design to Samsung Foundry or Intel Foundry, where the equivalent ecosystems are an order of magnitude smaller"; and **Fab 18 alone went from 4 customers / 8 tape-outs (2020) to 45 customers / 262 tape-outs (2024)** — 11x and 33x in four years — likely >$10B of cumulative EDA-related spend, which SemiAnalysis argues makes tape-out volume at the largest leading-edge sites "a better leading indicator for Big-3 revenue than per-engineer seat counts." Also: **TSMC never developed competitive internal EDA** (unlike Intel and Samsung, which both tried in the 1990s-2000s and abandoned it), investing in OIP partnerships instead; **Cadence expanded its TSMC collaboration for N2 and A16 flows**; and the multi-foundry dynamic across TSMC/Samsung/Intel/Rapidus is framed as a structural EDA tailwind. **Worth logging verbatim as an outside view of this page's own risk profile — SemiAnalysis's closing comparison:** *"EDA is the most defensible business model in semiconductors. **ASML faces High-NA adoption risk, and TSMC faces geopolitical concentration.**"* That is the same Taiwan-concentration point the Barron's "80% of long-term assets still in Taiwan" line makes, from an independent source and stated as the *defining* structural risk on the name. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **🆕 Structural (dated 2026-06-14, folded 07-24) — N6 as the measured process reference, and what SMIC's DUV-only path does and does not close (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** TSMC N6 (via a MediaTek Helio G99) is the physical baseline in SemiAnalysis's first STEEL teardown, and the finding is the right frame for the China-competition line on this page: **SMIC N+3 "reaches TSMC N6-class logic density, but it requires far more aggressive DUV multi-patterning, so it does not match N6 on process maturity or cost."** Measured detail on N6 worth carrying: **fin pitch 34nm** in the cross-section (vs the 33nm generally listed for N7's HD library) — N6 "did not shrink pitches directly; its density gains came from DTCO instead"; **HD cell height 240nm**, 2 fins/transistor, single-diffusion-break, **no COAG**, and M0/M2/M3 at a relaxed ~40nm consistent with SADP-class double patterning — i.e. **N6 gets to 107.7 MTr/mm² without needing quadruple patterning anywhere**, which is precisely the cost/maturity gap versus SMIC's SAQP-at-M0 route. TSMC also used the **reduced p-to-n isolation-spacing booster on the N3 family**. On the roadmap: a theoretical SMIC **N+4 would only reach TSMC-N5-class density (137.8 MTr/mm²)** and a theoretical N+5 with backside contacts still would not be "cost-competitive with the leading edge." **Historical customer note the page should carry:** **before export controls Huawei was TSMC's largest customer — the only customer on TSMC's first EUV node N7+, and among the first on N5 alongside Apple — which ended in late 2020.** Net: the teardown is, read from TSMC's side, a *measurement-backed* statement that the N6-and-below cost/maturity moat is intact even where a DUV-only competitor matches density on paper. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **🆕🔴🔴 Structural (2026-09-22) — the visibility claim is now mechanistic, and TSMC has started underwriting its own capacity plan against the ELECTRICITY GRID (UBS · Gaudois / Abrams / Lin / Arcuri et al, "Global I/O: Tech Hardware & Semis — 3Q26 UBS APAC Tech Tour", 27pp, off 40 APAC company meetings; TSM Buy, PT NT$3,650 — reiterated, unchanged since 07-27).** Two sentences carry this. **① *"Given the 3-5 year engagement lead time for advanced nodes with foundry especially for AI/HPC, TSMC ALREADY HAS GOOD VISIBILITY AND SEES THE STRUCTURAL AI/HPC OPPORTUNITY REMAIN ROBUST BY '29-'30. While there may be uncertainty of short term slowdown, the 5 year trend to '30 is resilient."*** ➤ **The mechanism is what makes this different from a guide: with a 3-5 year engagement lead time TSMC is not forecasting 2030 demand, it is already booking the design engagements that create it. That is the cleanest structural rebuttal on this page to the short-term-AI-slowdown thread.** **② *"TSMC has also been taking additional efforts in assessing the industry readiness for new datacenter construction, for example FROM '26, IT HAS INTERNAL ANALYSIS TO TRACK THE POWER SUPPLY BUILD-UP ESPECIALLY IN THE US."*** ➤ **Net-new behaviour and worth flagging on its own: the foundry now runs an internal tracker on US grid build-out, i.e. management's own downside scenario for the 2027-30 capacity path is a POWER scenario, not a demand scenario. Nothing previously on this page had TSMC looking past wafers/CoWoS/substrates to electricity.** ⚠️ **No GW, MW or dated target is attached — UBS reports the behaviour, not the output. Attach no GW number to it, and keep it clear of facility-GW / IT-load-GW / 800V-subset-GW bases used elsewhere on this wiki.** ⚠️ **Same note: UBS is Overweight Leading Edge Foundry and TSMC is on its APAC Tech Most Preferred list ('27E P/E 16.6x, '28E 12.5x, '27E P/BV 6.13x at NT$2,480, priced 21-Sep-26).** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_Global_IO_Tech_Hardware_Semis_3Q26_UBSAPACTe.html)
- **🆕🔴 Structural (2026-09-23) — TSMC is the largest single bucket in the first bottoms-up hybrid-bonding tool model this wiki has seen, and the measurement that model is built on was taken on TSMC's own floor (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Nabeel Aziz, "Bondernomics", 57pp).** ⚠️ **Subject company is BESI (Buy, PT €250 CUT from €350); [[TSM]] is NOT RATED at Redburn — no TSMC rating, PT or estimate. Read-through only.** **TSMC = a 448-bonder market opportunity by 2030, of which BESI 401**, out of a **721-unit logic opportunity (BESI 652)** versus **c150 bonders installed in the field today** — next largest buckets [[INTC]] 118/108, [[AAPL]] 111/99, [[AMD]] 111/101. **The foundation of the note is that AMD's HB fleet is installed AT TSMC in SoIC and the implied real-world throughput from AMD's 2025 output is 75 uph against a 1,500 uph spec, −95%.** Redburn rejects "TSMC invested ahead of the ramp" as the explanation *"given its further investments in its **AP7 fab in 2026 offer MORE THAN DOUBLE THE SoIC CAPACITY of the existing AP6 fab**"*, and states that **AP7 is underwritten by the ramp of AMD Helios racks in 2H26**. ⚠️⚠️ **CONTRADICTION WITH WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES, NAMED NOT SMOOTHED: Fubon has AP7 *phases two and three* SoIC expansion being SLOWED to prioritise CoWoS (Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03) and cut its own SoIC capacity from 90k to 70k in 4Q28 (2026-07-28). Redburn uses AP7's SoIC build as evidence TSMC is not over-built; Fubon says specific AP7 SoIC phases are deferred. Both stay on the page. They reconcile only if "double AP6" is DESIGNED capacity and Fubon's is a phase-TIMING deferral — neither source says so, so that reconciliation is not adopted.** ✅ **The forward drivers Redburn names are all TSMC-positive and all cross-filed: SoIC investment made *"with line of sight on Nvidia GPU adoption as a new high-volume application"* (BESI moved [[NVDA]] Feynman from its high-case to its mid-case at the June 2026 investor day; ⚠️ HB on Feynman is NOT confirmed — his own table has Nvidia GPU as microbump today); **[[AVGO]] has adopted 3DIC WITH TSMC for its custom ASIC customers**, and Broadcom's 3.5D XDSiP uses HB; and CPO, where **the PIC-to-EIC bond requires direct Cu-Cu hybrid bonding to reach 200Gbit/s per lane** (optical-engine ASP $450, CPO TAM $15bn skewed $13bn to scale-up, 20m optical engines by 2030) — which would make COUPE a hybrid-bonded product and pull the optical engine onto the same SoIC line.** **On packaging format: *"[[AAPL]]'s A20 Pro in the iPhone 18 Pro is expected to have moved from TSMC's integrated fan-out package-on-package (InFO PoP) to a WAFER-LEVEL MULTI-CHIP MODULE (WMCM) approach"*, which Redburn calls "a gateway to HB" — he models Apple inserting HB into the A-series for the first time in **2029**.** ⚠️ **BASIS: bonder UNITS, 2030 installed base. Not wpm, not kwpm, not SoIC lines, and not comparable to the CoWoS capacity figures on this page. The only other bonder mark here — "each 10,000-unit SOIC line needs 15-20 hybrid bonders" (Redburn · Conor O'Mara, 2026-06-23) — is a per-line intensity, a third basis again.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bondernomics.html)
- **🆕 CoPoS, as UBS actually specifies it — the roadmap detail behind the 07-26 stress test, from the full note (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", 2026-07-27).** The share arithmetic is already on this page; what the full note adds is the *execution calendar and the escape hatches*, both of which matter more to the 2028-30 numbers than the headline share split. **Baseline and trajectory:** TSMC holds **80-85% of advanced packaging in 2026-27E** (the table runs 84% / 82% / 72% / 68% / **67%** for 2026-30E in the base case, OSATs 16%→20%, Intel 0/0/9%/11%/13%) — so the stress test is not a step-down from 100%, it is a step-down from a mid-80s base with OSATs, not Intel, taking the larger absolute slice. **Timing, dated:** TSMC **began installing its CoPoS pilot line in late Q2 2026** for technology evaluation and qualification, and UBS expects TSMC to **finalize the first-generation process flow, equipment specifications and key suppliers by mid-2027** — that mid-2027 lock-in is the single most trackable CoPoS milestone on the page and it precedes the 2028 MP date by only ~18 months. First generation runs **310×310mm² panels**, migrating to **510×515mm² or larger** as the technology matures; **Nvidia's Feynman is UBS's expected first CoPoS client in H2'28-2029, followed by AMD and ASICs in 2029-30.** **Two upside options UBS names that the page did not carry:** (1) **"TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration"** — i.e. the EMIB-T defection at v9 is not modelled as permanent, and CPO is the mechanism that pulls it back, which ties the packaging debate directly to the COUPE thread already on this page; (2) the **14x-reticle CoWoS** already on TSMC's roadmap supports **~10 large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks**, is slated for production in **2028**, with potential to scale beyond 14x in 2029 — so the "EMIB-T scales better to ultra-large packages" advantage is bounded by a CoWoS roadmap that reaches the same territory. ⚠️ **Attribution note, so this is not misread downstream: the "~10 large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks" spec belongs to 14x-reticle CoWoS, NOT to CoPoS.** UBS's wording is "TSMC has added 14x-reticle CoWoS to its roadmap, with support for around 10 large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks… slated for production in 2028." **And the risk UBS explicitly de-fuses: glass is not the gate.** "Both packaging technologies should be in mass production in 2028 **without requiring glass-core substrates**," and if TSMC adopts glass **panel carriers** UBS "would not expect this to constrain CoPoS production or weigh on yields"; glass-*core* substrates are "unlikely to reach mass production before 2029-30E, if not later." That is a direct counterweight to the 07-26 MS read on this page that TSMC's glass path (Innolux/Ibiden, MP 2H28-2029) trails Intel's — on UBS's framing the glass ordering is largely irrelevant to the 2028 CoPoS ramp. **Manufacturing model:** TSMC keeps chip-on-panel (CoP) in-house and hands chip-on-substrate (oS) to OSAT partners — **ASE the primary Taiwan partner, Amkor the most likely on-substrate partner in Arizona** (consistent with the OSAT-overflow framing already on the page). Named CoPoS supply chain: depo/RDL Applied Materials/SCREEN/[[TOKYOELEC]]/Canon · wet GPTC/Scientech · debonding SÜSS/[[TOKYOELEC]]/EVG · CMP AMAT/Disco/Ebara/LAM/[[TOKYOELEC]]/GPM · die attach K&S/ASMPT · metrology KLA/Chroma/Camtek · substrate Ibiden/Unimicron/Toppan/Shinko/Nanya PCB/Kinsus. **Substrate detail, which corroborates the ~70%/~20% Ibiden/Unimicron split already in this page's "At a glance": UBS puts Ibiden's current ABF-substrate share in NVIDIA Blackwell / Blackwell Ultra GPUs at 70-80%**, expects it to hold position on CoPoS "as reliability and manufacturing track record are likely to outweigh cost considerations, given ABF package costs represent only a small portion of the overall bill of materials for GPU/HBM," and names **Unimicron a key CoPoS beneficiary** with **Nan Ya PCB also expanding capacity with a CoPoS focus**; UBS's substrate top picks are **Ibiden (Buy ¥20,500) and Unimicron (Buy NT$1,200)**. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)
- **🆕 Historical context (dated 2026-03-03, folded 07-27 — March-2026 vintage, does NOT supersede any newer TSM number on this page): the N2 price/mix read from the largest N2 customer (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman, "Apple Deep Dive: Risks and Opportunities in a year of transition", 2026-03-03).** Bernstein's iPhone-18 BOM work models Apple's **A20 as Apple's first processor on TSMC's 2nm (N2) process**, and puts a number on the node step-up from the buyer's side: **"the wafer itself is ~50% higher from N3 to N2."** The unresolved part is the offset — Apple has historically followed a ~three-year cadence on major architectural node transitions where "improved transistor density and die shrink typically help offset higher wafer costs," but Bernstein says it "remains unclear whether Apple will shrink the die in iPhone 18 to preserve cost structure or instead leverage the density gains to maintain or even expand die size" for on-device Apple Intelligence workloads. On top of the node move, Bernstein models **a shift to a more advanced packaging architecture** (Apple moving from InFO to WMCM, integrating CPU/GPU/Neural Engine and RAM on the same wafer) — and, given both the packaging shift and the N2 cost uncertainty, assumes a **40% increase in iPhone application-processor cost** in its BOM build. Read for TSMC: an independent, customer-side confirmation of leading-edge price/mix uplift on the highest-volume N2 program, plus incremental packaging content on the same unit. Vintage caveat kept explicitly — this is March-2026 work and predates the 2Q26 print, the N2-at-3%-of-wafer-revenue disclosure and the 2027 wafer-hike flow already on this page; it is context for the *direction* of N2 pricing, not a live estimate. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_252051.html)

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴 SEMICON TAIWAN 2026 IS RUNNING NOW (opened 2026-08-31) — largest on record, with the focus *"expanding to encompass the data center ecosystem led by cloud service providers"* and **[[GOOG]]'s AI Chief Technologist making his Asia keynote debut**.** TSMC presented at both the **IC Forum** and the **Silicon Photonics Summit** on day one. **Watch the remaining sessions for: any update to the A13/A12/N2U cadence, the HBM base-die node path (N12 for HBM4 → N3 for HBM5), the >14x-reticle CoWoS timeline, and COUPE customer adoption.** (BofA · Haas Liu 2026-08-31 · UBS TMI 2026-08-31)
- **🆕🔴 THE HBM BASE DIE IS NOW CONTESTED, AND TSMC HAS JUST PUBLICLY CLAIMED IT.** TSMC at SEMICON: HBM-scaling silicon *"from N12 for HBM4 to N3 for HBM5."* Same week: *"**SK hynix Reportedly Weighs Intel for HBM4E Base Dies amid TSMC COST PRESSURE and Supply Diversification**"* (UBS TMI, 2026-08-31). ➜ **Watch for confirmation from either side. A base-die second-source at [[INTC]] would be a share loss in a line TSMC has just named as a growth vector, and it would be Intel's second major external packaging/logic win after [[MEDIATEK]]'s TPU. Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[INTC]].**
- **🆕 THE I/O GAP AS A DATED PHYSICS CONSTRAINT: compute scaling **3x every two years** vs I/O scaling **1.4x every two years**, per TSMC.** ➜ **This is the cleanest quantitative reason optical I/O becomes compulsory rather than optional, and it is the constraint behind every CPO/NPO timing debate on this wiki. Corroborated by Digitimes the same day — *"TSMC sees SiPh going mainstream in 2027."* **Watch: COUPE grating-coupler insertion loss improvements, per-lane 200→400Gbps progress, WDM wavelength count (1 → 16+), and wafer-level test adoption — TSMC names the last as *"critical to reduce the wasted packaging cost."***
- **🆕 STIFEL'S THREE NAMED CATALYSTS, kept because each is a testable checkpoint and one of them is phrased as a PRICING test this page did not previously have (Stifel · Sahej Singh, initiation, 2026-09-01).** ① **N2 ramp execution and the 2026 guide** — *"revenue guided +30%+ in USD for 2026; quarterly evidence that N2 yields and demand are tracking would extend the raise-and-beat pattern."* ② 🔴 **CoWoS capacity additions of ~115-140K wpm exiting 2026 being ABSORBED WITHOUT ASP PRESSURE** — *"would confirm demand is still setting the pace for next-gen HPC compute."* ➤ **The capacity range is already on this page from Fubon/UBS/JPM; what is new is the test applied to it — the bull case is not that capacity lands, it is that it lands with pricing intact. A CoWoS ASP concession alongside the adds would be the first hard evidence the balance point has actually arrived.** ③ **Milestones on the $265bn Arizona / GIGAFAB programme that *"visibly diversify leading-edge supply"* de-risking the Taiwan-strait discount *"without diluting the model beyond the guided 2-3pp."***
- **🆕⚠️ TRUMP-XI, 2026-09-24 — UBS puts SPE and MEMORY as the most exposed verticals, with the ASML/Netherlands MATCH Act immersion-DUV headlines and new BIS measures on Chinese compute access via Thailand/Singapore as the live items.** *"Both sides look to enter negotiations from a position of strength… we would not be surprised to see additional headlines around restrictions to be floated in the coming weeks (or, on the other side, some easing of existing measures on Chinese co's)."* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-31)
- **🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-18 — POWER AND ELECTRICITY IS THE MONITOR TSMC ITSELF NAMES, AND IT IS THE ONLY DISCLOSED CAPEX-CUT TRIGGER.** *"Looking out through the end of the decade, the key thing we focus on is power and electricity… what if the power [and] electricity is only here [and] the chip demand is here — then why bother?… if you don't consider it, you're going to build too much and be left holding the bag."* **TSMC has been sending teams to the US to check data-center buildout and power progress, and "so far, the report back is actually quite impressive — that the CSPs have been well prepared."** **Watch: US interconnect queues and CSP power procurement, because that is the input TSMC has told you it would act on.** *(TSMC IR · Jeff Su, Itáu BBA Global Connect call, 2026-08-18)*
- **🆕🔴 2026-08-18 — THE 2026-28 CAPEX FLOOR: "significantly higher" than the $101bn of 2023-25, with 2026 at $62-64bn.** ⚠️ **The call renders the 2026 figure both as "$62-64bn" and as "$62bn at the midpoint" — internally inconsistent; verify against the primary before modelling, and do NOT back-solve a per-year 2027/28 number from "significantly higher."** Also stated: **">$20bn for one single new fab"** (basis unspecified). *(TSMC IR · Jeff Su, Itáu BBA Global Connect call, 2026-08-18)* ✅ **2026-09-10 update: the ASR ambiguity is resolved — TSMC IR restated the 2026 guide as *"now 60 to 64"* and the 2023-25 base as *"101 billion"*, so the "$62-64bn" rendering was the transcription error and the official US$60-64bn stands; the durability line added: 2026-28 *"significantly higher… very hard for our capex to go down in any meaningful way unless something cataclysmic happens"*, and the CFO's *"$20 billion… today is one fab"* matches the 08-18 ">$20bn" (TSMC IR @ investor group Q&A, 2026-09-10; Huang (CFO) @ GS Communacopia fireside, 2026-09-09 — Capstone Notion transcripts).**
- **🆕🔴 2026-08-18 — THE TIGHTNESS HAS MOVED FROM COWOS TO THE FRONT END, which changes the relief timeline.** *"More of the tightness has been concentrated on… advanced packaging or CoWoS… now, today, more of the tightness is… on the front-end wafer side."* **Front-end relief takes 2-3 years for a new shell versus OSAT partners who can absorb packaging — so the constraint is now in the slower-to-fix half of the flow.** The offsetting levers are **5nm→3nm tool conversion (plus "parts of other nodes"), which TSMC calls "something my competitors cannot do", and fab-productivity wafer-output gains.** **Watch: any disclosure quantifying converted capacity, and whether the CoWoS queue re-tightens as front-end output rises ("it's going to continue to be chicken and egg").** *(TSMC IR · Jeff Su, Itáu BBA Global Connect call, 2026-08-18)*
- **🆕⚠️ 2026-08-18 — ASKED AND NOT ANSWERED, so they stay open: CoWoS lead time / queue length; any capacity growth number ("I don't have a capacity number to give you"); and whether memory supply gates the buildout ("customers are working on it"). Also: TSMC will NOT sign long-term contracts or SCAs at any point in the cycle, so there is no contracted-backlog disclosure coming — prepayments are the only risk-sharing mechanism acknowledged, and the others are "between ourselves and our customers."** *(TSMC IR · Jeff Su, Itáu BBA Global Connect call, 2026-08-18)*

Net: headline beat + capex ahead of the bar, but the 3Q margin guide undershot → a margin-debate reaction (Taiwan futures softer post-print; JPMorgan market intelligence, 2026-07-16). The pre-print bogeys/consensus that follow are retained as the scoring baseline:
  - **Consensus bar into the 07-16 print (media roundup, @dnystedt, 2026-07-15):** Q2 revenue **NT$1.27tn** (already known via June monthly); **net profit +59% YoY to NT$632.6bn (~US$19.6bn), 10th straight quarter of growth**; **GM could exceed 68%** (vs the 65.5–67.5% guide); operating margin at a new high; R&D expense at a record high (advanced-process investment). **Q3 guide seen +9–12% QoQ in USD** (some to +15%); **full-year likely raised to mid-30s% YoY** (from "above 30%"; most-bullish case ~40%). Guidance FX assumption NT$31.7/US$. Watch: whether TSMC lifts the **2024–29 AI-semiconductor revenue CAGR to 70–80%** (from mid-to-high-50s now — an upward move driven partly by higher memory cost per system) (@dnystedt, 2026-07-15; @QQ_Timmy US-broker preview, 2026-07-14).
  - **Day-1 reaction thresholds — the bogeys (MS sales desk / Amir Amerian, 2026-07-13; "sales commentary, not research"):** expectations are **NOT low** into the print. (1) **2Q GM needs ≥69% for a positive day-1 reaction** (vs the 67.5% guide high-end). (2) **3Q revenue-growth guide midpoint likely needs ≥ low-teens % QoQ (if not +15%)** to react positively; less focus on the 3Q GM guide than on the 2Q GM *result* (investors already braced for N2 + Arizona dilution commentary), though Amir thinks 3Q GM midpoint needs ≥ flat QoQ even on a ~69% 2Q base. (3) **The single most important metric = the full-year revenue-growth guide** — last raised from "around 30%" to "above 30%," with buy-side actually expecting **~40% YoY**; the debate is mid-30s vs high-30s, but **"going to at least high-30s is a must for the stock"** to react positively. (MS sales / Amir Amerian, 2026-07-13)

We'll tell you in October"*, with the 3-4pt N2 drag *"very real"* and *"continu[ing] next year"* (TSMC IR @ investor group Q&A, 2026-09-10 — Capstone Notion transcript). Morgan) @ JPM 'TSMC: 3Q26 Preview' webinar, 2026-10-01 - Capstone notes (Notion transcript); JPM model, not guidance):** 3Q at the guide high end; 4Q guide ~+11-12% q/q (*"demand is definitely higher"*, capacity-capped); FY26 USD *"low 40s"*; GM ~66-67% in both quarters (N2 dilution worse in 4Q, N3 above corporate average); no numerical long-term update expected before January. - 2027 capacity-balance commentary ("we'll announce when we enter '27," C.C. Wei); 1Q26 the likely point at which TSMC confirms any FY27 CoWoS upside above 130k (Fubon, 2025-10-28).
- N3 GM crossover to corporate average in 2H26; N2 dilution onset in 2H26.
- Jan-2027 annual price reset (N3 ~10%, N2 ~5–6% per JPM, 2026-05-27); AI-wafer hikes 10%+ in 2026 (Fubon, 2025-10-28).
- Arizona ramp (Fab 2 N3 in 2H27, possibly pulled into late-FY26); A14 volume 2028. **US commitment escalated at the 2Q26 print — an additional US$100bn announced (2026-07-16), total Arizona now US$265bn / "four or more" new 2nm-and-below + advanced-packaging fabs** — overshooting the pre-print "~US$200bn under US pressure" framing (StoneX, 2025-08-20). Schedule demand-dependent (C.C. Wei).
- EMIB vs CoWoS-L decision on Google TPU V9 (MediaTek "Puma Fish"), expected late-2026/early-2027 (Gokul, 2026-05-27); EMIB-T meaningful from 2H26 (Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Fubon pushes back on the EMIB-T threat: Intel's **EMIB yield is only ~60-70% vs TSMC CoWoS 95%+** and EMIB-T "is not yet ready to enter mass production," so the priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L (Fubon, 2026-06-05). **Update (Fubon, 2026-07-08):** Fubon's latest checks say **Google initially planned to use Intel EMIB-T for its TPU V9 in late-2027/2028** and asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to **95%+** before production — but Fubon sees that target as "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," so it thinks **Google ultimately relies on TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion** (a datapoint feeding Fubon's total-CoWoS 210-220kwpm end-2028 forecast) (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08).
- **🔴 FY27 CAPEX — and whether US$80-85bn is even enough (Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03).** Fubon now hears FY27 capex going **"to maybe between 80 to 85 billion" to support the 3nm and 2nm expansion** (superseding his own US$80bn from 07-17 and US$75bn from 07-08; JPM ~US$78bn, Redburn US$70-75bn on EUV availability), and adds the caveat that matters more than the number: **"I'm not sure that is enough to support all the customers demand… in 2027, TSMC's capacity supply in both 2 nanometer and 3 nanometer will be VERY, VERY TIGHT."** ⚠️ **Watch the January-2027 formal capex disclosure against this range, and read the tightness as the supply-side underwriting of the ≥10% 2027 wafer-hike thesis already on this page. [[AVGO]] is explicitly staying at TSMC for foundry, so no second source relieves it.**
- **🔴 TSMC'S OWN IN-HOUSE EMIB ALTERNATIVE — a new item, and it is the counter to any "EMIB-T is a permanent second source" thesis (Fubon, 2026-08-03).** Fubon's checks: **"TSMC is also working on their IN-HOUSE EMIB SOLUTION for different chip design. But currently, it is still at a very, very early stage. And there is no production timeline yet."** Alongside it, **TSMC's public commentary on Intel's EMIB has become "more open-minded,"** which Fubon reads as TSMC having **"gone through negotiation with both Google and MediaTek"** (his inference, not a disclosure). ⚠️ **Too early to affect the 2028 [[GOOG]] TPU decision, but it bounds the DURATION of Intel's packaging window — watch for any TSMC disclosure putting a node, fab or date on it.** See [[INTC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **🔴 SoIC — AP7 PHASES 2 AND 3 are the specific capacity being slowed, and the second cause is a CPU-demand PUSH-OUT (Fubon, 2026-08-03).** Verbatim: *"Yes, mainly in AP7 and to be more specific, it's in phase two and phase three… they were supposedly to add SoIC capacities there. But… TSMC is slowing down the SoIC expansion a little bit. Because it looks like the CPU demand is also being pushed out… they will prioritize the CoWoS production first."* ⚠️ **This supersedes the standing framing that "TSMC's new AP7 fab phases are being dedicated to SoIC" (Gokul, 05-27), and the CPU-push-out half sits against the agentic-CPU-resurgence thread on this page — track it as a timing question, and watch whether the Vera/Venice CoWoS pull holds while SoIC is deferred.** Read-through to BESI (hybrid bonders) on [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **CoWoS / CoPoS capacity:** Fubon raises 2027 year-end CoWoS to **180kwpm** (from 170k) and models 2028 reaching **220kwpm + 20-30kwpm CoPoS** (a top-priority TSMC project, though MP timing 2028/29 still uncertain); strong 2027 allocation to TPU (AVGO + MediaTek) and AWS — Fubon believes **AVGO's TPU V9 is not cancelled** and enters MP late 3Q27 (two-3D-stack chip, 2× N2P + 2× N3P compute dies) (Fubon, 2026-06-05). MS independently raised its **2027 CoWoS capacity assumption to 200kwpm by year-end (from 170k)**, implying ~60% Y/Y AI-XPU industry growth, and breaks down the preliminary 2027 customer allocation: **Nvidia CoWoS-L ~910k (+~40% Y/Y)** single-sourced for Blackwell/Rubin (with strong CoWoS-R bookings implying Vera CPU shipments nearly doubling — consistent with Joe Moore's +52% Y/Y NVDA data-center rev); **Google TPU** the #2 user via MediaTek 180k CoWoS-S (~3.6mn TPU v8t ZebraFish, above MS's 2.5mn) and Broadcom 365k CoWoS-S (~330k to v8i SunFish ≈ ~3.9mn units, plus v7 Ironwood / Tomahawk 5/6); **CPU now a material 2.5D consumer** on agentic-AI demand — Nvidia 3nm Vera CPU to **5.75mn units in 2027**, AMD 2nm Venice CPU to **6.75mn (vs ~1.25mn in 2026)**. MS reiterates OW TSMC (MS Asia AI Supply Chain, 2026-06-23).
- **Second-source noise:** Apple/other customers reportedly in talks with Intel for a second-source partnership; Fubon doesn't think Apple will use Intel "even in some legacy products" — reads it as Apple leverage for capacity allocation given Intel foundry "has not yet provided a comparable service vs. TSMC"; Qualcomm may shift some allocation to Samsung on TSMC tightness (Fubon, 2026-06-05).
- NVIDIA roadmap: Rubin stays on N3P in 2H26 (too late to redesign to N2), Feynman jumps to A16 in 2028; NVIDIA takes ~2/3 of CoWoS in 2025 and >70% in 2026 (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). COUPE first shipments (NVIDIA) — CPO inflection.
- **HBM4 base-die ramp (N12/N3):** TSMC's confirmed N12/N3 base-die process variants for HBM4 start fabricating on behalf of Nvidia/Broadcom/hyperscaler ASIC designers — a new logic-wafer revenue stream layered on top of (not instead of) the existing CoWoS packaging fee for the same HBM stack, since the base-die wafer round-trips back to TSMC for CoWoS after SK Hynix/Micron TSV+stacking. Watch for base-die volume ramp timing alongside HBM4 qualification (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11, p.25/41).
- **CoWoS demand pull from the memory/AI cycle:** HBM's mandatory-interposer requirement (1,000+ wire routing density, unachievable on PCB/substrate) means every incremental HBM-attached XPU unit is also an incremental CoWoS unit — reinforcing the CoWoS capacity-raise cadence already tracked above (Fubon 180k/220k 2027/28, MS 200kwpm 2027, Nomura 1,800-2,000kpcs 2027F) (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11, p.3).

## Risks
- Overseas-fab GM dilution (2–3pts early → 3–4pts later) as US/Japan/Germany footprint scales (Q1 FY26; former exec); overseas build cost ~10% (Japan) to ~60% (US) above Taiwan (Fubon, 2025-10-28). **Restated by the CFO as an ANNUAL drag with the mechanism — *"2 to 3 percentage points every year… widen to 3 to 4 percentage points every year in the later part of the five-year period… Fab 1 will improve in Arizona… But then Fab 2 will come in… drag down the profitability. And then Fab 2 improves, Fab 3 will come in"* (Huang (CFO) @ GS Communacopia fireside, 2026-09-09 — Capstone Notion transcript).**
- Geographic/geopolitical concentration in Taiwan — earthquake + cross-strait risk is the stated rationale for diversification (former exec, 2026-04-14). Quantified: **~80% of TSMC's long-term assets are still in Taiwan** despite the Arizona/Japan/Germany build-out — the explicit risk flag in Barron's "Fab Five" cover framing (Barron's, 2026-06-01).
- U.S.–China export controls (BIS entity list); TSMC self-limits to ~3 generations behind for restricted destinations (former exec). China AI (H20/B20/MI308) restriction ~1% revenue, negligible (Bernstein deck, May-2025).
- Monopoly/antitrust scrutiny constrains pricing power and the multiple despite sole-source position — ~2/3 leading-edge share invites US action (former exec; StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20).
- N2/A14 capex-per-wafer rising sharply (N2 ~US$28bn/50kwpm vs ~US$20bn N3, ~US$16bn N5; A14 higher still) — capital intensity risk if revenue growth fails to outpace capex (CFO Q4 FY25; Fubon, 2025-10-28; though mgmt expects revenue to keep outpacing capex, Q1 FY26).
- Input-cost shock from Middle East (specialty chemicals/gases) — unquantified (Q1 FY26). Materials (RDL interposer in-house, ABF substrate from Ibiden ~70%/Unimicron ~20%) are the binding CoWoS constraint as package sizes scale, not equipment (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20).
- Advanced-packaging value migration to OSATs/substrate (EMIB-T, CoPoS, FoCoS) (Gokul, 2026-05-27; Q425 Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **UMC-Intel rumor (skepticism, not a TSM-specific risk but competitive-landscape noise):** DIGITIMES reports rumors that United Microelectronics (UMC) may deepen cooperation with Intel (12nm to Intel 3), drawing skepticism given process gaps and cash needs (DIGITIMES, 2026-06-30).
- Cyclical consumer/smartphone exposure amid memory-driven component inflation (C.C. Wei, Q1 FY26); smartphone units near-flat (+1–2%) vs AI ~100% growth (StoneX, 2025-08-20).

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — 3Q REVENUE IS TRACKING **~4% AHEAD OF CONSENSUS** ON TAIWANESE PRESS MATH, AND THE COMPANY'S OWN VP OF ADVANCED PACKAGING PUTS **CoWoS CAPACITY GROWTH ABOVE AN 80% CAGR THROUGH 2027.** TSMC IS ALSO BUILDING A **"MINI-LOOP"** TO VALIDATE PACKAGING TOOLS BEFORE THEY ENTER PRODUCTION — AND HAS INSPECTED **AUO** PLANTS FOR CONVERSION.**

### 🔴 (A) THE 3Q REVENUE TRACK — PRESS ARITHMETIC, FLAGGED AS SUCH

(Rothschild & Co Redburn · **Timm Schulze-Melander** via *"TMT Daily"* · Rowan Adley, **2026-09-21** — ⚠️ **TMT specialist SALES, not research; TSM is N/R at Redburn.** Underlying is Taiwanese media.)
- **"Taiwanese media reports suggest **TSMC September revenue could be NT$520bn**, implying **3Q26 revenue of NT$1,502bn, 3% vs consensus NT$1,448bn.** In September, this would be **+1% MoM (vs NT$515bn in August) and +57% YoY. Normal seasonality is for −1% MoM.**"** ⚠️ **Redburn's own headline says *"tracking 4% ahead"* while the body computes 3% — a 1pp internal inconsistency in the note; the arithmetic (1,502 / 1,448 − 1 = +3.7%) supports "~4%", so USE THE COMPUTED FIGURE, NOT THE HEADLINE.** ➤ **The load-bearing part is not the beat, it is that a +1% MoM September against a −1% normal seasonal is a two-point positive deviation in the month that sets the quarter.**
- **TAPE: *"TSMC +1% is now just **2% off a new closing high**"*; MediaTek +6.4%, AUO +9.9%, SOX +2.8% on the session (Rothschild; Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-09-21).**

### 🔴🔴 (B) ADVANCED PACKAGING — A COMPANY-SOURCED CAGR, A NEW VALIDATION MODEL, AND A CAPACITY SPLIT

(Commercial Times, relayed by Jefferies Asia TMT sales · **Conor O'Mara**, 2026-09-21 — ⚠️ **sales commentary, not research**; plus UBS · **Timothy Arcuri** *"SemiBytes Weekly"*, 2026-09-21.)
- 🔴🔴🔴 **COMPANY SOURCE: *"TSMC **Vice President of Advanced Packaging Technology and Service Jun He** estimates that **CoWoS capacity will grow at a compound annual rate of MORE THAN 80% THROUGH 2027, with growth continuing through 2029.**"*** ➤ **A named executive putting a multi-year CAGR on the single most-tracked capacity number in AI semis. Cross-check against UBS's level-based figures below rather than treating them as alternatives.**
- **UBS's level-based split, and it is more granular than the 09-16 version this wiki carries: *"industry **CoWoS capacity now expected to reach 260kwpm exiting C2027 versus 160kwpm exiting C2026, including **180kwpm at TSMC, 60kwpm at ASE and 20kwpm at Amkor**"*; *"we understand **[[AMD]] and [[NVDA]] are well positioned to capture the incremental capacity upside**, supporting continued accelerator ramps into 2027."* ✅ **NET-NEW vs the 09-16 entry: the per-vendor split. 160 → 260kwpm is +62.5% in one year, consistent in direction with Jun He's >80% CAGR but NOT the same measurement — his is TSMC's own CoWoS, UBS's is INDUSTRY including ASE and Amkor. Do not quote them interchangeably.** ⚠️🆕 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-24 — UBS ITSELF MOVED THIS NUMBER ONE DAY LATER: industry CoWoS now *"270kwpm by end-'27, versus 160kwpm by end-'26"* (UBS · Gaudois/Abrams/Lin et al, *"Global I/O: 3Q26 APAC Tech Tour"*, 2026-09-22). The end-'26 base of 160kwpm is unchanged; the end-'27 level goes 260k → 270k. ⚠️ **UBS gives NO revised per-vendor split with the new total, so the 180 TSMC / 60 ASE / 20 Amkor breakdown quoted above is a STALE decomposition of a superseded number — do not re-use it.** The stated mechanism for the extra capacity is that *"TSMC is likely to bring in ANOTHER OSAT as an additional 'wafer-on-substrate' outsourcing partner alongside ASE."* Old value retained here and in the `## Changelog` per the thesis-drift rule.**
- 🔴 NET-NEW OPERATING MODEL — THE "MINI-LOOP": construction of Kaohsiung's Baipu Industrial Park began **2026-09-21** with TSMC expected to anchor the cluster. *"The company plans to set up a modular **'Mini-Loop' line to VALIDATE NEW EQUIPMENT AND PROCESS INTEGRATION BEFORE the equipment is introduced into CoWoS production**, aiming to **improve validation efficiency by 25% to 50%**."* The stated rationale is that *"many back-end equipment suppliers are smaller and have more limited R&D resources, making it increasingly difficult for them to conduct equipment and process validation independently as AI chips evolve rapidly."* ➤ **Read this as TSMC internalising a function its back-end vendors can no longer perform — it tightens TSMC's grip on the packaging supply chain and is a mild negative for the bargaining power of sub-scale OSAT tool vendors. It also means packaging ramp risk shifts toward TSMC's own validation throughput.**
- FORMAT ROADMAP: UBS has **TSMC targeting CoPoS 310x310mm process/equipment selection by mid-C2027, with mass production in C2028**, versus [[INTC]]'s EMIB-T (silicon-bridge yields *"targeted to cross over 50% in 1H:27"*, supporting *">2m substrates for [[MEDIATEK]] TPU v9 demand"*). ⚠️ **Divergent roadmaps on different dates, not a head-to-head race.** Separately TrendForce reports TSMC *"eyes AUO site for CoPoS"* and has **inspected two AUO plants for possible purchase and conversion into advanced-packaging facilities** — AUO **+9.9% / limit-up**, and INTC is courting the same company for CPO and MicroLED substrate packaging. ⚠️ **Press-sourced; neither party has confirmed.**
- MATURE NODE — TSMC'S EXIT IS RE-PRICING THE TAIL: *"mature node foundries are planning further price hikes given surging demand of PMIC, MCU, sensors, MOSFETs, and **TSM's move to phase out its mature node fabs**, with **UMC seeking a 'substantial' hike into next year, and Powerchip planning a 40% increase**"* (Commercial Times via Jefferies; also Redburn, which uses it to reiterate **BUY on [[TXN]]**). **Vanguard also seeing increased demand.** ➤ **A TSMC capital-allocation decision is now the proximate cause of an analog cost-inflation cycle — relevant to [[TXN]], [[ADI]], [[NXPI]], [[MCHP]], [[ON]], [[IFX]] and [[TSEM]].**
- **APPLE SILICON, CONFIRMED BY TEARDOWN: @SemiAnalysis_ published a STEEL Teardown Lab result — **[[AAPL]] iPhone 18 Pro Max carries A20 silicon on TSMC N2** (2026-09-20).**
