COUPE uses **TSMC-SoIC bonding** to integrate electronic ICs (EICs) with photonic ICs (PICs), while supporting both **grating couplers and edge couplers.**"* **THE DUAL BANDWIDTH-SCALING STRATEGY, dated and quantified: *"'**Fast and Narrow**' pushes per-channel speeds from **200 Gbps to more than 400 Gbps** while expanding channel counts from **16 to over 128**, lifting total bandwidth from **3.2 Tbps to more than 12.8 Tbps**. '**Slow and Wide**' expands WDM from a single wavelength to **4, 8, 16 and beyond**."* **THE I/O WALL, which is the demand driver stated as a ratio: *"AI compute grows ~3x every two years while I/O grows only ~1.4x, creating an 'I/O WALL'; copper is limited at high frequency over distance, so optical transmission is required to support datacenter scale-up/scale-out"* (@QQ_Timmy). **Two integration paths named: **CPO** (optical engine on the substrate) and **OOI** (optical interposer, engine adjacent to the XPU) — *"power efficiency can improve ~4-10x and latency fall ~10-20x."*** **MANUFACTURABILITY, which is the part the market underrates: *"TSMC has enabled **WAFER-LEVEL OPTICAL TESTING for edge-coupled devices, allowing DEFECTIVE DIES TO BE SCREENED OUT BEFORE COSTLY CPO ASSEMBLY**"*, and *"has introduced an **OPTICAL PDK** that enables electronic-photonic co-simulation… **The PDK supports [[CDNS]] and [[SNPS]] tools.**"* ➤ **Direct, named EDA read-through — filed to both.** **FORWARD: *"modulators, SOA and light sources may go directly on the BACK of COUPE"*; COUPE also usable as an **optical interposer up to ~3.3x reticle**, later combined with CoWoS, supporting CWDM/DWDM. **Compared with copper: *"more than 10 TIMES the power efficiency."*** |
| **09-01** | ⚠️ **TWO COUNTERWEIGHTS IN THE SAME WINDOW — A COWOS ORDER DELAY AND A PRICING REPORT THAT IS *NOT* FROM TSMC** | **supply / margem** | **mixed** | **① *"**TSMC delays CoWoS orders as CoPoS plans take shape**"* (Digitimes, via Barclays desk and UBS, 09-01). ⚠️ **No magnitude, no customer, no timeline in either relay. Logged because it is the first CoWoS *delay* headline on this page since the allocation debate began, and because it is consistent with (not contradictory to) the 14x-reticle CoWoS / CoPoS roadmap step in 2028 — a transition can cause near-term order slippage. Do not read it as demand.** **② *"TSMC **may raise prices across ALL process nodes by another 10% to 15%** amid strong demand for AI-related chips… The market also reportedly sees follow-on price hikes for **N2, N3 and N5 of 10% by end-2027**"* — ⚠️⚠️ **UBS flags the sourcing explicitly: *"media report, citing MARKET EXPECTATIONS (NOT from TSMC)."* Treat as market expectation, not guidance. Corroborating direction only: Samsung is separately reported able to hike 4nm/5nm 10-15%.** **③ Supporting capacity datapoint from a broker's own strategy work: *"TSMC's total **CoWoS capacity is projected to reach 180,000 WAFERS PER MONTH by the end of FY27**"* (Jefferies Taiwan strategy · Arthur, 09-01).** **④ Also logged: *"Taiwan chip equipment maker **Skytech opens advanced process lab with TSMC backing**"*; *"Japan's chip materials makers reportedly **deepen Taiwan ties** as AI reshapes supply chains"* — Ajinomoto and Daikin expanding, following AGC, JSR, Merck and Entegris (Digitimes / Nikkei, 09-01). **⑤ *"Taiwan's chip prowess is built on democracy and rule of law, president says"* (Reuters) — political, no numbers.** |
| **09-01** | **THE [[AAPL]] FOUNDRY READ, WHICH IS BENIGN BUT WORTH THE ROW** (Bernstein tracker, via TMT spec sales · Tyler Seidman) | **demanda** | **neutral** | *"For foundry/TSMC, the shipment of the **Pro variants remained strong but iPhone 17e weaker than 16e has made the N3P CYCLE SIMILAR TO N3E lately.** The team continues to believe that **if Apple or any mobile customers RELEASE leading-node capacity, the impact to TSMC should be NEGLIGIBLE given it's SUPPLY CONSTRAINED.**"* ➤ **That is the correct read and it is the whole answer to "what if iPhone units disappoint": in a supply-constrained leading edge, a released wafer start is immediately re-allocated to a bidder paying more. The mix question (N3P vs N3E) is real; the volume question is not, while the constraint holds.** |
| **08-31** | 🔴 **Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers — headlines flash: TSMC's SEMICON Taiwan presentation quantifies the I/O WALL and lays out the COUPE CPO roadmap** | produto / demanda | **bull** | 🔴 **THE FRAMING NUMBER, and it is the cleanest justification for optical I/O yet put on the record BY THE FOUNDRY ITSELF: TSMC said the industry is running into *"an increasingly severe I/O WALL"* — **compute performance increasing 3x every 2 years against conventional I/O bandwidth growing only ~1.4x every 2 years.*** **TSMC quantified CPO benefits at **4-10x energy efficiency and 10-20x lower latency vs copper.*** **COUPE (Compact Universal Photonic Engine) uses **SoIC 3D bonding** for photonic integration, with either **COUPE-GC (grating coupling)** or **COUPE-EC (edge coupling)** for optical coupling.** **THE BANDWIDTH-SCALING PATH, as TSMC laid it out: (1) rising per-channel transmission rates — **moving up from the current 200 Gbps** — while **increasing lane counts from 16 to 128+** to lift total bandwidth per module; and (2) additional **wavelength division multiplexing, planned to go from 1 wavelength to 4, 8, or 16+.*** ⚠️ **Read for TSMC specifically: COUPE routes CPO volume through SoIC, i.e. through the same 3D-stacking capacity that HBM base dies and advanced logic already compete for. This is a positive demand signal AND a claim on the constrained resource — cross-read against the SoIC capacity forecasts already logged on this page and in [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).** **Also in the same flash and relevant to TSMC's HBM position: **[[MU]] and [[SKHYNIX]] shifting from 12nm/10nm HBM4 logic to TSMC 3nm for HBM4E**, while [[SAMSUNG]] keeps HBM4E on its internal 4nm node — and **HBM4 base dies cost 3-4x more than core dies**, so the migrating part is the high-value one. ⚠️ **Offsetting: SK Hynix is reportedly weighing [[INTC]] Foundry for a portion of that same HBM4E base die volume — the first credible second source for the socket.** See [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MRVL_Purchase_Commits_Expansion_DRAM_Contract_Pricing_CXMT.html) |
| **08-31** | 🔴🔴 **TSMC at SEMICON Taiwan 2026 — April Li (Global Head of AI & HPC Business Development) keynote + K.C. Hsu (head of advanced packaging), relayed by Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, TechNews, Liberty Times, imec, Mirror Daily and the corpus (@aleabitoreddit, @ParadisLabs, @QQ_Timmy)** | demanda / produto / supply | **bull** | ★★ 🔴🔴 **THE SINGLE BEST QUANTIFICATION OF THE I/O WALL ANY SOURCE HAS GIVEN THIS WIKI, AND IT IS THE *CONSEQUENCE* NOT THE RATIO: *"**Data movement alone can account for up to 60 PERCENT OF SYSTEM ACTIVITY in typical workloads, leaving accelerators operating at BELOW 40 PERCENT UTILIZATION**."* ➜ **This page already carried the input (compute ~3x vs I/O ~1.4x every two years — restated at SEMICON as *"AI 算力約每兩年成長 3 倍，I/O 僅約 1.4 倍，形成「I/O 牆」"*). The 60%/40% figure is the OUTPUT: it says more than half of what an AI datacentre does today is moving bits, not computing on them. Every optical, CPO, SoIC and interconnect thesis on this wiki now has one number to point at, and it is TSMC's own.** |
🔴🔴 **THE COMPOUND CLAIM: *"TSMC expects **SoIC and CoWoS integration to boost overall system computing performance to 50 TIMES its 2024 level by 2029**"* (TechNews). **Roadmap dates attached: N2P-on-N3P 3D integration in 2026, then **A14-on-A14 in 2029**, with **interconnect pitch shrinking to 4.5 microns**.** **Demand frame: *"AI computing demand is growing **FIVEFOLD ANNUALLY**"*; AI packages *"might contain **more than 1 TRILLION transistors by 2030**, making multi-die architecture and heterogeneous integration essential."* Li's strategic framing, worth keeping verbatim: *"In this new era, **market leaders will not be the ones who simply make better models, but those who BUILD THE MOST INTEGRATED SYSTEMS**."* **Four named pressure points: logic scaling, interconnect efficiency, memory performance, power delivery and cooling.**
🔴 **AND TSMC RESTATES THE HBM BASE-DIE CLAIM: it *"is also working to improve high-bandwidth memory performance by **using advanced logic technology in high-bandwidth memory base dies**."* ⚠️⚠️ **Hold this directly against the same week's Korean-media reports that **SK hynix is evaluating [[INTC]] for next-generation HBM base dies** — and against the corpus sizing that puts Micron + SK hynix's 2027 base-die需求 at ~112mn dies ≈ **20k WPM of N3, nearly 10% of TSMC's TOTAL 2027 N3 capacity** (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 08-31). **TSMC is publicly claiming a franchise that its largest prospective customer may be leaving for CAPACITY reasons rather than capability reasons. That is the highest-consequence open item on this page and it is not resolved.** See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) and [[INTC]].**
🔴 **SILICON PHOTONICS — TWO DATES THAT LOOK CONTRADICTORY AND ARE NOT (K.C. Hsu): *"**silicon photonics could account for more than 50% of the optical transceiver market in 2027**"*, and *"**some producers will mass produce CPO in 2H26. TSMC is on track to enter production of CPO in 2H26**"* — about four months after TSMC's May CPO progress announcement. ⚠️ **Set against Jefferies' own conference feedback the SAME DAY that *"CPO timing is pushed more towards **2H28 at the earliest**"*. The reconciliation is in Hsu's own next sentence and it is the most useful line of the day: *"**Foundry can scale up optical engines, but the real bottleneck for LARGE-SCALE DEPLOYMENT lies in OTHER PARTS of the supply chain, including LASERS, OPTICAL FIBERS, FIBER CONNECTORS, and PRODUCT TESTING**"* (independently captured by @aleabitoreddit citing UDN, corpus, 08-31). **2H26 is TSMC's own production readiness; 2H28 is volume deployment. Not a contradiction — a two-year gap between the foundry being ready and the system being buildable, and the gap is owned by other people's components.** See [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).**
🔴 **COUPE, WITH REAL NUMBERS FOR THE FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE: *"its **2–4-micron bonding pitch limits transmission loss at 112 Gbps to just 0.06 dB, compared with 1.38 dB for micro-bumps**"* (a ~23x reduction), with *"power consumption and **latency to 10–20 nanoseconds**."* COUPE uses **SoIC bonding to integrate EICs with PICs**, supporting **both grating couplers and edge couplers**. **Two scaling paths: (i) per-channel 200 Gbps → **>400 Gbps** with channel count 16 → **>128**, total bandwidth **3.2 Tbps → >12.8 Tbps**; (ii) WDM from a single wavelength to **4, 8, 16 and beyond**.** **Compact universal photonic engine claimed at *"more than **10 times power efficiency** compared with traditional copper wire-based applications."*** 🔴 **Two ecosystem items that are read-throughs, not TSMC items: **wafer-level optical testing for edge-coupled devices, *"allowing defective dies to be SCREENED OUT BEFORE costly CPO assembly"*** (a yield/cost lever — see [[COHR]]/[[LITE]] and the VIAV read on the theme page), and an **optical PDK for electronic-photonic co-simulation that *"supports [[CDNS]] and [[SNPS]] tools"*** — the first EDA read-through from CPO on this wiki. **imec VP Philippe Absil, at the same forum: *"**Co-packaged optics is being adopted FASTER than originally expected.** I've seen market reports that were showing very moderate and gradual adoption, moving into 2030."*** **Market context: optical transceiver sales +25% in 2025 and +50% in 2026; the 100G-and-above market +60% in 2025.** |

| 07-24 | Chipstrat · Austin Lyons ("TSMC could spend $10B more on N2 capacity. Why won't they?") | capital | mixed | **The sharpest framing yet of the under-investment question the page has been circling — TSMC's capex intensity is running ~18 points below the last supercycle.** In the pandemic-era supercycle TSMC spent **53% of revenue on capex**; in the AI supercycle, on roughly **$100bn/yr of operating cash flow**, it is spending only **34-36%**. "Matching the 2021 playbook today would mean **another $30 billion a year**." Lyons poses the tension directly: management says AI demand is "unbounded," so either (a) 2021-23 scarred management into treating ~35% as the comfort level, or (b) it is a quiet statement about long-term demand — "given that fabs are a ten-year+ asset, **today's CapEx is TSMC's opinion on demand for the next decade**." Useful counterweight to the uniformly bullish 2027-pricing flow on this page: the company writing the biggest cheques in the industry is still spending well below its own precedent. **Node-mix detail, net-new:** N2 debuted at **3% of wafer revenue**, but every node has debuted at roughly **$1bn of revenue** — and since wafer prices rise each generation, flat debut dollars implies **shrinking debut volume**; more layers and longer cycle times mean the same capacity ships fewer wafers in the first 90 days. So "**N2's debut is the fewest, most expensive wafers TSMC has ever sold**." **N5 is still the single biggest node family at 33%** six years in — because it is the AI node (Blackwell on N4P, TPUs, Trainium, most custom XPUs were all designed in 2022-24 when N5 was the mature choice). **On the margin peak:** GM 67.7% was a **fifth straight record**, guided down to 66.0% on N2 dilution of **3-4pts — worse than the 2-3pts guided in April, because the ramp got steeper**. Gross *profit* keeps climbing: **~$27.2bn in Q2, ~$29.8bn implied by the Q3 guide, ~$120bn annualised**. Lyons thinks **67.7% may be the high-water mark until ~2028** (N2 dilutes ~8 quarters, A16 ramps behind it, Arizona Fab 2 lands 2H27) — but flags the falsifiable test: **if Q3 prints above 66.5%, N2 pricing is covering its own ramp**, which is the cleanest read on pricing the market will get. Contrasts this peak with 4Q22's 62.2% (an FX bump at a cyclical top that stood eleven quarters as demand collapsed): this one comes from loading a new node as fast as physically possible into spiking demand. **Management quote, net-new:** C.C. Wei on the call said **A14 will be "an even larger and long-lasting node for TSMC than N2, just like a 2-nanometer technology is a larger and longer-lasting node than 3-nanometer."** Also notes **three greenfield N3 fabs approved** (Taiwan, Arizona, Japan) for a four-year-old node as Rubin-class accelerators move to N3 through 2027, and that N3 is "very tight" with GM set to **cross the corporate average in 2H26** while its 2022 tools depreciate off in 2027. _(Paywalled after the margin section; the capex-gap quantification and pricing sections were not accessible.)_ (Chipstrat · Austin Lyons, 2026-07-24) |

**Quarter synthesis:** the debate has shifted from "does AI demand hold?" to "how much margin can TSMC extract" — the flow (Fubon, Redburn, UBS, JPM) is unanimously bullish on 2027 pricing (hikes ≥10% incl. Apple, GM toward 70%) and adds the server-CPU as a new leg. New from 06-26: TSMC's Shanghai Symposium revealed a CoWoS 5.5× reticle at 98% yield + 6T SRAM GAA area win, pointing to higher CoWoS throughput. New from 06-27: **SFG "Feeding the Foundry Beast"** (Hosseini, $432.35, Positive) frames SCA/LTA templates migrating from memory to foundry logic, compressing TSMC's revenue volatility and justifying multiple expansion. New from 06-28: **UBS raises PT to NT$3,400** (from NT$3,000) ahead of the July 16 Q2 print, lifting 2026 growth to 37%, 2027 to 38%, and capex to $60/$80/$95bn; N3 capacity road to 220kwpm and N2 to 160kwpm by 2028E; +5-10% leading-edge pricing in early 2027. Foundry 2.0 data (Counterpoint via Jefferies) confirms TSMC +41% YoY in Q1 2026 with CoWoS still constrained. OpenAI Jalapeno (3nm) + Jalapeno2 (A16+CoWoS) + QCOM Dragonfly/AI300 (3nm/2nm+CoWoS) add incremental demand vectors beyond the NVDA/AVGO/Google anchor. New from 06-29/30: **MS Charlie Chan raises PT to NT$2,888** (from NT$2,588, OW) into the July 16 call (expects capex guide to $56bn, rev to ~40% YoY); the foreign-IB PT table (Aletheia/UBS/Macquarie/CLSA NT$3,330-3,500) sits well above. The Jefferies buy-side GM survey (is 70% too high vs VA 66.9%) frames the key Q2 debate. **Packaging is now a two-front story:** DIGITIMES/@QQ_Timmy flag CoPoS fast-tracked to ~2029 MP (5-6x output), while MS Amir Amerian's glass/panels note + the former-AMD-director expert call bracket the CoWoS-vs-EMIB-T debate (expert: CoWoS still the "gold standard"; EMIB-T early/speculative but performance/cost-advantaged >9.7 reticle). Backfilled from earlier in the quarter: **Barron's "Fab Five" cover (06-01)** puts a hard number on the Taiwan-concentration bear case (~80% of long-term assets) alongside a cheap-growth valuation framing (21x FY27 P/E, 0.58x PEG, +90% EPS by 2027); **SemiAnalysis's HBM deep-dive (08-11)** clarifies TSMC's HBM economics as a **double monetization** — base-die fab (N12/N3, for NVDA/AVGO/hyperscalers) plus CoWoS packaging on the same physical unit after it round-trips through SK Hynix/Micron for TSV+stacking — reinforcing the CoWoS-demand-pull thesis from the memory/AI cycle without TSMC ever holding the TSV/stacking IP itself. New from 06-29/07-01: **UBS (Sunny Lin) raises its TSMC CoWoS capacity forecast to 130k/180k WPM end-2026/27 (from 120k/150k)** — a genuine upward revision on top of the already-bullish JPM (115k/175k/220k) and MS (200kwpm 2027) trajectories — with backend/AP now ~12-15% of sales '26/27E and ~50% CAGR over 5 years; MS separately published a 50-page CoWoS-update report breaking the ramp out by CPU/GPU/ASIC/Optical segment (2026-06-29), and DIGITIMES reports the whole supply chain is under a gag order while TSMC fast-tracks CoWoS and CoPoS (2026-06-30). **Packaging-moat debate deepens:** an AlphaSense expert call (former AMD director) calls CoWoS the clear leader vs Intel's EMIB-T with a 5-10yr-durable moat, while MS's Amir Amerian "glass/panels" sales note surfaces a new angle — TSMC's Glass Core Substrate discussions with Ibiden/Innolux (June 11, Japan) — read as proof-of-concept only, adoption ~2H28, full-year-earnings impact not until 2029. **Market-share/WFE reality check:** a Capstone expert call (former Samsung EVP) puts TSMC leading-edge share at 85-95% (Samsung a distant #2, 10-15% overflow ceiling) and calls the Street's $300bn WFE narrative "completely unrealistic" (sees growth past $200bn, not to the highs, on fab-capacity/power constraints) — a useful bottoms-up check against the sell-side's uniformly bullish capex trajectory elsewhere on this page. Jefferies (Cristina Titu, 07-01) notes shares +3% in Asia as investors position for a possible 70% GM print next quarter, and DIGITIMES separately flags UMC-Intel cooperation rumors (12nm→Intel 3) met with skepticism. **New from 07-02:** UBS's "Sector Keys July 2026" reiterates Buy/PT NT$3,400, restates the N2-to-220kwpm-by-2028 and server-CPU-to-$44bn theses, raises capex to $60/80/95bn and CoWoS to 180/70kwpm (TSMC/OSATs) end-2027, and pegs TSMC at 17x/13x 2027/28E PE vs SOX 34x (+62% YTD) — consolidating the bull case already on the page. Cutting the other way, SemiAnalysis's ECTC 2026 roundup is the cleanest single read on the packaging-competition front: TSMC's microfluidic-cooling demo (5.3kW@8LPM) is a multi-kW-package enabler, but TSMC ran only 3 ECTC papers (vs Intel 12 / Samsung 11) and Intel's EMIB-T is framed as "the most credible alternative to CoWoS" for large-package accelerators — plus a wave of interposer-less/panel-level alternatives (EMIB-T, FO-EB, glass-core, DBrM) all target the circular-wafer size/utilization limits of CoWoS-R/-L (though CoWoS stays ahead on DTC/eDTC + IVR + active LSI and volume). **New from 07-06 (into the July 16 print):** **JPM (Gokul) raises PT to NT$3,100 (from NT$2,500), Overweight**, lifting FY26/27/28E EPS +5%/+10%/+16% and, notably, the **Datacenter-AI revenue CAGR to 69% ('24-29E) from 59% — above TSMC's own mid-to-high-50s guide** (the sell-side now modelling DC-AI *ahead* of management), with 2Q26 GM close to 70% (JPMe 69.5%) and capex raised to $58/$78/$84bn '26/27/28 — a fresh upward mark on both the GM-toward-70% and rising-capex debates. **MS (Charlie Chan)** published a 58-page constructive preview + CoWoS update (CPU/GPU/ASIC/Optical/China AI Compute, Industry View Attractive) into the same print. **Citi (Laura Chen)** adds a Taiwan design-service read-through (prefers MediaTek/GUC for AI-ASIC growth into 2027; foundry/advanced-packaging/HBM/ABF supply "remains tight") supportive of TSMC's ASIC/CoWoS demand. **Bernstein (David Dai)** reiterated OP/US$430 (NT$2,780; 20x forward Q5-8 EPS NT$139) inside its ASML High-NA deep-dive, framing **TSMC as the slowest leading-edge logic player to adopt High-NA (~2030, A10)** — behind Intel (2028) and Samsung (~2029) and after DRAM (~2027) — reading TSMC's "HNA too expensive" stance as prudence (wait for availability/throughput to lower cost-per-exposure) rather than a rejection; a near-term litho-cost positive but a process-leadership tell that rivals adopt HNA first. Net: the desk is uniformly bullish into July 16 on GM (~70%), capex (rising) and DC-AI demand (now modelled above management's guide). **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note is a mixed read-through — Musk is grateful to TSMC but wants it to expand faster than TSMC is comfortable with ("we either build the terafab or we don't have the chips"), and UBS thinks Terafab could reach TSMC-scale WFE within 4-5yrs (a potential new >$50B/yr WFE spender). Near-term it corroborates the capacity-can't-keep-up-with-AI pricing tailwind, but it flags a credible new leading-foundry-scale entrant as a long-dated competitive risk on top of the (still dominant) share/moat picture elsewhere on this page. **07-08 (into the July 16 print):** Fubon's "Expecting GM upside going forward" preview is the cleanest bull-side setup mark — **Buy reiterated, PT NT$3,500 (top pick)**, 2Q26 on track to beat (GM ~67.1%), 3Q26 guide expected to beat (+10-12% QoQ vs cons HSD%), FY26 guide "very likely" raised again to +30-35% YoY, and FY27 GM pushed toward the 70% benchmark on a widening 15-20% S/D gap + 10%-for-all wafer hikes incl. Apple (FY26/27 EPS NT$103.90/144.13, market-high vs cons NT$99.52/127.84). Two new datapoints: **Arizona phase-1 GM has already reached ~50% at full UTR / 90% yield — faster than expected** (a direct rebuttal to the overseas-dilution bear point, corroborating the earlier 06-05 Fubon "AZ P1 ~50% after 2 yrs" read), and **Google's planned EMIB-T for TPU V9 (late-27/28) likely falls back to TSMC's 2028 CoWoS** as Intel struggles to hit the 95%+ yield Google requested by mid-2027 — feeding Fubon's total-CoWoS 210-220kwpm end-2028 forecast (a positive CoWoS read layered on top of the UBS/JPM/MS/Nomura capacity-raise consensus). **07-08/07-09 (final setup into July 16):** a **JPM (Gokul) 2Q26-preview expert call** puts 2Q26 GM at **~69.5-70% vs guide ~67.5%** (above buy-side, esp. overseas near mid-60s) but flags it as a **short-lived** catalyst, with 3Q26 GM slightly lower QoQ (N2 + overseas dilution, offset by N3 turning accretive in 2H26); the fresh marks are **2027 blended ASP +25-26% YoY** (broad ~10% N2/N3 hikes), **N3 ramping faster to 240-250kwpm by 2029-30 → 10-16% EPS upside for '27/'28**, **DC-AI toward ~70% of total by 2029**, FY26 capex stable $56-58B but **no concrete 2027 numeric capex guide likely (a possible disappointment for US investors)**, aggressive competitive pushback (90-95% N2 share), and **75-80% of new-merchant-CPU CoWoS (AMD Venice/NVDA Vera) shifting to OSATs (ASE/Amkor)** — an OSAT tailwind and an in-house-CoWoS-freeing read. **GS (Evelyn Yu, 07-09)** reiterates the "stronger growth trajectory" theme (TSMC accelerating the capacity build, MediaTek riding AI-ASIC momentum). Demand corroboration: **@QQ_Timmy's June GB NVL72 checks** (~8,000 racks in June: Foxconn ~3,300, Quanta ~2,300-2,400, Wistron ~1,200-1,300; brokers holding ~7-8k/mo) and a moat framing that TSMC's **EDA/IP ecosystem (OIP)** is the underrated pillar beyond PPA/EUV/yield. **DIGITIMES (07-09)** adds that AI demand is pushing TSMC + Samsung to raise foundry prices (with Rapidus aiming to undercut on 2nm) — corroborating the 2027 price-hike thesis. Net: the pre-print flow stays uniformly bullish on GM (~70%), pricing (broad 2027 hikes) and DC-AI mix, with the only cautionary notes being the "short-lived-catalyst" GM framing and the risk that TSMC declines to hand US investors a 2027 capex number on July 16. **The single dissenting rating arrives 07-09 from TD Cowen (Krish Sankar): Hold, PT $400 on the TSM-US ADR — ~8% BELOW the $436.98 spot and the only downside-implying rating on the page** — even though its capex assumptions ($56-60/$80/$100B for '26/'27/'28) are the most aggressive here and its EUV read (38 of 88 ASML tools in 2027, below its ~45-tool demand) reinforces the '27 EUV-ceiling debate; i.e. the caution is a valuation/multiple call on an ADR that has already run, not a demand or margin call. **07-09 (GS webinar detail):** Goldman's Evelyn Yu webinar fills in the specifics behind its "stronger growth trajectory" framing — **FY27 capex raised to $78bn (from $70bn)**, FY26 top-line **~40% YoY** (vs the >30% guide), **2Q26 GM ~68.5% toward 70%**, and the most aggressive CoWoS mark on the page: **pure CoWoS ~220kwpm by end-2027 (from ~130k end-2026), or ~280kwpm incl WMC**, raised +30k on the Nvidia Vera CPU — vs JPM/Gokul's ~175k (upside ~200k, mostly OSATs). The two brokers **converge on $78bn FY27 capex** but **diverge on CoWoS-27 (GS 220k vs JPM 175-200k)** — divergence kept, not reconciled. GS frames rush/hot-run/super-hot-run at **~10-20% of ≤5nm output at a 50-100% premium** and a **mid-single-digit 2027 hike with upside** ("similar to COVID-era" tightness) as the core GM levers. Filled-in file-1 detail (Gokul expert call): N3 utilization **~125-140% incl cross-fab back-end (N7/N12)**, ~600k/25-30% N3 gap, **no 2H26 baseline hike** (only hot/super-hot-run), mature 12" not shrinking, wafer-out ~170k→240-250k N-adv end-28→29-30. **07-11 (Susquehanna Technology Hardware Weekly, Mehdi Hosseini):** SIG adds a fresh advanced-packaging frame — CoWoS ~130K→195K wpm (YE26→YE28), SoIC ramping through 2028, adv-packaging $/wafer $5→$8 (2021-24→2025-28) — and the sharpest COUPE read on the page: **COUPE stays FAU-constrained through YE27** (PIC <0.5K→~5-10K wpm; FAU holds COUPE shipments <0.5M units/qtr, a Spectrum-X yield constraint), so the high-volume CPO ramp is a **2028** story and the near-term supply-chain leverage sits in the **FAU layer** — where GLW's glass/FAU stack (co-developed w/ GlobalFoundries) is the alternative to CoWoS-L/COUPE (see [[GLW]]). **07-14:** a Mirae Asset SK Hynix note flags **TSMC's record June rev NT$443bn (+68% YoY)** and a **2Q26 revenue-guide beat** as evidence the AI capex backdrop stays strong (combined big-tech/neocloud backlog $2.1tr, +24% QoQ; 2-yr-recognition $656bn, +38% QoQ → higher hyperscaler capex "all but inevitable") — a light read-through into the 07-16 print. This corroborates @dnystedt's 07-10 rumor of **≥200K CoWoS wpm by 2027** (conservative), while SIG's event calendar pins the ASML (Jul 15) and TSMC (Jul 16) prints. **Into the 07-16 print (07-13 → 07-15):** the primary June-revenue print (@jukan05/@QQ_Timmy) landed at **NT$442.68bn (+67.9% YoY), a record** and the first June MoM increase since 2021 — implying **Q2 ~US$40.2bn, ~US$0.1bn above the guidance high end** (H1 already above full-year 2022; run-rate ~US$167bn/+37% FY26). The **media consensus bar** (@dnystedt, 07-15) frames the call: **net profit +59% YoY to NT$632.6bn (US$19.6bn, 10th straight up quarter), GM possibly >68%, op-margin/R&D at new highs, Q3 guide +9–12% QoQ, FY likely raised to mid-30s% (bull ~40%)**, and the key upside watch is whether TSMC **lifts the 2024–29 AI-semi revenue CAGR to 70–80% (from mid-to-high-50s)**. The one live tension is **capex** — BofA/Josh Evans flags a "likely capex raise 7/16," but @dnystedt/JPM expect TSMC **not** to hand out a hard 2026/2027 number (may only frame the 3-year plan), the key read-through for [[ASML]]. Demand corroboration: **KeyBanc raised gen-purpose server-CPU unit growth to +30% (from +18.5%)**, with AMD taking incremental TSMC N4 wafers freed by MediaTek smartphone-build cuts and NVDA lifting Vera CPU capacity to 2.5–3.0M units — reinforcing the server-CPU leg. On competition, two 07-15 reads net out roughly neutral-to-positive: **Samsung is sounding out DSPs to outsource the back-end design of Google's 2nm TPU I/O die**, but the TPU compute die stays on **TSMC 1.4nm** and Samsung's 2nm wins are explicitly framed as **overflow "TSMC cannot accommodate"** (a capacity-tightness tell); and **Intel is pulling most of Nova Lake back in-house** (80–90% of tiles vs a prior 60–70%-to-TSMC plan) on 18A yield gains — a modest offset to the Intel-outsourcing thesis. Also (07-13): MS's CPO note lists TSM among the silicon-photonics foundries ($TSM/$GFS/$TSEM), corroborating COUPE positioning. **Bogeys / day-1 thresholds (07-13/07-14):** MS's sales desk (Amir Amerian) is explicit that **expectations are not low** — **2Q GM needs ≥69%** (vs the 67.5% guide high-end), **3Q rev-guide midpoint ≥ low-teens % QoQ (if not +15%)**, and — the swing metric — the **full-year rev-growth guide must go to "at least high-30s"** (buy-side already expects ~40%) for the stock to react positively; JPM (Gokul, 07-13) similarly expects **2Q sales at the guide high-end with GM likely to beat**, and MS (Charlie Chan, 07-14) reiterated **OW** alongside a 60-page CoWoS-update preview. Net: the setup is unambiguously bullish on the fundamentals, but the day-1 reaction hinges on clearing an already-elevated bar (≥69% 2Q GM + a high-30s FY guide). **POST-PRINT (07-16) — the window resolved:** a beat on revenue, FY-guide and capex, but a 3Q GM guide (65–67%) below the ~68% bar, so it reads as "beat-but-margin-debate." 2Q GM **67.7%** cleared the 67.5% guide yet **missed the ≥69% day-1 bogey**; the FY26 revenue guide was raised to **"slightly above 40%"** (cleared the "high-30s a must" swing metric); FY26 capex was reportedly lifted to **US$60–64bn** (BofA + a Bloomberg call-transcript reading — above the ~US$58bn bogey); N2 is already **3% of wafer revenue**. The margin debate (3Q dilution from N2 + overseas fabs) is now the near-term swing factor. See **Current state (Q2 FY26)** for the full scorecard and primary-source detail.

**🆕 Addendum 2026-09-01 — an OW analyst names the fade, and it is positioning rather than fundamentals.** MS Asia's Charlie Chan, asked for his latest TSMC view: *"we are **still very positive, very high quality**… into the prints, there are some previews, some expectations start go up. I think this time around probably some institution that price hike into next year. Yeah, but after that it's kind of **some kind of profit taking pressure**. So I mean, **TSMC is just too big**, right? So **we don't see meaningful near-term catalyst**… **Quarterly itself should be should be fine.** But in my view **people probably want to pay some more attention in other trends like ASIC or CPU**."* ➤ **This is the missing half of the pattern this page has logged three times (07-23 Nomura "shares react negatively despite guidance beat", the print-day fade, the 08-31 conference read): the 2027 price hike is being PRE-TRADED into the print, so it functions as an expectations event, not an estimate event — and an Overweight house is steering incremental attention to ASIC/CPU rather than to TSMC.** His two named medium-to-long-term catalysts are **(a) next-year price-hike upside** and **(b) *"Intel may [move] their server CPU to TSMC at 1.4 nanometer"*** — ⚠️ **the second is unsourced, unsized and undated in an ASR transcript, and belongs on this page as a Street expectation only.** Read alongside Bernstein's High-NA primer the same day, which reiterates **TSMC on Low-NA until ~2030/A10** and frames the choice as prudence plus better cost-per-exposure later, *"but at the risk of not benefiting from HNA immediately"* while [[INTC]] runs High-NA in HVM on 18A today. **Net: nothing in the 09-01 flow changes a TSMC number — it changes the reason to expect the stock to lag the numbers.** (Morgan Stanley Asia memory webcast · Shawn Kim / Charlie Chan, 2026-09-01; Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV Primer - The shrinking never stops", 2026-09-01)

## Intra-quarter — Q3 FY26 window (post-2Q26 print, opens 2026-07-16)
**🆕🔴 2026-10-01 (notion-ingest — Capstone notes, Notion transcript) — THE JPM 3Q26 PREVIEW *CALL*: THE WRITTEN NOTE'S NUMBERS, PLUS WHAT GOKUL ONLY SAID OUT LOUD — *"DEMAND IS DEFINITELY HIGHER"* THAN THE +11-12% Q4 GUIDE, NO 70% GM IN 2027 (67-68%), CAPEX BIAS *ABOVE* US$100bn IF THE WHOLE iPHONE GOES TO A14 IN 2H28, TEXAS = SPACEX/TESLA ON SUPPLY-CHAIN READS, AND 60-70% OF *FUTURE* CoWoS DEMAND TO OSATs WITH NO ROYALTY.**

_Source: Gokul Hariharan (J.P. Morgan) @ JPM 'TSMC: 3Q26 Preview' webinar, 2026-10-01 - Capstone notes (Notion transcript) — `_equity_calls/Semis/2026-10-01_JPMorgan_TSMC-3Q26-preview-call-Hariharan.md`. Speaker CONFIRMED from the Outlook calendar confirmation; host "Sharara" (JPM sales, ASR spelling) and client questioners UNCONFIRMED. ⚠️ Everything numerical below is JPM's MODEL ("JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01") or JPM's supply-chain CHECKS — not TSMC guidance: TSMC has given no 2027 numbers and Gokul expects no numerical long-term update before January. The public layers of the same view — the 09-30 written note (block (A) below) and the JPM desk "TSM Preview Call Notes" (Joshua Meyers, 10-01) — are not re-counted here; only the call-only increments are logged. **Recurring series:** 1Q26 preview call (2026-04-02: 1Q +7% q/q, GM ~67% vs 65% guide-high; 2Q +6-8%, GM 66-67%; ~30-31 EUV tools 2026) → 2Q26 preview call (2026-07-08: 2Q GM ~69.5-70%; 2027 ~10% N2/N3 hikes → blended ASP +25-26%; FY26 capex US$56-58bn; ~US$220bn cumulative 2026-28; CoWoS-27 ~175k→~200k mostly OSATs) → this call. Machine transcript: CoWoS [transcribed "CoaS"/"CoVOS"/"Covox"/"Covax"/"CoWAS"/"CoaOS"], COUPE [transcribed "Coop"/"co-op"], Kyber [transcribed "Kube" — verify], Trainium [transcribed "Trinium"], ASE [transcribed "ASC"], fab [transcribed "VFAP"/"VAP"] corrected per `_data/transcription_glossary.json`; the archived transcript is unchanged._

- 🔴 **The print, as JPM frames it (model, not guidance):** 3Q26 *"come into the high end of guidance range"*; 4Q26 guide *"about low teens, about 11, 12 percent"* q/q — ⚠️ *"demand is definitely higher than that, but I don't think they have the capacity to really drive a lot more growth"*; FY26 USD growth *"in the low 40s"*. GM *"66, 67% kind of range in Q3 and Q4"* on a bigger N2 dilution plus some TWD appreciation. 4Q vs 3Q: N2 dilution worsens (N2 mix much bigger) while **N3 crosses above corporate-average GM** — net *"not very different within the same like 67% kind of range"*. Buy-side read: *"I don't see like a lot of dispersion for this quarter so far"*; even the guide-high is *"only like a one or two percent kind of beat on the full year number"*, so *"people want them to say more about CapEx and next couple of years growth"* (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).
- 🔴🔭 **2027 GM — the call is firmer than the note: *"For 2027, I don't think the gross margin can reach 70%"*.** JPM at *"67%, 68% kind of range"*: price increases across the board, but *"a lot of the two nanometer depreciation will start kicking in"* and the N2 yield/margin penalty *"will not go away before maybe Q4 of next year or Q1 of 28"*. 2028: N2 close to corporate average, A14 dilution *"manageable"* (first 1-2 quarters of ramp) → *"some quarters in 2028, we do think there is a chance that we could get to 70% gross margin or higher"*. 🔭 This sits BELOW Fubon's quarterly path (70.2% / 70.4% in 3Q-4Q27, FY27F 69.3%; Sherman Shang, 2026-09, block (B) below) and below the Capstone internal view from the GS-Communacopia debrief (*"no way this guy's margin will not go to 70% in 2027, probably already in 4Q"*, 2026-09-16 — Changelog 09-21); it is consistent with the house model, which never assumed a 70% 4Q26 (Debate bullet, 2026-09-10) (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).
- **Estimates:** *"26, not much change. 27, 28, we are lifting our numbers by about 5% to 6% each"* (the magnitude behind the 09-30 EPS NT$147.80 / 190.42); data-center AI revenue CAGR **~70%**, *"largely unchanged"* (vs ~69% 2024-29E on the 08-12 note). ⚠️ The 2027 CoWoS growth figure in the same sentence did not survive the transcription (*"our numbers are about growth in CoWoS next year"* — number missing); use the written note's grid (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).
- 🔴 **N3 / N2 / pricing — as said:** 2026 N3 gap *"around 600K"* wafers (supply vs demand); 2027 *"could be even bigger given NVIDIA will be taking close to like 55, 60% of the N3 [transcribed "entry"] wafers"*; **Apple and AMD** named as *"pretty big drivers of N2 demand"* in 2027. Blended price **+~25% in 2027 vs "high teens" in 2026**; like-for-like **~+10% on both N3 and N2**; *"for the first time in this cycle"* **+3-5% on N7 / 12nm / 16nm** (the note's "low-to-mid single digit", now a range) (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01). ⚠️ Keep this beside the Digitimes Jan-2027 "+3-6% all processes" relay (Changelog 09-24): JPM's +3-5% is the MATURE-node leg only; leading edge is +10%.
- 🔴🔴 **Capex US$86bn (2027) / US$100bn (2028) — and the upward bias is explicit:** drivers are *"continuing strong pull-in for N2, but also some pull-in for A14"*. JPM supply-chain checks: **A14 ~30-40k wpm by end-2028**, with fabs [transcribed "VAPs"] in **Hsinchu and Kaohsiung**; TSMC *"trying to bring in some iPhone production in 2028 into A14"*, wafer starts *"probably in Q2 [2028]"*; *"if they're able to actually do all of iPhones on A14 in second half 28, then I think the CapEx number probably will go up even higher from the 100 billion"* — *"the bias is definitely on the upward side"*. Why the pull-in: (1) A14 *"tracking better than expected"*, **SRAM yields already ~90%+** (*"quite good for this stage of the product ramp"*); (2) to avoid 2026-27's N3 bunching of smartphone + HPC — if Apple moves to A14 in 2028, *"almost all of the N2 demand will probably be allocated for HPC products"*. Full-iPhone-on-A14 would need **~60-80k wafer starts by 2Q28** (said as "70K to 80K… or 60 to 70K") — *"that's not in our numbers"* (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call + JPM supply-chain checks, 2026-10-01). 🔭 vs this page: house sensitivity (2026-07-17) carries capex US$62 / 80 / 100bn and **A14 at 30k end-2027 / 60k end-2028** — JPM's A14 30-40k by END-2028 is half the house's 2028 figure while its 2027 capex is US$6bn above the house; Fubon US$80bn / 95bn is below JPM, UBS US$90bn / 105bn (09-28) above it.
- **EUV sufficiency (client question):** on JPM's numbers *"EUV should still be sufficient"* into 2028 — TSMC *"is trying to basically ensure that they keep around 45 to 50% market share within ASML's EUV shipment pretty much every year"*; the full-iPhone-A14 case *"might be a little bit of a stretch"*; *"lead times are definitely extending"*, with *"some like price premium"* to get tools on time (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01). → read-through to [[ASML]] / `themes/semicap-wfe.md` (not edited here; house Bristlemoon check above assumes 45 TSMC tools in 2027/28).
- 🔴 **Texas second US cluster — JPM supply-chain checks:** *"Based on our checks, I think it is credible. I think they are in final discussions"*; ramp *"more like 2030 and beyond"*; an announcement possibly *"end of this year or early next year"*; **first 3-4 US phases stay Arizona**, follow-on phases *"split between Arizona and Texas"*. *"Some of the supply chain indicators are basically suggesting that this is largely to do with supporting SpaceX or Tesla"* — if so, the ramp *"could be faster than what Arizona has seen, given you'll have a dedicated customer"*. On TerraFab (client question): *"I don't think TerraFab is going away. They are already ordering some tools for pilot line, but I don't think their production is starting before 29 or 2030"*; Tesla/SpaceX under-engage TSMC today because TSMC has *"only one campus, which is already kind of sold out to most of their bigger clients"*; *"I don't think it's either or kind of situation right now"* (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call + JPM supply-chain checks, 2026-10-01). ⚠️ Channel read, not a company statement; TSMC has not confirmed Texas (09-29 EDN row in the log below).
- 🔴🔭 **Packaging — the outsourcing ratio, said as a share of future DEMAND:** *"TSMC focus still seems to be mostly on 3D SoIC, while CoWoS [transcribed "CoWAS"] is likely to be more and more pushed towards the OSAT partners, either on a partnership basis or on a full outsourcing basis"*; *"maybe 60-70% of the future CoWoS demand will be supplied by the OSATs rather than TSMC itself"*, especially lower-end / CPU packages that *"don't require very large package size"*; TSMC keeps large-package accelerator CoWoS, 3D SoIC (*"still very small and pretty new"*, stays in-house) and does *"some kind of EMIB-like R&D"*; CoPoS [transcribed "CoaPos"] *"going a little bit slow"*. **No royalty:** on the ASE [transcribed "ASC"] on-substrate partnership *"the customer is still owned by TSMC. So you just sell it back to TSMC"*; when a customer is sent to a full-stack OSAT partner *"typically, there is no license"* — TSMC recommends tool set / configuration. 🔭 Different basis from the written note's **non-TSMC 23% / 30% of CoWoS-class CAPACITY end-27/28**: 60-70% is a share of future CoWoS wafer DEMAND, closer to JPM's own 75% → 85% → 90% outsourced-share ladder on the ASE note (2026-07-30, Changelog 07-31). Detail (ASPs, EMIB-T, TPU v9, CoPoS, COUPE) on `themes/cowos-packaging.md` (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).
- **CoWoS pricing, as a TSMC revenue input:** CoWoS-L ~**US$15-16K per wafer** (varies with RDL layers / LSI count — *"not like a standard ASP"*), *"I don't think there is a big increase in CoWoS price next year on a like-for-like basis"*; CoWoS-R ~US$8-9K, CoWoS-S ~US$10-11K per wafer (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).
- **CPO / COUPE timing (client question):** COUPE [transcribed "Coop"] *"is going slow"* — optical loss across the signal chain (PIC hybrid bonding → FAU → final packaging), *"still some gap between what is desired from the customer, which is NVIDIA, versus what TSMC is able to deliver"*; first-generation COUPE for the Spectrum-X CPO switch [transcribed "Spectrum X CPU"] *"is still ramping up"*, yield *"not at a desired level"*; Rubin Ultra CPO is talked about, but *"we may have to wait until Feynman before we really start to see a proper scale-up CPO kind of adoption using the Kyber [transcribed "Kube" — verify] architecture"* (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01). → `themes/optical-cpo.md` / [[NVDA]] (not edited here).
- **Other accelerator reads (for the N3 / CoWoS demand stack):** Trainium 3 [transcribed "Trinium"] chip shipments *"not seen any changes"* — JPM's *"last three changes have been to the upside"* (~+15% for 2026 two weeks ago, +5-10% for 2027), lifecycle *"around 5 million plus"* units; TSMC expanded **CoWoS-R** partly for Trainium 3 and **Maya 200 / Maya 300**; the Digitimes "behind expectations" item is read as a **PCB / downstream board** constraint, not chips. TPU v9 [transcribed "HUMU-FISH" = HumuFish] is *"all EMIB"* with no CoWoS path (detail on `themes/cowos-packaging.md`) (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call + JPM supply-chain checks, 2026-10-01).
- **Europe marketing colour:** investor interest centred on MediaTek, TSMC capacity upgrades, advanced packaging and substrates; memory is *"a mixed bag in terms of sentiment"* on the de-spec narrative — *"memory needs a completely new catalyst to get investors really come back"* despite strong fundamentals (JPM · Hariharan, 3Q26 preview call, 2026-10-01).

_Broker reactions to the 2Q26 print / earnings call; the next /wiki-ingest run continues this window._

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-10-01 (/run-inbox, unit "taiwan") — TWO PRIMARIES INTO THE OCT-15 PRINT: JPM RAISES THE PT TO NT$3,300 AND CAPEX TO US$64/86/100bn; THE FUBON DECK BEHIND THE 09-26/09-28 JEFFERIES SALES RELAYS LANDS AND CORRECTS THREE OF THEIR CAPACITY NUMBERS.**

**(A) J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan / Jennifer Hsieh / David Chou / Jason Chen / Subham Singhania, "TSMC: Expect strong 3Q26 results, healthy 4Q26 guidance and a faster pace of capacity ramp: Raise PT to NT$3300", 2026-09-30 (OW; Jun-27 PT NT$3,200 → NT$3,300 on ~20x 12m-forward P/E; px NT$2,475 on 29 Sep).**
- 🔴 **3Q26 at the top of the guide:** JPMe revenue **US$45.8bn (+14% q/q)** vs the US$44.6-45.8bn guide (+12% q/q at the midpoint); **GM 66.8%** vs the 65-67% guide, on higher leading-edge UTR and price hikes (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan "TSMC: Expect strong 3Q26 results…", 2026-09-30).
- 🔴 **4Q26 guide expectation: +10-12% q/q in USD (JPMe +11%, US$50.96bn), GM 65.5-67.5% (JPMe 66.8%)**, capped by N3 capacity and no further price increase this year; that takes FY26 USD revenue to **+41%**, in line with the "slightly above 40%" guide. JPM does not expect a quantitative 2027 capex guide before Jan-27 (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- 🔴🔴 **Capex raised to US$64bn / US$86bn / US$100bn for 2026/27/28, from US$62bn / US$81bn / US$90bn** (FY26 at the top of the US$60-64bn guide; the 2027 bar in JPM's Figure 7 reads 85.5); **~US$250bn cumulative 2026-28**. Drivers: faster N3/N2 ramp, an earlier A14 and CoWoS (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- 🔴 **Pricing, 2027:** a broader price increase, **~+10% on leading edge (N3, N2)**, milder on N5/N7 and CoWoS, and a **low-to-mid-single-digit increase on mature nodes (N7 and above), the first of this upcycle**; JPM expects multi-year price contracts. **Blended wafer ASP +17% in 2026 and +25% in 2027** (HPC N3 carries a 20-25% premium over mobile; hot / super-hot runs carry 50% / 100% premiums). N2+N3 ~60% of wafer sales in 2027 vs ~40% in 2026 (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- **Growth and estimates:** 2027 USD revenue **+39% (from +35%)**, 2028 ~+30%; **Adj. EPS FY26/27/28E NT$108.62 / 147.80 / 190.42 (prior 108.15 / 141.37 / 180.01; BBG 107.06 / 140.57 / 176.50)**; revenue NT$5,473bn / 7,603bn / 9,816bn; GM 66.9% / 67.8% / 68.6%. Quarterly EPS 3Q26E NT$27.92, 4Q26E NT$31.37, 1Q27E NT$33.55. GM mid-to-high 60s near term, with a chance of approaching 70% once N2 dilution eases (late-27 / early-28) (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- 🔴 **Capacity, year-end k wfpm:** **N3 117 / 178 / 223 / 250 (2025-28E)** with N3 revenue US$26bn / 47bn / 78bn / 91bn; **N2 17 / 60 / 118 / 210 (2028 raised from 177k)** with N2 revenue US$9bn / 36bn / 83bn (2026-28E). N3 stays tight in 2027 (the ~600k-wafer 2026 supply-demand gap widens) and **NVDA takes 55-60% of N3 wafer shipments in 2027**; N3 eases only in 2028. N2 share stays 95%+; nine fab phases over three years (1-2 in Fab 20, 5+ in Fab 22, 1 in AZ P3) (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- 🔴 **A14 pull-in:** checks say risk production in 1H27 and MP ramp in 1H28, two to three quarters early, on SRAM/device yields already 90%+; JPM models **~US$1.8bn A14 revenue in 2H28** and sees risk to the upside. A second US fab cluster in Texas is "credible", but beyond 2030 (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).
- **Packaging / competition:** TSMC CoWoS **200k / 225k wfpm end-27/28 with non-TSMC 60k / 95k** (non-TSMC 23% / 30% of CoWoS-class capacity), the same marks as JPM's 09-17 note; SoIC 35k end-27, 70k end-28. Intel EMIB-T is a viable lower-cost option up to 10-12x reticle, but substrate yields are **55-60%** on JPM's checks. JPM benchmarks Intel 18A ≈ N3E and 14A (late 2028) ≈ N2/N2P (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-30).

**(B) Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, "Taiwan upstream 2026 outlook" (Sept-2026 deck, 119pp, Jefferies Hong Kong co-branded) — the primary behind the 09-26 / 09-27 Jefferies sales relays below. TSMC Buy, TP NT$3,500 (unchanged), px NT$2,470.**
- **Fubon estimates:** EPS FY26F / FY27F **NT$108.80 / 155.41** (FY27 5% above JPM's new 147.80); sales NT$5,389bn / 7,541bn. ⚠️ **On 4Q26 Fubon is the lower house:** sales NT$1,441bn (3Q26F, +13.5% q/q) and **NT$1,543bn (4Q26F, +7.1% q/q, TWD)** vs JPM's NT$1,453bn / NT$1,616bn (+11% q/q in USD). Quarterly GM **3Q26F 67.1%, 4Q26F 67.4%, then 67.2% / 68.9% / 70.2% / 70.4% through 1Q-4Q27** (FY27F 69.3%). Fubon expects TSMC to reset its long-term GM target to "55% & higher" (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang "Taiwan upstream 2026 outlook", 2026-09).
- 🔴 **Capex model: US$62bn / 80bn / 95bn for 2026/27/28**, of which advanced packaging is 20% (US$12.4bn / 16bn / 19bn; CoWoS/CoPoS 85% / 82% / 75% of that). **Industry foundry capex US$118.5bn / 143bn / 170bn**, with TSMC ~56% of 2028 and ~72% of the 2024-28 increment. ⚠️ Fubon's capex path is now **below JPM's** in every year (US$80bn vs US$86bn in 2027; US$95bn vs US$100bn in 2028) (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang, 2026-09).
- 🔴 **Node capacity, k WPM (Fubon Table 5):** **N2 30 / 50 / 70 / 80 through 1Q-4Q26, then 80 / 90 / 120 / 150 through 2027** (4Q27 exit 150k, "+20k vs. prior estimate"; 2027 average 110k); **N3 160k at 4Q26 → 210k at 4Q27**. 2027 demand exceeds supply by **64.9% at N2 and 32.7% at N3** (110-120% implied utilisation); N3 runs at 120% utilisation through 2027 on Fubon's node table (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang, 2026-09).
- **Pricing:** another big round of wafer price increases in 2027; the **A16 wafer starts at US$37-38k** (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang, 2026-09).
- **AI content and customer mix:** TSMC AI revenue **US$55.3bn (2026E) → US$101.3bn (2027E)**, 41% core / ~43% all-in of a US$235.7bn 2027 revenue. 2027E customer mix **NVDA 29%, Apple 13%, AMD 13%, Broadcom 12%, MediaTek 8%, Qualcomm 5%, Marvell 4%, Intel 2%** (NVDA 25% in 2026E) (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang, 2026-09).
- **Packaging:** TSMC CoWoS **120k (4Q26) → 180k (4Q27) → 220k (4Q28)** plus CoPoS 30k by 4Q28 (250k combined); CoWoS output 1.17mn wafers (2026) → 1.89mn (2027) → 2.46mn (2028E), with CoPoS 225k in 2028. SoIC 30 / 50 / 70 kwpq at end-FY26/27/28, deferred behind CoWoS (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang, 2026-09).
- ⚠️ **Relay corrections against this primary** (the 09-26 / 09-27 rows stay in the log below): N2 was raised to 150k from a **prior ~130k** ("+20k"), not from 120k; Fubon's **N3 end-27 figure is 210k, not "220K"** (JPM's own N3 end-27 is ~223k, so the relay's number looks like a different house's); the deck's 2028 capex is a point **US$95bn**, so the "US$95-100bn" and "US$95-110B" ranges were verbal; the "20 front-end fabs" is the deck's **"20 possible build units"** (13 Taiwan core + 1 boundary + 6 overseas, "exact '20' remains a range"); the 19GW / 31GW is Fubon's **accelerator nameplate TDP (18.9GW 2026F → 31.3GW 2027F)**. The deck calls 180k CoWoS "20% above the prior 150kwpm forecast", so the relay's "unchanged" holds only against the 180k this page already carried. The FOCI ~30% CPO yield and "TSMC can support ~30GW" are not in the deck.

UBS: *"FROM '26, IT HAS INTERNAL ANALYSIS TO TRACK THE POWER SUPPLY BUILD-UP ESPECIALLY IN THE US."* ALSO: INDUSTRY CoWoS TO **270kwpm END-'27** (UBS'S OWN 260k FROM THREE DAYS EARLIER IS SUPERSEDED — SEE `## Changelog`), A SECOND WAFER-ON-SUBSTRATE OSAT ALONGSIDE ASE, WFE ORDERS "VERY STRONG" WITH ONE VENDOR AT **2x REVS INTO FY28/3**, AND HIGH-NA STILL **NOT** BASE CASE FOR A14. SEPARATELY, REDBURN'S FIRST BOTTOMS-UP HYBRID-BONDING TOOL MODEL MAKES TSMC **THE SINGLE LARGEST BUCKET IN LOGIC: 448 BONDERS BY 2030.**

(UBS · **Nicolas Gaudois / Randy Abrams / Sunny Lin / Timothy Arcuri / Kenji Yasui / Jimmy Yu / Jerry Su / Jimmy Yoon / Diana Chang / Luke Yoo / Atsuhiro Kinoshita / Shingo Hirata**, *"Global I/O: Tech Hardware & Semis — 3Q26 UBS APAC Tech Tour: AI strength unaltered, Semicap noticeably bullish"*, **2026-09-22**, 27pp, off **40 company meetings** across China/HK/Taiwan/Korea/Japan + the 2026 Taiwan Summit; priced 21-Sep-2026. **[[TSM]]: UBS Buy, price NT$2,480, PT NT$3,650** (Sunny Lin; **'27E P/E 16.6x, '28E 12.5x, '27E P/BV 6.13x**) — ✅ **A REITERATION, NOT A CHANGE: NT$3,650 is the mark this page has carried since 2026-07-27. TSMC is also on UBS's APAC Tech MOST PREFERRED list and UBS is OVERWEIGHT Leading Edge Foundry.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_Global_IO_Tech_Hardware_Semis_3Q26_UBSAPACTe.html))

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND-DURATION STATEMENT, VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE FURTHEST-OUT VISIBILITY CLAIM ON THIS PAGE: *"Given the 3-5 year engagement lead time for advanced nodes with foundry especially for AI/HPC, TSMC ALREADY HAS GOOD VISIBILITY AND SEES THE STRUCTURAL AI/HPC OPPORTUNITY REMAIN ROBUST BY '29-'30. While there may be uncertainty of short term slowdown, the 5 year trend to '30 is resilient."*** ➤ **The mechanism is the useful part and it is one this page has never spelled out: the 3-5 year engagement lead time means TSMC is not forecasting 2030 demand, it is already BOOKING the design engagements that produce it. That is a structurally different kind of visibility from a capex guide, and it is the direct counterweight to the "short-term AI slowdown" thread running through this quarter's flow.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW BEHAVIOUR, AND IT IS THE MOST INTERESTING LINE IN THE NOTE: *"TSMC has also been taking additional efforts in assessing the industry readiness for new datacenter construction, for example FROM '26, IT HAS INTERNAL ANALYSIS TO TRACK THE POWER SUPPLY BUILD-UP ESPECIALLY IN THE US."*** ➤ **The foundry is now underwriting its own capacity plan against GRID READINESS — i.e. TSMC has concluded that the binding constraint on its customers' deployments may sit outside the semiconductor supply chain entirely, and has built an internal tracker for it. Nothing on this page has previously had TSMC looking past wafers, CoWoS and substrates to electricity. Treat it as a new input into how the 2027-30 capex path gets sized, and as a tell that management's own downside scenario is a POWER scenario rather than a demand scenario.** ⚠️ **No GW figure, no MW figure and no dated target is attached — UBS gives the behaviour, not the output. Do not attach any GW number to it, and do not mix it with facility-GW, IT-load-GW or 800V-subset-GW bases used elsewhere on this wiki.** Cross-file [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) if a reciprocal entry is wanted.

Another vendor characterised TSMC as VERY AGGRESSIVE FOR BOTH N3 AND N2."*** ➤ **Two independent supplier voices, one of them modelling a doubling of its TSMC revenue into FYE-Mar-2028. Note the decomposition UBS supplies and do not skip it: the 2x is NOT pure wafer growth — it explicitly includes MORE PROCESS STEPS per wafer at advanced nodes and POSSIBLE SHARE GAINS at that vendor. Supplier revenue ≠ TSMC capex.** ✅ Consistent with the capex thread this page carries (FY26 US$60-64bn, "next three years even more significantly higher") and with the A14 orders now appearing in the same sentence as N3/N2/A16 — the first time A14 shows up as a live ORDER driver rather than a roadmap node.

**(B) REDBURN "BONDERNOMICS" (2026-09-23) — TSMC IS THE LARGEST SINGLE BUCKET IN THE FIRST BOTTOMS-UP HYBRID-BONDING TOOL MODEL**

(Rothschild & Co Redburn · **Nabeel Aziz** (w/ Timm Schulze-Melander), *"Bondernomics"*, **2026-09-23**, 57pp. ⚠️⚠️ **SUBJECT COMPANY IS BE SEMICONDUCTOR (BESI): Buy retained, PT €250 CUT FROM €350, px €187 @ 18-Sep-2026. [[TSM]] IS "NOT RATED" AT REDBURN — no TSMC rating, price target or estimate anywhere in the note. Everything below is a read-through from a tool-demand model.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bondernomics.html))

- 🔴🔴🔴 **TSMC IS THE MODEL'S BIGGEST NUMBER: a 448-BONDER MARKET OPPORTUNITY BY 2030, OF WHICH BESI CAPTURES 401** — out of a **721-unit total LOGIC opportunity (BESI 652)** and against **c150 bonders installed in the field today**. Next largest is [[INTC]] at 118/108, then [[AAPL]] 111/99 and [[AMD]] 111/101. ⚠️ **BASIS: hybrid-bonding TOOL UNITS on a 2030 installed-base basis. This is NOT wpm, NOT kwpm, NOT SoIC lines and NOT comparable to the CoWoS capacity figures above. The only other bonder figure on this page is Redburn's own 2026-06-23 field mark — *"each 10,000-unit SOIC line needs 15-20 hybrid bonders"* (Conor O'Mara) — which is a per-line intensity, a different measurement again.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE OBSERVED-THROUGHPUT FINDING, AND IT IS MEASURED ON TSMC'S OWN FLOOR: *AMD's hybrid-bonding fleet is installed AT TSMC in SoIC*, and the implied real-world throughput from AMD's 2025 output is **75 units/hour against a 1,500 uph specification — −95%.*** ➤ **This is the single most consequential number in the note and it lands on this page because the tools are in TSMC's fab. Redburn's own reading of the two candidate explanations is the part to carry: he considers *"TSMC invested in capacity ahead of the ramp"* the LESS LIKELY explanation, *"given its further investments in its AP7 fab in 2026 offer MORE THAN DOUBLE THE SoIC CAPACITY of the existing AP6 fab"* — i.e. he reads TSMC as ADDING SoIC capacity, not sitting on idle capacity, and therefore attributes the gap to real productivity limits in D2W hybrid bonding.** ⚠️⚠️ **NAME THE CONTRADICTION, DO NOT SMOOTH IT: this page carries the OPPOSITE read on AP7 from Fubon, twice and more recently — *"mainly in AP7 and to be more specific, it's in phase two and phase three… TSMC is slowing down the SoIC expansion a little bit. Because it looks like the CPU demand is also being pushed out… they will prioritize the CoWoS production first"* (Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03), and the 07-28 capacity cut from 90k to 70k in 4Q28. Redburn (09-23) uses AP7's SoIC expansion as evidence that TSMC is NOT over-built; Fubon (07-28/08-03) says specific AP7 SoIC phases are being deferred in favour of CoWoS. BOTH REMAIN ON THE PAGE. They are reconcilable only if "more than double AP6" is a DESIGNED capacity and Fubon's cut is a TIMING deferral of phases 2-3 — which is plausible but is NOT stated by either source, so it is not adopted here.**
- 🔴🔴 **AP7'S UNDERWRITING IS NAMED: *"TSMC's AP7 investment is underwritten by the ramp of AMD Helios racks in 2H26."*** ➤ **A specific customer programme tied to a specific fab investment — and it gives the AP7/SoIC timing dispute above a testable hook: if Helios volume slips, the AP7 SoIC case weakens; UBS's own note in this same run has ODMs putting the Helios higher-volume inflection at **Q227** (compute boards start shipping Q426), which is LATER than "2H26" and therefore leans, mildly, toward Fubon's deferral read.** Cross-file [[AMD]].
- 🔴🔴 **PACKAGING ROADMAP — THE APPLE STEP THAT REDBURN CALLS "A GATEWAY TO HB": *"Apple's A20 Pro in the iPhone 18 Pro is expected to have moved from TSMC's integrated fan-out package-on-package (InFO PoP) to a WAFER-LEVEL MULTI-CHIP MODULE (WMCM) approach."*** He frames WMCM as the gateway because the smartphone SoC has moved to **chiplets** (microbump-bonded today purely on cost), and models **Apple inserting hybrid bonding into the A-series for the first time in 2029**. ✅ **This CORROBORATES the WMCM/N2 production detail this page already carries from Culpium (Tim Culpan, 2026-08-05) and from the SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown confirming A20 on N2 (09-20) — and it is the first source on this page to put a DATE on the next packaging step (A-series HB in 2029) rather than just the current one.** Cross-file [[AAPL]].
- 🔴 **SoIC's forward driver, as Redburn sees it: TSMC's SoIC capacity investment is being made *"with line of sight on Nvidia GPU adoption as a new high-volume application"* — and BESI moved Nvidia **Feynman** from its HIGH-case to its MID-case at the **June 2026 investor day**.** ⚠️ **HB adoption on Feynman is NOT confirmed by NVIDIA; Redburn's own table has Nvidia GPU = NO today (Rubin dual chiplet, microbump bonded) with HB "expected with Feynman."** ➤ **Read it as the supplier's probability-weighting moving up a notch, not as a design win.** Cross-file [[NVDA]].
- 🔴 **BROADCOM: *"Broadcom has adopted 3DIC technology WITH TSMC for its custom ASIC customers"* (reiterated at BESI's June 2026 Investor Day), and Broadcom's 3.5D XDSiP process uses hybrid bonding.** ✅ **A named second high-volume SoIC customer beyond AMD — and it is the ASIC pool, which is where this page's CoWoS allocation debate concentrates.** Cross-file [[AVGO]].
- 🔴 **CPO: the PIC-to-EIC bond *"requires direct Cu-Cu hybrid bonding to hit 200Gbit/s per lane"*; optical-engine ASP **$450**, CPO TAM **$15bn skewed $13bn to scale-up**, **20m optical engines by 2030** (one PIC-to-EIC bond each).** ➤ **The physics claim is the one that matters for [[TSM]]: if 200G/lane requires direct Cu-Cu, then COUPE's roadmap and TSMC's SoIC line converge — the optical engine becomes a hybrid-bonded product, not a microbumped one. Consistent with the 2-4 micron COUPE bonding pitch and 0.06dB-at-112Gbps loss figures this page already carries from SEMICON Taiwan (K.C. Hsu, 08-31).** ⚠️ **TAM figures are Redburn's (sourced to Mike Harrison, "Tripping the Light Fantastic", 2026-05-01) and are NOT reconciled against the optical-TAM framings held elsewhere on this wiki — different pricing logics.**
- ✅ **AND THE ONE LINE THAT IS A MILD NEGATIVE FOR TSMC'S PACKAGING SHARE: *"market tightness in leading-edge 2.5D has led to overflow orders from TSMC to the OSATs AND TO INTEL."*** ➤ **Third-party confirmation of the overflow channel — and the first note to name [[INTC]] alongside the OSATs as an actual recipient rather than a candidate. Of a piece with the share-leakage marks this page already carries (BofA 08-20: CoWoS-R for CPU indirectly outsourced to ASE; @dnystedt 08-30: "Intel outsourcing more as it focuses on EMIB").**

  ⚠️ **BASIS NOTE, LOAD-BEARING: JPM's "Total TSMC" row *includes* sub-contracting of oS to ASE/SPIL (from 2025) and of interposer to Vanguard. "Total others (OSATs)" counts only OSATs' own full-stack CoWoS sold direct to fabless. ➤ THIS RESOLVES AN APPARENT CONTRADICTION WITH [[themes/semicap-wfe]], which carries an "outsourced share" going 8% (2024) → 46% (2025) → 75% (2026E) → 85% (2027E). That series is on a DIFFERENT BASIS — it counts the sub-contracted work JPM keeps inside the TSMC row. On JPM's basis the OSAT share of total consumption is 6.8% (2026E) → 18.8% (2027E) → 25.8% (2028E). The two are not comparable and must never be netted.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE CANONICAL NVDA-SHARE NUMBER GETS A THIRD SOURCE, AND IT HOLDS.** `_meta/assumptions.md` carries **`cowos-allocation` canonical: NVDA ~50-55% of TSMC CoWoS 2027** (Fubon ~50%+; Nomura <55%). **JPM's new table computes to 1,060 / 2,006 = 52.8% of TSMC in 2027 and 1,240 / 2,471 = 50.2% of the industry** (2028: 54.6% of TSMC, 52.1% of industry). ➤ **Squarely inside the canonical band, from a third independent house. Added as a variant row to the ledger — the band is now well-corroborated rather than two-source.**
- 🔴🔴 **SoIC GETS A CAPACITY LADDER *AND* A CONVERSION CONSTANT — the constant is the useful part.** *"We expect TSMC to speed up SoIC capacity expansion from 2H27, reaching **35K wfpm by end 2027 and 70K wfpm by end 2028**"*, driven by **NVDA Feynman adopting A16-on-A16 logic-logic stacking**, plus MTIA 600, OpenAI Serrano and possibly one TPUv10 family member. ➤ **The conversion, stated as back-of-envelope by JPM itself: *"~10k wfpm of SoIC capacity to support 1M accelerators (each containing 2 reticle size base dies, similar to AMD MI 350 or 450)."*** ⚠️ **JPM flags this is far softer than the CoWoS conversion — *"Converting SoIC capacity to chip output is not as straightforward as CoWoS, given the wide variance in bottom-die specifications and yield."* Logged as an ESTIMATE-grade constant, not a canonical one; it should not be used to back out accelerator units without a stated yield/spec assumption.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE PACKAGING ROADMAP FORK — TSMC IS PUSHING CUSTOMERS TO 3D SoIC RATHER THAN EVER-LARGER 2.5D, AND THE REASON IS SILICON WASTE.** *"TSMC's preferred approach is for customers to leverage 3D SoIC as the additional dimension for scaling, rather than trying to scale CoWoS-L to extremely large package sizes (**14x reticle size will only yield 3 packages per CoWoS wafer**, resulting in significant wastage of silicon)."* Current largest package is **MI450 at 5.5x reticle**. **AMD MI500 scaled DOWN from a previously planned 14-15x to ~9x (Plan B ~6x); NVDA Feynman caps at 6-7x precisely because it uses 3D SoIC.** ⚠️ **GPU customers are willing (AMD already, NVDA from Feynman); large ASIC clients (TPU, Trainium) are still hesitant — MTIA 600 and OpenAI Serrano likely the first SoIC-based ASICs in 2028-29.** ➤ **Direct read for this page: the reticle-scaling path management described on 09-09/10 as "5.5x → 14x in a couple of years" is, per JPM's checks, NOT the path the customers are taking. Logged as a live tension between first-party guidance and third-party supply-chain checks — not resolved.**
- 🔴 **CoPoS SLIPS — AND THIS IS A DOWNGRADE VS THE PAGE'S EXISTING TIMELINE.** *"TSMC's CoPoS is progressing more slowly than expected, with **risk production likely only in 2028 and mass production in 2029 at the earliest**"*, on **warpage and glass cracking**. Corroborated from the OSAT side (ASE's CoPoS-like PLP *"remain in the engineering phase and are not expected to reach mass production before 2029"*). ⚠️ **[[themes/outros-asia]] carries UBS on GPTC's share rising to 70-80% in CoPoS from ~50% in CoWoS — that share call is unaffected, but the DATE it applies to moves out.**
- 🔴 **A COST NUMBER ON THE CoWoS ALTERNATIVE, WHICH IS WHY THE SUBSTRATE LAYER KEEPS WINNING:** demand for **EMIB-like solutions (RDL on substrate)** is rising from fabless customers *"due to lower costs compared to CoWoS packages of equivalent reticle size (**up to 50% cheaper at optimal yields**)"* — and **TSMC is itself exploring the same approach with substrate vendors Unimicron and Kinsus** and with OSATs. ⚠️ **This 50% figure is consistent with the one already on [[INTC]] ("50% cheaper"), and the two should be treated as the same claim from different angles, not as independent confirmation.**

EPS NT$108.15 / 141.37 / 180.01** (vs BBG consensus 107.38 / 140.98 / 176.64 — JPM ~0.7%/0.3%/1.9% above); Adj. P/E **22.4x / 17.2x / 13.5x**; FCFF **2,201,778 / 1,861,920 / 3,230,668** (note the FY27E dip — capex-driven); ROE 42.7% / 40.3% / 38.0%. _Shares o/s 25,930m; mkt cap US$1,977bn; 52-wk NT$2,535-1,265; BBG ANR 39 Buy / 2 Hold / 0 Sell._


**🆕 Signal vs management — Q3 FY26 window, re-evaluated 2026-09-15** (this window carries no seeded signal table; this dated block is the synthesis, and it supersedes nothing above):
- **AI demand / duration** — *Management (2Q26 call, 2026-07-16):* AI demand robust, FY26 revenue growth raised to >40%, mid-to-high-50s AI CAGR. *Flow:* UBS Taiwan Summit Day 1 reports TSMC **already has visibility on the AI opportunity through 2030** and views the longer-term trend as still robust, with the whole Taiwan supply chain **"broadly constructive"** despite the weekend AI-safety headlines (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Diana Chang, 2026-09-15). ➤ **Signal: ✓ CONFIRMS, and EXTENDS the claim from intensity to DURATION — the first *through-2030* visibility statement on this page.**
- **🆕 AI demand / duration — RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-18 (New Street Research · Ferragu, *"Here we are – $4tn AI capex in 2030?"*, 2026-09-18).** *Flow:* an outside house raises 2030 AI capex to **$4,150bn** — the third mark in one earnings cycle (~$2.4tn → ~$3tn *"following TSMC and ASML's earnings"* → $4.15tn) — with **~275GW** of installed AI capacity in 2030 and **XPUs + CPUs ≈ $1.87tn** of 2030 spending. ➤ **Signal: ✓ CONFIRMS the duration claim from the outside, and it is the first note on this page to attribute part of its own forecast raise to TSMC's print. ⚠️ But it is a FORECAST, not evidence, and its TSMC sentence ("abandoned its mid-to-high 50s% CAGR guide") is NSR's inference — the page's primary has C.C. Wei declining a figure, so the mid-to-high-50s mark STANDS.**
- **Capex** — *Management:* FY26 US$60-64bn (raised from ~US$58bn), next three years *"even more significantly higher"*, no numeric 2027/28 guide. *Flow:* UBS attributes the raise to **stronger conviction on hyperscaler cloud-AI demand and industry data-centre deployment** (2026-09-15). ➤ **Signal: ✓ CONFIRMS the number and supplies the missing rationale — demand conviction rather than tool-price inflation. NOTHING SUPERSEDED.**
- **CoWoS / packaging** — *Management:* CoWoS *"very tight"*, CoPoS pilot with mass production years out. *Flow:* UBS reports **large-size CoWoS and CoPoS pushed IN PARALLEL**, with **3D IC stacking and co-packaged optics as an additional moat for total solution** (2026-09-15); Digitimes the same day still has **CoWoS short alongside 3nm and 2nm**. ➤ **Signal: ✓ CONFIRMS tightness; ⚠ NUANCES the roadmap — parallel platforms, not a CoWoS-to-CoPoS hand-off.**
- **Customer allocation** — *Management:* has never disclosed an allocation order. *Flow:* Digitimes (2026-09-15, relayed by MS desk and UBS; @dnystedt 00:45 UTC) has **[[NVDA]] and [[AAPL]] driving the 3nm/2nm/CoWoS shortage, AWS winning more 3nm recently, and [[MEDIATEK]] *"on the short end of capacity allocation"***, plus a possible **split-node smartphone strategy through 2027**. ➤ **Signal: ⚠ NEW — an observable rationing order that is a customer-quality signal for TSMC and a constraint for the names below it. Channel report, unconfirmed.**
- **Glass core / EMIB-T response** — *Management:* discussed Glass Core in Japan on 2026-06-11 with Ibiden and Innolux as development partners; no mass-production commitment. *Flow:* an ex-Intel AlphaSense expert (2026-09-15) puts meaningful adoption at **2028-2029**, says the first use is **replacing ORGANIC SUBSTRATES rather than silicon interposers** with **CPO switch ASICs the earliest high-volume application**, and frames TSMC's glass core as a **substrate swap** against Intel's **platform-integral** approach. ➤ **Signal: ✓ CONFIRMS the 2028-29 timing MS already had; ⚠ CONTESTS the framing that glass core is TSMC's answer to EMIB-T. Expert opinion, one source, unconfirmed by either company.**

- **🆕🔴🔴 RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-24 (/run-inbox WORK UNIT E — UBS *"Global I/O: 3Q26 APAC Tech Tour"*, 09-22, off 40 company meetings; Redburn *"Bondernomics"*, 09-23, a BESI note with TSM Not Rated). FOUR ITEMS MOVE; ONE NUMBER DRIFTS; ONE CONTRADICTION IS OPENED AND LEFT OPEN.**

Do not read it as one.** ✅ **Net-new detail: A14 now appears inside the live ORDER list alongside N3/N2/A16 for the first time on this page.** ✓ **confirms.**
  - ✅ **`Leading-edge roadmap / High-NA` — CORROBORATED AND SHARPENED, NOTHING SUPERSEDED.** UBS: *"TSMC's base case is NOT to use [High-NA] for A14. Adoption is expected for A10 MP '30 onwards for a few selective layers. An earlier insertion remains possible, but not confirmed, for A14."* ➤ **Consistent with the ASML/TSMC joint statement of 09-08 (HVM from 2030) and with Bernstein's repeated ~2030/A10 mark (07-06, 09-01 primer). What is NEW: (a) A14 is explicitly NOT the base case, which the page previously held only by inference; (b) A10 adoption is "a few selective layers", i.e. selective insertion — a cap on the tool-count read-through to [[ASML]]; (c) an earlier A14 insertion is named as possible but unconfirmed.   - ⚠️⚠️ **`SoIC / AP7` — A CONTRADICTION IS OPENED AND IS NOT RESOLVED.** Redburn (09-23) treats TSMC's **AP7 fab investment in 2026 as offering "MORE THAN DOUBLE THE SoIC CAPACITY of the existing AP6 fab"**, **underwritten by the AMD Helios rack ramp in 2H26**, and uses it as evidence TSMC is not sitting on idle SoIC capacity. One testable hook: UBS's own note in this same run has ODMs putting the AMD Helios higher-volume inflection at Q227 (compute boards shipping Q426), LATER than Redburn's "2H26" — which leans mildly toward the Fubon deferral read.** ⚠️ **And Redburn's underlying measurement is uncomfortable for the SoIC bull case regardless of who is right on AP7: AMD's HB fleet installed at TSMC implies 75 uph against a 1,500 uph spec, −95%.**
  - **`Capital / competition (moat)` — one mild negative, third-party sourced.** Redburn: *"market tightness in leading-edge 2.5D has led to OVERFLOW ORDERS FROM TSMC TO THE OSATs AND TO INTEL."* ➤ **First note to name [[INTC]] as an actual overflow recipient rather than a candidate; of a piece with BofA's 08-20 CoWoS-R-to-ASE mark and the 08-30 "Intel outsourcing more as it focuses on EMIB" relay. Also: Vera CPU on CoWoS-R with **Amkor (2H26/2027) and ASE (late 2027)**; **LP40 migrating to CoWoS-R in late 2027**; **Spectrum-X CPO a meaningful CoWoS contributor in 2H27**; and an explicit un-modelled upside — *"NVDA deciding to introduce NVLink Switch CPO, which may require significant CoWoS capacity support — at this point, we are not modeling this into our"* forecast. |

Economically it's better to wait for HNA to further improve in availability and throughput, which results in lower cost per exposure**"*, and the litho-tool math backs it — for **large stitched logic die, 1x High-NA vs 2x Low-NA runs 1.6x in 2025 → 1.1x in 2030 and never crosses 1.0x**, i.e. *"it will still remain cheaper to use double exposure LNA rather than single exposure HNA over the next five years."*** ⚠️ **The cost of the choice, named in the same sentence: *"(but at the risk of NOT BENEFITING FROM HNA IMMEDIATELY)"* — [[INTC]] is in HVM on 18A today with 18A layers dual-qualified at NXE-matched yield, taking process-simplification benefit (3x 0.33 NA → 1x 0.55 NA cuts a flow from ~40 steps to <10; P19 local interconnect ~35%) that TSMC defers.** **What this means for TSMC's own litho bill: logic litho intensity ~32% at 2nm, *"similar levels or higher with HNA at 1.4nm, and increase further at the 1nm node"*; by 2030 advanced logic runs ~25-30 EUV exposures including ~4-6 High-NA — so TSMC's A14/A10 capex is a Low-NA-heavy build for now, consistent with the house model's A14 "+20% Low-NA intensity vs N2 → 13.2 tools/25kwpm" above.** ⚠️ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED on this page: the 07-06 "TSMC slowest, ~2030 at A10" mark stands; what changed is [[INTC]] (2026/18A, not 2028/A14) and Samsung LOGIC (~2030/A10, not ~2029/SF1.4).** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML__High-NA_EUV_Primer_-_The_shrinking_never_stops.html) |
