Wei's agentic-AI CPU resurgence, Fubon's own "server-CPU very strong growth 2027+", the Vera/Venice CoWoS pull). Logged as a TIMING tension and deliberately NOT netted away.** **NET-NEW #2, competitively the most interesting: TSMC IS BUILDING ITS OWN EMIB. "Based on our checks, TSMC is also working on their IN-HOUSE EMIB SOLUTION for different chip design. But currently, it is still at a VERY, VERY EARLY STAGE. And there is NO PRODUCTION TIMELINE YET." Alongside it, TSMC's public commentary on Intel's EMIB has become "MORE OPEN-MINDED," which Fubon reads as TSMC having "gone through negotiation with both Google and MediaTek" — recorded as HIS INFERENCE, not a disclosure. The read: the incumbent is answering EMIB-T rather than only absorbing the overflow into CoWoS-L/CoPoS; too late to affect the 2028 [[GOOG]] TPU decision, but it caps the DURATION of Intel's window and is now the standing counter to any thesis treating EMIB-T as a permanent second source.** **NET-NEW #3, the advanced-packaging competitive SEQUENCE, which this page had only as a static picture: ASE is a PARTNER not a competitor in 2026-27 ("I wouldn't say TSMC is ASE's competitor… because the CoWoS capacity is very very tight, so some customer just go to ASE for the production"), turning competitive "in the following years" via TSMC's Arizona advanced-packaging fab — TSMC keeps the front end (CoW/CoP) and the on-substrate work goes to Amkor, "the most likely competitor for ASE, but NOT NOW" — and the mechanism is that ASE "currently has no plan to follow TSMC's footprint to build their fab in the US." ⚠️ Attribution caveat recorded rather than hidden: the OSAT name is rendered by the ASR as "code"/"Cowd" and is read as Amkor on context (US footprint, Arizona on-substrate work), corroborated independently by UBS on this page (07-27: "ASE the primary Taiwan partner, Amkor the most likely Arizona on-substrate partner"). Nothing beyond that inference is asserted.** **Also logged, as restatements rather than new data: capacity is the binding constraint on Google's 12-15mn 2028 TPU target ("TSMC, they don't have enough capacity to do that… that's why I say Intel's is a must have in 2028" — detail on [[INTC]]/[[GOOG]]/[[MEDIATEK]]); and on the overbooking/double-booking bear, Fubon defers entirely to management's own earnings-call answer (production plan negotiated with all customers, "no overbooking, double booking," chips do not become inventory, still positive on AI demand and still raising capex). - **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the CoWoS outsourcing ratio is now quantified through 2028, and TSMC's gross-margin expansion is decomposed into price vs cost.** No TSMC rating or PT superseded (read-across plus third-party analysis). ⚠️ **The most reusable number added: the OUTSOURCED share of CoWoS goes 8% (2024) → 46% (2025) → 75% (2026E) → 85% (2027E) → 90% (2028E), with ASE's oS-outsourcing revenue $81mn → $697mn → $2,019mn → $3,809mn → $5,437mn** (JPM · Hariharan, ASE 2Q26, OW PT NT$650→NT$700, 2026-07-30). **The implication for how this page reads TSMC capacity commentary: with ~three-quarters of CoWoS already outsourced and heading to 90%, TSMC's own CoWoS capacity figures increasingly UNDERSTATE effective system capacity — CoWoS supply questions have to be answered at the TSMC+OSAT level, not TSMC alone.** ASE guided **LEAP revenue to at least DOUBLE in 2027 (implying $7bn+)** off a 2026 base already ahead of its $3.5bn guide; JPM models **~$4bn/$8bn/$12bn for 2026/27/28** and Jefferies-Fubon models **US$8.5bn in FY27, above company guidance**, with **FoCoS capacity including SPIL reaching 50k wpm by end-FY27** and ASE described as **"the most aggressive player in booking CoWoS tools"** (Shang, 2026-07-31). **Full-Process CoWoS is the 2027 driver — starting with AMD Venice this year, scaling in 2027, with possible extension to NVDA Vera or AWS Trainium depending on AMD execution; yield "progressing well."** ASE raised 2026 capex a SECOND time by $2bn to **US$10.5bn** across 20+ facility projects, and is lifting its structural ATM GM range from 24-30% to **above 30%** (Jefferies: mid-30s in FY27). ASE positioned as **"the primary partner for TSMC and the largest wafer-level packaging capacity provider outside of TSMC, much bigger than Amkor or Intel,"** with privileged access to leading-edge technologies **including COUPE**; panel-level packaging (310×310mm) ramps 1Q27, with CPO and EMIB contributing from 2027 and more meaningfully in 2028. **Separately, and materially for the margin debate: Chipstrat decomposes TSMC's recent gross-margin expansion as "gross margin rose 9.1 points, and manufacturing performance explained 3.2 of those points. The other 5.9 points? PRICING" — roughly two-thirds price, not cost, against management framing that points to utilisation and cost reduction. TSMC "historically played it safe, erring on the side of undercharging… That's changing"** (Lyons, 2026-07-31). | 2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch) | **4 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** (Fubon Buy NT$3,500, UBS Buy, Nomura Buy, BofA all stand). **The headline is that the EMIB-T threat finally got stress-tested by a house that covers both sides, and TSMC wins in the bull case for Intel.** UBS sizes the **advanced-packaging TAM at US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR**, and then hands Intel its best plausible outcome — **13% industry share / US$16bn of sales by 2030, packages for ~24mn accelerator dies, with CoPoS not reaching MP for Nvidia until 2028 and for others until 2029-30** — and TSMC *still* compounds advanced-packaging sales at a **50% CAGR 2026-30, holds 65-70% share, and takes advanced packaging to 18% of total sales in 2030E from 9% in 2026E.** In UBS's *lower* case (stronger EMIB-T adoption) that becomes **47% CAGR / 62% share / 16% of sales** — i.e. **the base-vs-bear spread is roughly 2pts of revenue mix over four years.** That is the most useful bound on packaging-disruption risk this page has, and it argues the debate is worth far less to TSMC's numbers than to Intel's. **Not smoothed over: UBS also says EMIB-T reaches MP in H2'27-28, slightly AHEAD of CoPoS in 2028, and names TSMC's own three execution risks — "CoPoS warpage, panel process uniformity, and new equipment readiness."** The compensating technical case: CoPoS offers "tighter interconnect, better signal integrity, broader integration options such as 3D stacking (and eventually, CPO)"; EMIB-T's edge is structural cost (no interposer) and scalability to ultra-large packages on large substrates. UBS's own framing of the outcome — **EMIB-T as "a meaningful second-source for selected hyperscaler ASICs" while CoPoS stays "the preferred platform for customers prioritizing the highest performance and execution reliability"** — is a share-of-mind concession, not a share-of-revenue one. **Second, and it corroborates the above from a completely different seat: an ex-ASM International senior process engineer (AlphaSense expert call, 07-25) independently concludes that TSMC's dominance holds and that the binding constraint is TOOL availability — specifically bonding tools — "not… financial or market factors,"** with demand for CoWoS and CoPoS "outpacing supply for the foreseeable future," the market **bifurcated (TSMC in complex, OSATs in mature)**, and the TSMC-NVIDIA relationship "deeply integrated… making it difficult for competitors to break in." Caveat kept on the page: it is a 2-page expert-network summary and the expert's background is front-end ALD/process integration, so it is a well-informed prior rather than a supply-chain audit. **Third — a pattern worth naming rather than a one-off: on glass-core substrates TSMC is ALSO behind Intel.** MS's checks have **Innolux + TSMC + Ibiden reaching MP only in 2H28 or 2029, versus Intel/Lens Tech with a pilot line by end-2026 and meaningful volume from 2H27** — the same ~12-18-month ordering as EMIB-T vs CoPoS. On both bridges and substrates, Intel is earlier and TSMC is betting on the higher-performance version arriving later. Glass core is directly relevant because it attacks **warpage**, one of the three CoPoS risks UBS names. **Fourth, restated because it is management's own words and was not on this page in this form: "TSMC highlighted at its 2Q26 call that back-end sits in deeper shortage than front-end, with packaging capacity so tight it now limits customer growth"** (via Jefferies) — i.e. packaging, not wafers, is the named growth constraint, which is the demand-side justification for the entire capex build above, plus **two ADDITIONAL advanced-packaging fabs announced on the call** (a positive read to [[KLAC]]'s Advanced Packaging SPC line, C26 forecast $1B / +57% y/y). Minor housekeeping note: BofA's weekend desk recap carried TSMC 2026 capex as "$58B" in a one-line summary while its own semicap section used **$60-64B from $52-56B** — the **$60-64B guide already confirmed on this page (07-16 call) stands**; the $58B is a modelled/actual figure, not the guide. Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[NVDA]], [[KLAC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md). |

(2026-06-14)**, both folded as **dated structural bullets in Debate/thesis, NOT as intra-quarter rows** — their dates fall inside the Q1 FY26 window which was **CLOSED by the 2Q26 print (07-16)**, so per the run's backfill convention they are logged as structural context rather than injected into a closed log. From the EDA primer: TSMC as **customer and gatekeeper** (Calibre-clean + PrimeTime mandated at 7/5/3nm, PDKs co-developed ~24 months pre-production), **Taiwan = 17% of Big-3 EDA revenue from <2% of global GDP, "entirely a TSMC ecosystem effect"**, the OIP Silicon-IP library 3,000 (2010) → **93,000 items (2025)**, **Fab 18 from 4 customers/8 tape-outs (2020) → 45/262 (2024)**, TSMC never built competitive internal EDA (Intel/Samsung tried and abandoned), Cadence's expanded TSMC N2/A16 collaboration, and the outside-view risk line *"ASML faces High-NA adoption risk, and TSMC faces geopolitical concentration"* — an independent restatement of the Taiwan-concentration bear already on the page (Barron's "80% of long-term assets still in Taiwan"). From the SMIC teardown: **TSMC N6 is the measured baseline** and **SMIC N+3 "reaches TSMC N6-class logic density… [but] does not match N6 on process maturity or cost"**; N6 fin pitch measured **34nm** with density from **DTCO not tighter pitches**, HD cell 240nm, no COAG, M0/M2/M3 ~40nm on SADP with **no quadruple patterning needed anywhere** (the cost/maturity moat, quantified); TSMC used the reduced p-to-n isolation booster on N3; a theoretical SMIC N+4 reaches only N5-class density; and the historical note that **Huawei was TSMC's largest customer pre-controls — the sole customer on N7+, TSMC's first EUV node, and among the first on N5 alongside Apple — ending in late 2020**. Cross-filed [[SMIC]], [[INTC]], [[ASML]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[AAPL]]. |
| 2026-07-24 | **/wiki-ingest — 2 log rows on the Intel-print read-across; additive, no rating/PT/estimate superseded.** **BofA · Haas Liu, "Intel read through: Limited competition and supply disruption to TSMC ecosystem" (07-24)** — the competitive verdict on Intel's Q2'26 beat, the **14A HVM-2028 commitment** and the capex raise to **>$20B (CY27 "significantly above")** is that the read-across to TSMC is **limited on both competition and supply**. Logged as the necessary counterweight to the same-day bull-side Intel headlines: the capex raise is unambiguously positive for **[[ASML]] / semicap** (MS revised Intel Low-NA EUV demand from **1 to 2-4 tools this year**), but that is a **supplier** read-through, not a **share** read-through — and on share the existing page framing holds (**TSMC ~90-95% of 2nm in the first two product waves 2028-29, Intel+Samsung combined ~5-6%**, JPM · Gokul 07-08; TSMC cheaper on a yield/PPA-adjusted basis even at a higher sticker). Where Intel *is* landing is **advanced packaging (EMIB-T)**, which TSMC has publicly framed as welcome complementary capacity given CoWoS tightness — **overflow, not displacement**. Tracked as unresolved: **UBS's 07-24 checks now put MediaTek among likely EMIB-T customers for its v9-gen TPU** — a third independent corroboration (after JPM 07-01 and UBS/Sunny Lin 07-01; see [[MEDIATEK]], [[INTC]]) — against **Fubon's 07-08** read that the **95%+ yield bar Google set is "very challenging" by mid-2027**, implying a fallback to TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion. **Austin Lyons / Chipstrat (07-24), "TSMC could spend $10B more on N2 capacity. Why won't they?"** — an independent framing of the capital-discipline question central to this page: with N2 demand plainly exceeding supply (super-hot-run mix at **20-30% from 10-20%**, each tier ~+50% ASP), the binding constraint is **TSMC's own choice, not the order book**. It cuts both ways on two live threads here — it strengthens the **capex-conservatism** assumption SemiAnalysis uses to argue **~50k+ WSPM of N3 overflow could flow to Intel** ([[INTC]]), and it makes the **70% GM bridge** in the house sensitivity work easier at the same time as it enlarges the overflow-to-competitors risk. Framing piece, no new company data. Edits: 2 new Q3'26-window Full-log rows (both 07-24). |
